3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.3931pt\raise-8.32506pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-10.16164pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 32.60144pt\raise-10.16164pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 84.36868pt\raise-10.16164pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

This definition does indeed reflect the formulas written in the physics literature. Locally, the Lagrangian density
L

may be written as
L=\bar{L}\big(x^{\mu},\{u_{I}^{a}\}_{|I|\leq k}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}
for some smooth function
\bar{L}\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,
which by Prop.
3.5
depends (locally) on jets up to degree
|I|=k
. Hence, abusing notation
slightly, the value of the local Lagrangian density
\mathcal{L}(\phi)
on a field
\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)

may be represented by

\mathcal{L}(\phi)=L\circ j^{\infty}\phi=L\big(x^{\mu},\phi^{a},\{\partial_{I}\phi^{a}\}_{|I|\leq k}\big)=\bar{L}\big(x^{\mu},\phi^{a},\{\partial_{I}\phi^{a}\}_{|I|\leq k}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}=\bar{\mathcal{L}}(\phi)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}\,,

as is common in the physics literature (see e.g.
[
HT92
]
[
DF99
]
).

Example 3.10

(
O(
n
)-model Lagrangian).

Let
M\times W\rightarrow M
be the trivial vector bundle over spacetime M equipped with a Lorentzian (or Riemannian) metric
g
and
W\cong\mathbbm{R}^{N}

equipped with a Euclidean inner product
\langle-,-\rangle
. The “
{\rm O}(n)
-model’’ Lagrangian
27
27

27

Higher polynomial terms,
i.e., “interactions”, are also considered. Generally, this is accomplished by adding a term
V(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)
for
V:\mathbbm{R}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

some polynomial function.
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\big(c_{2}\cdot\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(g^{\mu\nu}\cdot\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\cdot\partial_{\nu}\phi_{a}+c_{2}\cdot\phi^{a}\phi_{a}+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot\phi^{a}\phi_{a}\cdot\phi^{b}\phi_{b}\big)\cdot\sqrt{|g|}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}

for some “coupling constants”
c_{2},c_{4}\in\mathbbm{R}
, where
*_{g}:\Omega^{k}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-k}(M)
is the Hodge operator
of
g
,
\phi^{a}\cdot\phi_{a}:=\langle\phi,\phi\rangle=\delta^{a}_{\,\,b}\cdot\phi^{a}\cdot\phi^{b}

and similarly for
\langle-,-\rangle_{g}:=g\otimes\langle-,-\rangle
as pairing on
\Omega^{1}(M)\otimes W
. By the local
coordinate expression, the Lagrangian density is local
\mathcal{L}(\phi)=L\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)
, for the Lagrangian bundle map

L:J^{\infty}_{M}(M\times W)\rightarrow\wedge^{d}(T^{*}M)
over M given locally by

L=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(g^{\mu\nu}\cdot u^{a}_{\mu}\cdot u^{b}_{\nu}\cdot\delta^{a}_{\,\,b}+c_{2}\cdot u^{a}u^{b}\cdot\delta^{a}_{\,\,b}+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot u^{a}u^{c}\cdot\delta^{a}_{\,\,c}\cdot u^{b}u^{d}\cdot\delta^{b}_{\,\,d}\big)\cdot\sqrt{|g|}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}\,.

In the case of Minkowski (or Euclidean) spacetime
(M,g)=(\mathbbm{R}^{d},\eta)
, the expression simplifies further with the
determinant being
\sqrt{|\eta|}=1
.
Taking further the fiber to be one dimensional
W\cong\mathbbm{R}
, the Lagrangian reduces to that of “
\phi^{4}
scalar field theory”.
Choosing instead the spacetime
(M,g)=(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t},\delta)
to be the
1
-dimensional ‘time’ line with the canonical metric,
the Lagrangian reduces to that of particle mechanics coupled to a background potential.

Collecting the above results and observations, we see that any local Lagrangian density constitutes a smooth map.

Lemma 3.11

(
Local Lagrangian is smooth).

A local Lagrangian density
\mathcal{L}:\Gamma_{M}(F)\rightarrow\Omega^{d}(M)
canonically extends to a smooth map of smooth sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\cong\Omega^{d}(M)\hat{\otimes}y(\mathbbm{R})\,.

Proof.

For any
\tilde{\phi}^{k}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
smoothly
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-parametrized section of
J^{\infty}_{M}(F)
,
the Lagrangian density smooth bundle map
L:J^{\infty}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
underlying
\mathcal{L}
induces the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.53645pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 37.5365pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.73933pt\raise-10.34167pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 16.11449pt\raise 0.11613pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\phi}^{k}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.5365pt\raise-3.63608pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.73933pt\raise-12.98996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.1379pt\raise-12.98996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 84.73933pt\raise-12.98996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,,

where the outer part commutes since the two inner triangles commute. Hence the composition
L\circ\tilde{\phi}^{k}

defines a smoothly
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-parametrized
n
-form on
M
, i.e.,
L\circ\tilde{\phi}^{k}\in\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
.
Moreover, the assignment
\tilde{\phi}^{k}\mapstochar\rightarrow L\circ\tilde{\phi}^{k}
is manifestly functorial with respect to pullbacks
of maps
\mathbbm{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, and so
L
naturally defines a map of smooth sets

L:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,.

The smooth Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}:=L\circ j^{\infty}
is defined by pre-composing with the smooth infinite prolongation map

j^{\infty}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)
from (
52
), that is,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

(54)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;L(j^{\infty}\phi^{k})\,,

where
\phi^{k}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
. Finally, it is a smooth map as a composition of smooth maps.
∎

At this point, we have described all the ingredients needed to define a bosonic, smooth and local field theory via objects and maps in the
category of smooth sets.

Definition 3.12

(
Local Lagrangian Field Theory).

A (bosonic)
smooth, local Lagrangian field theory
is defined to be a pair

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})

where
\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\in\mathrm{SmthSet}
is a smooth field space of sections
of a (finite-dimensional) fiber bundle
F
over the space-time
M
, and

\mathcal{L}=L\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
is the smooth Lagrangian density defined by a smooth bundle map

L:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
.

Remark 3.13

(
Finite- vs. infinite-degree jet bundles in physics).

(i)
Each of the fundamental Lagrangians appearing in theoretical physics, e.g. General Relativity, Yang--Mills
28
28

28

Strictly speaking,
our discussion applies verbatim for Yang–Mills and Chern–Simons
only if
the field space is taken to be
\mathcal{F}=\Omega^{1}(M,\mathfrak{g})=\Gamma_{M}(T^{*}M\times_{M}\mathfrak{g})
,
i.e., connections on
the trivial
G
-bundle

M\times G\rightarrow M
. Generally, however, the field space consists of connections
A\in\mathrm{Conn}(P)

on an arbitrary principal
G
-bundle over
M
, which are not (canonically) the set of sections of a fiber bundle over
M
. For a fixed topological
sector
P\rightarrow M
, one can choose a reference connection
A_{0}
to yield a bijection
\mathrm{Conn}(P)\cong_{A_{0}}\Gamma_{M}\big(\mathrm{ad}(P)\big)

where
\mathrm{ad}(P)=P\times_{G}\mathfrak{g}
is the adjoint bundle. The jet bundle formalism then applies on the corresponding smooth set of the right-hand side,
therefore all diffeomorphisms and symmetries defined necessarily preserve
A_{0}
. Namely, such an approach treats the
perturbation
theory around
a fixed connection
A_{0}
. Consistently accounting for
all
topological sectors nonperturbatively
and
furthermore (local) gauge symmetries simultaneously
forces a further natural generalization of smooth sets (Rem.
6.37
).

and Chern–Simons theories, factor
globally
through a finite degree jet bundle
J^{k}_{M}F
.
29
29

29

In particular, these fundamental Lagrangians all depend
(at most) on second-order jets. However, higher-order jet dependence corrections are often suggested to support deviations on experimental data.

(ii)
Generic algebraic operations on fixed order Lagrangians, such as integration by parts (and further operations in the
variational bicomplex; see
section

5.1
), result in objects that necessarily factor through higher jet bundles,
and so it is only natural to not fix an order in the jet bundle and consider the infinite limit instead.

(iii)
To our knowledge, there are no explicit examples of theories that only
locally
factor through finite order jet bundles, as in the
locally pro-manifold picture. Working in this slightly more general setting is nevertheless natural in order to place ourselves within the
convenient setting of smooth sets. On the other hand, to our knowledge, there is no fundamental physical principle that excludes the possibility of
locally finite order Lagrangians, and it would be interesting to find explicit examples and properties of such theories.

Remark 3.14

(
Non-local Lagrangian Field Theory).

Although we will not pursue this here, the setting of smooth sets naturally accommodates a more general notion of a smooth Lagrangian field theory,
given by a field space
\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
and a (possibly non-local) smooth Lagrangian density

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,.

Common examples of such Lagrangians include (effective) field theories which factor through direct products of
J^{\infty}_{M}(F)

(also termed “multi-local”). Nevertheless, as will be made clear throughout this manuscript, the
locality
property is a crucial
ingredient for the majority of well-known constructions and results of classical field theory.

The statement (and proof) of Lem.
3.11
holds in more generality, for differential operators
viewed as maps of sections of bundles that factor through the infinite jet bundle.

Lemma 3.15

(
Differential operators as smooth maps).

Let
F
and
G
be two (finite-dimensional) fiber bundles over
M
, and let
P
be a smooth bundle map covering a diffeomorphism
f:M\rightarrow M

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{M\,.}

(i)

P
naturally defines a smooth map

\displaystyle P:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(G)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{k}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;P\circ\tilde{\phi}^{k}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}},f^{-1})\,,

where
\tilde{\phi}^{k}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
is any
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-parametrized section
of
J^{\infty}_{M}(F)
.

(ii)
Furthermore, it defines a differential operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:\Gamma_{M}(F)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\Gamma_{M}(G)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;P\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)\circ f^{-1}

which extends to a smooth map
\mathcal{P}:=P\circ j^{\infty}
, where

j^{\infty}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)
is the
smooth infinite jet prolongation (
52
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(G)

(55)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;P\circ j^{\infty}(\phi^{k})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}},f^{-1})\;.

Proof.

This follows as in Lem.
3.11
.
∎

For future reference, let us note that any map smooth
P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow G
covering a diffeomorphism as in Lem.
3.15

canonically induces a
smooth
‘prolongated’ map of infinite jet bundles
\mathsf{pr}P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}G

covering
P
.
30
30

30

A special case of this definition, where it is restricted to bundle maps
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow G
arising
as pullbacks of bundle maps
F\rightarrow G
, appears in
[
Sau89
, Def. 7.2.10]
in the Fréchet setting
and
[
An89
, Def 1.2]
in the pro-manifold setting.

Definition 3.16

(
Prolongation of jet bundle map).

Let
P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow G
be a smooth bundle map covering a diffeomorphism
f:M\rightarrow M
. The
prolongation

of
P
is the bundle map defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{pr}P:J^{\infty}_{M}F

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}G

\displaystyle j^{\infty}_{p}\phi

\displaystyle\longmapsto j^{\infty}_{f(p)}\big(P\circ j^{\infty}\phi\circ f^{-1}\big)\,,

where
\phi:U\rightarrow F
is any representative local section. By construction, it covers the original bundle map

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{pr}P}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{G\,,}

and hence also the diffeomorphism
f:M\rightarrow M
.

The prolongation
\mathsf{pr}P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}G
is:

(a)
well-defined, i.e., independent of the choice representative section
\phi:U\rightarrow F
since the right-hand
side manifestly depends only on the derivatives of
\phi
at
p\in M
, all of which are encoded in
j^{\infty}_{p}\phi
,

(b)
smooth as an application of Prop.
3.7
by expanding in local coordinates.

By construction, it follows that the induced smooth map on sections

\mathsf{pr}P\circ j^{\infty}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}G)
(Lem.
3.15
)
is related to that of
\mathcal{P}:=P\circ j^{\infty}(-)\circ(\mathrm{id},f^{-1}):\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(G)
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{pr}P\circ j^{\infty}=j^{\infty}\circ\mathcal{P}\,.

(56)

Let us return to the field-theoretic setting where the
smooth
differential operator maps of Lem.
3.15

naturally appear. As explained, of course, one such case is a smooth Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

induced by a bundle map
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
over
M
. Completely analogously, we may define
local

p
-form valued maps on the field space, for any
0\leq p\leq d
. Indeed, consider Lem.
3.15
in the case
where
G=\wedge^{p}T^{*}M
as a bundle over
M
. Then
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{p}T^{*}M)\cong\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
,
with its
*
-plots being usual smooth
p
-forms on the base
M
.
In this case, precomposing a bundle map
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{p}T^{*}M

by the jet prolongation map gives a smooth map

\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,.

Definition 3.17

(
Local currents on field space).

A local (smooth)
p
-form current
on field space is a smooth map

\mathcal{P}:=P\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

induced by a smooth bundle map
P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{p}T^{*}M
.

Example 3.18

(
Vector-valued field space currents).

Constructing local currents for trivial vector field bundles as in Ex.
2.11
is straightforward. For instance:

(i)
Let
\phi=\phi^{a}\cdot e_{a}
be some basis decomposition of
\mathcal{F}=[M,W]
. Then

\mathcal{P}_{1}(\phi)=\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi^{a}=\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{1}(M)

defines a local
1
-form current, for
P_{1}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T^{*}M
given locally by
u^{a}_{\mu}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}
.

(ii)
Let
B=(B_{[ab]}):W\times W\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
be any antisymmetric bilinear map. Then

\mathcal{P}_{2}(\phi)=B\circ(\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi,\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi)=B_{[ab]}\cdot\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi^{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi^{b}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{2}(M)

defines a local
2
-form current, for
P_{2}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{2}T^{*}M
given locally by
B_{[ab]}\cdot u^{a}_{\mu}u^{b}_{\nu}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\nu}
.

(iii)
Recall the field space vector fields
\mathcal{Z}^{A}(\phi)=A^{a}_{\,b}\cdot\phi^{b}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{b}}
and

\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}(\phi)=\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\cdot\nu^{\mu}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}
from Ex.
2.24

and Ex.
2.25
, respectively. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}^{A}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=-\langle\mathcal{Z}^{A}(\phi),\,\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle:=-\mathcal{Z}^{A}(\phi)^{a}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi=-A^{a}_{\,b}\cdot\phi^{b}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi_{a}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1}(M)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\iota_{\nu}\big(\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)-\langle\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}(\phi),\,\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle=\iota_{\nu}\big(\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)-\nu^{\mu}\cdot\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi_{a}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1}(M)

define local
(d-1)
-form currents. The corresponding jet bundle maps can be read off by expanding in coordinates.

Integration defines another natural map of smooth sets. More precisely, if the base spacetime
M
is compact
and oriented,
31
31

31

Throughout this text we shall assume the spacetime
M
is orientable, with a fixed choice of orientation.
Treating non-orientable spacetimes requires (minor) technical modifications (e.g., Lagrangians and currents as valued
in forms twisted by the orientation bundle) which we will make explicit here. Such modifications are implicit in
[
DF99
]
,
and technical details may be found in
[
De18
]
.

then integration along
M
defines a smooth map

\displaystyle\int_{M}:\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\,.

(57)

Explicitly, for any
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-parametrized top-form
\omega_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}}\in\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
, the value
of the function
\int_{M}\omega_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}}\in y(\mathbbm{R})(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k},\mathbbm{R})
is given by

\bigg(\int_{M}\omega_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}}\!\bigg)(x):=\int_{M}\iota_{x}^{*}\omega_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}}\,,

where
\iota_{x}
is the inclusion
M\xrightarrow{\sim}\{x\}\times M\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times M
.
In plain words, one integrates along
M
while keeping the
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-dependence fixed. If
M
is not compact,
the smooth integration map is only defined on the smooth subset of compactly supported
forms
\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert},c}(M)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
.

Composition of a smooth Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
with the smooth integration map defines the action functional as
a map of smooth sets

\displaystyle S=\int_{M}\circ\mathcal{L}\;:\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\,.

(58)

If
\mathcal{L}
is interpreted as a
d
-form current, then the action may be thought of as its charge over
M
.
If the spacetime
M
is not compact, the integration map is only defined on the smooth subset of compactly supported fields

\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M,c}(F)\hookrightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
,
on which the action functional is still defined as a smooth map.
32
32

32

Or more generally smooth fields with
appropriate decay behavior. Alternatively, one integrates against test bump functions instead.

Analogously to the case of the action functional, we may integrate a
p
-form
current along any
p
-dimensional (oriented, compact) sub-manifold to obtain a smooth, ‘local’
real-valued function on field space.

Definition 3.19

(
Charges on field space).

The
charge
of a local
p
-form current
\mathcal{P}
along a
p
-dimensional oriented, compact submanifold

\Sigma^{p}\hookrightarrow M
is the smooth map

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}:=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}\mathcal{P}=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}\quad:\quad\mathcal{F}\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;y(\mathbbm{R})\,.

The physical interpretation is the traditional one. For any field
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
, a point in field space, the charge map
assigns the real number
\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}\phi\in\mathbbm{R}
, i.e., the total flux through the submanifold

\Sigma^{p}
of the current
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
corresponding to the chosen field configuration.
The upshot is that such charges are in fact smooth maps within the category of smooth sets, which allows one to
study them rigorously.
In particular, we may employ currents and their charges as the
basic ingredients for general
local
functionals on field space, which are a particular subalgebra of all smooth real-valued maps.

Definition 3.20

(
Local functionals on field space).

The algebra of (smooth)
local real-valued functionals

C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathcal{F})

on the field space
\mathcal{F}
is the (minimal) sub-algebra generated by charges of currents, and more generally by integrating currents

\mathcal{P}:=P\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
against bump functions along non-compact oriented
submanifolds.
33
33

33

This can further be enlarged to include limits of charges against bump functions, and hence observables
of distributional nature. This extension will not be needed for our discussion.

Remark 3.21

(
Algebras of currents and local functionals).

The set of currents inherits a (graded) algebra structure from the target
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
, i.e.,
the wedge product of the currents induced by

P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{p}T^{*}M
and
P^{\prime}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{p^{\prime}}T^{*}M
is given by

\mathcal{P}\wedge\mathcal{P}^{\prime}:=P\wedge P^{\prime}\circ j^{\infty}\;\;:\;\;\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p+p^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,.

The resulting current induces charges
(\mathcal{P}\wedge\mathcal{P}^{\prime})_{\Sigma^{p+p^{\prime}}}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
by
integrating over submanifolds

\Sigma^{p+p^{\prime}}
of dimension
p+p^{\prime}
. Note that this is not directly related to the product of the corresponding charges,

\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}\cdot\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{p^{\prime}}}=\bigg(\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\int_{\Sigma^{p^{\prime}}}P^{\prime}\circ j^{\infty}\bigg)\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})

induced by first integrating against submanifolds
\Sigma^{p}
,
\Sigma^{p^{\prime}}
of dimension
p
and
p^{\prime}
, respectively.

In the case of
p=0
, where
\Omega^{0}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)=[M,\mathbbm{R}]
is the smooth space of smooth
\mathbbm{R}
-valued functions on
M
.
By integration over
0
-dimensional manifolds, i.e.,
points in
M
, we mean evaluation at the given point.

Example 3.22

(
Field point amplitude).

For any chart
\big\{x^{\mu},\{u_{I}^{a}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}
of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
above a point
x\in M
, we have the smooth, local point evaluation maps

\hat{\phi}^{a}_{I}(x):=\mathrm{ev}_{x}\circ u^{a}_{I}\circ j^{\infty}\quad:\quad\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{0}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\cong[y(M),y(\mathbbm{R})]\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\,.

On
*
-plots
\mathcal{F}
, i.e., on field configurations
\Gamma_{M}(F)
, this extracts the amplitude (of the derivatives) of the
field at the point
x
, in the given chart,

\hat{\phi}^{a}_{I}(x)(\phi)=\partial_{I}\phi^{a}(x)\,,

and similarly for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of fields. In the physics literature, these ‘point observables’ are a common source of confusion,
since they are usually denoted by exactly the same symbol as the field itself.

We close off this section by defining the appropriate notion of a symmetry of a Lagrangian field theory. With the notion of diffeomorphisms
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
as in Def.
2.21
at hand, one can expect a (smooth) symmetry of
a local Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
to be any diffeomorphism
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
that preserves the Lagrangian,
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{L}
.
However, since ‘exact local Lagrangians’ induce trivial dynamics (see Eq. (
127
) onwards), it is
natural to relax the condition to preserve the Lagrangian up to an exact Lagrangian. Indeed, it is this notion of symmetry (in its local version) that preserves
the corresponding (smooth) space of on-shell fields (Prop.
5.28
), and (in its infinitesimal version) induces (smooth) conserved
currents via Noether’s 1st theorem (Prop.
6.14
). In the current setting of
finite
symmetries, as we will see, physical examples dictate that
a Lagrangian may be further only preserved up to a pullback by a spacetime diffeomorphism, in which case the (smooth) space of on-shell fields is again preserved (Prop.
5.42
).

Definition 3.23

(
Symmetry of Lagrangian field theory).

(i)
A
local symmetry
of a local Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
is a
local
diffeomorphism
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

such that there exists a local current (d-1)-form
\mathcal{K}
that makes diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}~~}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)}

commute. That is,
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}
where

\mathcal{K}:=K\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
for some bundle map

K:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
and
\mathcal{D}:=D\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
for some bundle map

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

as per Lem.
3.15
.

(ii)
A
spacetime covariant symmetry
of a local Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
is a diffeomorphism
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
,
the differential operator induced by a bundle map
D:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
covering a diffeomorphism
f:M\rightarrow M
, such that there exist
local current (d-1)-form
\mathcal{K}
that makes the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,.}

commute. In other words,
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}=f^{*}\circ\mathcal{L}+f^{*}\circ\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}
where

\mathcal{K}:=K\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
for some bundle map

K:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
and
\mathcal{D}:=D\circ j^{\infty}(-)\circ(\mathrm{id},f^{-1}):\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
for some bundle map

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{M\,.}

as per Lem.
3.15
. Colloquially, one may say
\mathcal{D}
is a “local symmetry of
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
up to a spacetime diffeomorphism”.

Note that the invertibility of a local diffeomorphism
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
implies that the induced
34
34

34

By dimensionality, it is obvious that

D=\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ\tilde{D}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
itself cannot be invertible.
prolongated bundle map
\mathsf{pr}P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F

(Def.
3.16
) is necessarily an automorphism, with the inverse of
\mathcal{D}
given by
\mathcal{D}^{-1}=(\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ(\mathsf{pr}D)^{-1})\circ j^{\infty}
.
A similar statement holds for the diffeomorphism of a spacetime covariant symmetry. The diffeomorphisms of Ex.
2.26

arising from bundle automorphisms maps
\tilde{f}:F\rightarrow F
covering diffeomorphisms are a special case of the above, by precomposing via

\pi^{\infty}_{0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
.

We have already introduced the ingredients of an explicit example of such symmetries.

Example 3.24

(
Symmetries of
{\rm O}(N)
-model).

Consider the
{\rm O}(N)
-model Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}:[M,W]\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
of Ex.
3.10
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}\,.

(i)
Recall the postcomposition induced diffeomorphisms
\mathcal{D}=g_{*}:[M,W]\rightarrow[M,W]
of Ex.
2.24
.
Choosing
g:W\rightarrow W
to be an orthogonal transformation,
e_{a}\mapstochar\rightarrow g^{b}_{\,\,a}\cdot e_{b}
for
[g^{b}_{\,\,a}]\in\mathrm{O}(N,\mathbbm{R})
,
which (by definition) preserves the inner product on
W
, we obtain a local symmetry of the Lagrangian field theory:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}(\phi):

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(g\circ\phi)=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}(g\circ\phi)\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}(g\circ\phi)\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle g\circ\phi,g\circ\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle g\circ\phi,g\circ\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle g\circ\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,g\circ\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\phi)\,,

where
\mathrm{d}_{M}(g\circ\phi)=\mathrm{d}_{M}(g^{a}_{\,\,b}\cdot\phi^{b}\cdot e_{a})=g^{a}_{\,\,b}\cdot\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi^{b}\cdot e_{a}=g\circ\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi
, since

g^{a}_{\,\,b}\in\mathbbm{R}
for each
a,b
. The calculation carries through identically for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of fields. This is a symmetry of the theory with

\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}=0
and
f=\mathrm{id}_{M}
.
35
35

35

Any choice of
(d-1)
-form current
\mathcal{K}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}
for some ‘trivial’ current
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}
will serve to fill the diagram.

The symmetry is local, with
\mathcal{D}=g_{*}

factoring through
J^{\infty}_{M}F
via
J^{0}F=F
as in Rem.
2.26
.

(ii)
Recall the precomposition induced diffeomorphisms
\mathcal{D}:=f^{*}:[M,W]\rightarrow[M,W]
of Ex.
2.25
.
Choosing
f:M\rightarrow M
to be an isometry of the background metric
g
,
36
36

36

For instance, a Poincaré transformation in the case of Minkowski
space
(M,g)=(\mathbbm{R}^{d},\eta)
.
i.e.,
f^{*}g=g
and so further
f^{*}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}
, we obtain a spacetime covariant symmetry of
the Lagrangian field theory:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}(\phi):

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(f^{*}\phi)=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}(f^{*}\phi)\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}(f^{*}\phi)\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle f^{*}\phi,f^{*}\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle f^{*}\phi,f^{*}\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle f^{*}\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,f^{*}\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle_{f^{*}g}+c_{2}\cdot f^{*}\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot f^{*}(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot f^{*}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(f^{*}\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot f^{*}\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot f^{*}(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot f^{*}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=f^{*}\circ\mathcal{L}(\phi)\,,

where we used standard properties of pullback map on forms, metrics and functions on the spacetime
M
. Evidently, this is a spacetime
covariant symmetry of the theory with
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}=0
which is furthermore
not
local. Nevertheless, by Ex.

2.25
the corresponding vector field on
[M,W]
is “local” (Def.
6.1
)
factoring through
J^{\infty}_{M}F
via
J^{1}_{M}F
, and the above finite spacetime covariant symmetry induces an
infinitesimal local

symmetry of the theory (see Lem.
6.17
and Ex.
6.18
).

Remark 3.25

(
On spacetime covariant symmetries).

The physics literature usually focuses on
local
symmetries of Def.
3.23
as a symmetry
of a local Lagrangian field theory, and more often only in its infinitesimal version (see Def.
6.11
)
with the all-important application in Noether’s Theorems (see Prop.
6.14
, Prop.
6.23
).

(a)
We generalize slightly with Def.
3.23
(ii) to include, in particular,
cases where one may lift a spacetime diffeomorphism to a diffeomorphism on field space (Rem.
2.26
).
This is necessary to accommodate important examples appearing in physics, with Ex.
3.24
being a particular instance.
Other important field theories with such symmetries include the (2nd order) metric formulation of General Relativity, Yang-Mills theories,
and Chern-Simons theories.
37
37

37

As emphasized in Rem.
2.26
, finite spacetime diffeomorphisms
apply for pure general relativity, and at most when it is coupled to fields with trivial field bundles. Similarly for Yang-Mills
and Chern-Simons theories, it strictly applies only to the trivial topological sector.

(b)
Crucially, the infinitesimal version of any spacetime covariant symmetries is, in fact, an infinitesimal
local
symmetry (Lem.
6.17
), which we believe is the reason their finite ‘non-local’ aspect is often bypassed in the literature.

(c)
One can check that
local
symmetries
\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})

a local Lagrangian field theory form a (smooth) subgroup of all local diffeomorphisms.
38
38

38

The action of a composition of two
local

symmetries gives

\mathcal{L}\circ(\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\mathcal{D}_{2})=(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{1})\circ\mathcal{D}_{2}=(\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\circ\mathcal{K}_{1})\circ\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\circ\mathcal{K}_{2}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\circ(\mathcal{K}_{1}\circ\mathcal{D}_{2})\,.

The latter term is also local since
\mathcal{K}_{1}\circ\mathcal{D}_{2}=K_{1}\circ j^{\infty}\circ D_{2}\circ j^{\infty}=(\mathcal{K}_{1}\circ\mathsf{pr}D_{2})\circ j^{\infty}
where

\mathsf{pr}D_{2}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\xrightarrow{\sim}J^{\infty}_{M}F
is the prolongated bundle automorphism (Def.
3.16
).
Thus the composed local diffeomorphism preserves the Lagrangian up to the exact local Lagrangian
\mathrm{d}_{M}\circ(K_{2}+K_{1}\circ\mathsf{pr}D_{2})\circ j^{\infty}
.

From a strict mathematical point of view, however, for a given local Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}=L\circ j^{\infty}
, the pullback smooth Lagrangian
f^{*}\circ\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

is
not
local in the sense of Def.
3.9
. In principle, one could expand the definition to include the orbits
of local Lagrangians under
\mathrm{Diff}(M)
, such that symmetries of Lagrangian field theories may genuinely form a
groupoid

(and not a group). We do not follow this route in the current manuscript, as to avoid unnecessary confusion with the standard nomenclature.

To rigorously phrase the variational calculus and infinitesimal local symmetries of local Langrangians, the notions of vector fields
and differential forms on the infinite jet bundle become indispensable. We now move to the description of these concepts within the
category of smooth sets.

4
Differential geometry on the infinite jet bundle

4.1
Tangent bundle and vector fields

The original manuscripts on the infinite jet bundle
[
Ta79
]
[
Sau89
]
[
An89
]
define vector fields algebraically as
derivations on the algebra of functions
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. We define the tangent bundle to the infinite jet bundle directly as a smooth set,
and show how vector fields in the above sense are recovered as its geometrical smooth sections inside the category of smooth sets. When we enrich
our spaces with infinitesimal structure, we will show in
[
GS24
]
how this is naturally recovered as the ‘synthetic tangent bundle’ of the infinite jet bundle.

Recall by (
51
) that the infinite jet bundle smooth set may be equivalently identified as

y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\,y(J^{k}_{M}F)\,.
Similarly, there is an induced diagram of finite-dimensional tangent bundles

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{d}{\pi^{k}_{k-1}}}T(J^{k-1}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\cdots\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{1}_{M}F)\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{d}\pi^{1}_{0}}T(J^{0}_{M}F)\cong TF\,.

with the maps being the pushforwards of the projections
\{\pi^{k}_{k-1}:J^{k}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{k-1}_{M}F\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
.
Embedding the diagram along
\mathrm{SmthMfd}{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{y}}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, we define the tangent bundle of
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

as the corresponding limit.

Definition 4.1

(
Infinite jet tangent bundle).

The tangent bundle smooth set
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\in\mathrm{SmthSet}
of the infinite jet bundle
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is defined as

\displaystyle T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big):=\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathrm{lim}}^{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\,y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\,.

(59)

The points of this space, i.e., the set of tangent vectors of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is given by

T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)(*)=\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathrm{lim}}^{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\,y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)(*):=\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathrm{lim}}^{\mathrm{Set}}\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\,,

which is represented by

\bigcup_{s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F}T_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\bigcup_{s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F}\big\{\{X_{s}^{k}\in T_{\pi_{k}(s)}(J^{k}_{M}F)\,|\,\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}X^{k+1}_{s}=X^{k}_{s}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,\big\}\ .

That is, a tangent vector
X_{s}\in T_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
at
s=j^{\infty}_{p}\phi\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
is represented by an (infinite) family
of tangent vectors
\{X^{k}_{s}\in T_{\pi_{k}(s)}(J^{k}_{M}F)\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
on each finite order tangent bundle at

\pi_{k}(s)=\pi_{k}(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)=j^{k}_{p}\phi\in J^{k}_{M}F
, compatible along the pushforward projections.
In a local coordinate chart
\big\{x^{\mu},\{u_{I}^{a}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}
of
J^{\infty}_{M}F\in\mathrm{LocProMan}
around
s
, such a family
may be represented by an infinite (formal) sum

\displaystyle X_{s}=X^{\mu}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\Big|_{s}\,,

(60)

for an infinite list of real numbers
\{X^{\mu},Y_{I}^{a}\}\subset\mathbbm{R}
, with each
X^{k}
corresponding to the case where the
sum is terminated at order
|I|=k
.

Lemma 4.2

(
Infinite jet tangent vectors as infinitesimal curves).

The set of tangent vectors
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)(*)
is in bijection with equivalence classes
of curves in
J^{\infty}_{M}F\in\mathrm{LocProMan}

\displaystyle T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)(*)\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\rm FrMan}}(\mathbbm{R}^{1},J^{\infty}_{M}F)/\sim_{\mathcal{O}(t^{1})}\cong y(J^{\infty}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{1})/\sim_{\mathcal{O}(t^{1})}\,,

(61)

where the equivalence relation is agreement up to first order derivatives at
0\in\mathbbm{R}^{1}
.

Proof.

By the limit property of
T\big(y(J^{\infty}F)\big)
, a tangent vector
X_{s}
at a point
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
corresponds uniquely
to a compatible family
\{X_{s}^{k}\in T_{\pi_{k}(s)}(J^{k}_{M}F)\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
of tangent vectors on each finite order jet bundle.
Since each
J^{k}_{M}F
is a finite-dimensional manifold, each tangent vector
X^{k}_{s}
in the family is represented by some curve

\gamma^{k}_{s}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\rightarrow J^{k}_{M}F
through
\pi_{k}(s)\in J^{k}_{M}F
, i.e.,

X^{k}_{s}=\dot{\gamma}^{k}_{s}(0)=[\gamma]
, with the equivalence relation being that the derivatives agree up to first-order derivatives.
The compatibility relation
\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}X_{s}^{k+1}=X_{s}^{k}
implies that the family of curves is compatible in the sense that

[\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ\gamma^{k}_{s}]=[\gamma^{k-1}_{s}]
.

By restricting on a compatible family of charts around each
\pi_{k}(s)\in J^{k}_{M}F
, a representative family of curves may be chosen such

\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ\gamma^{\,k}_{s}=\gamma^{k-1}_{s}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{k}_{M}F\,,

for each
k\in\mathbbm{N}
. By the limit property of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, such a family uniquely corresponds to a curve
\gamma_{s}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
.
A different choice of a representative family
\{\gamma_{s}^{k^{\prime}}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\rightarrow J^{k}_{M}F\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
determines another curve

\gamma_{s}^{\prime}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
, which however is equivalent to
\gamma_{s}
under the induced relation. The induced equivalence on

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{FrMfd}}(\mathbbm{R}^{1},J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is as expected: Any two curves through
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
are equivalent

\gamma_{s}\sim\gamma_{s}^{\prime}
if and only if

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big|_{t=0}(\phi\circ\gamma_{s})=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big|_{t=0}(\phi\circ\gamma_{s}^{\prime})

for any local chart
\phi
valued in
\mathbbm{R}^{\infty}
around
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}
.
∎

The equivalence classes of curves viewpoint is taken as a definition of tangent vectors in
[
Sau89
]
.
On the other hand, the definition as a smooth set allows us to consider more than bare points in the tangent
bundle of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
. The
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots are given by

\displaystyle T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})={\rm lim}_{k}^{{\mathrm{SmthSet}}}\,y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)(\mathbbm{R}^{n}):={\rm lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{Set}}\,{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Man}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{n},T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\,,

(62)

which is represented by

\bigcup_{s^{n}\in y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})}\Big\{\{X_{s^{n}}^{k}:\mathbbm{R}^{n}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\;\big|\;\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ X^{k+1}_{s^{n}}=X^{k}_{s^{n}}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,\Big\}\ ,

where
X^{k}_{s^{n}}(x)\in T_{\pi_{k}\circ s^{n}(x)}J^{k}_{M}F
for each
x\in\mathbbm{R}^{n}
being implicit in the notation.
In local charts, one may further represent such families with infinite formal sums as done with
*
-plots

\displaystyle X_{s^{n}}=X^{\mu}_{s^{n}}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s^{n}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I,s^{n}}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\Big|_{s^{n}}\,,

(63)

where now
\big\{X^{\mu}_{s^{n}},\{Y^{a}_{I,s^{n}}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}
denote an infinite list of smooth functions, with
X^{k}_{s^{n}}

corresponding to the case where the sum is terminated at order
|I|=k
.

On the fiber
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots over each
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plot of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, there exists a
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})
-linear structure
induced by the linear structures on each
T(J^{k}_{M}F)
, natural in
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
. Thus, there is a map of smooth sets

\displaystyle+\;\;:\;\;T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\times_{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)}T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

(64)

\displaystyle\big(\{X^{k}_{s^{n}}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,,\,\{\tilde{X}^{k}_{s^{n}}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\{X^{k}_{s^{n}}+\tilde{X}^{k}_{s^{n}}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,,

which respects the
y(\mathbbm{R})
fiber multiplication smooth map

\displaystyle\cdot\;\;:\;\;y(\mathbbm{R})\times T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

(65)

\displaystyle\big(f_{n},\{X^{k}_{s^{n}}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\{f_{n}\cdot X_{s^{n}}^{k}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,,

where
f_{n}:\mathbbm{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
is a plot of
\mathbbm{R}
. In terms of their coordinate formal sum representations,
the smooth addition map corresponds to the intuitive formal addition

\!\!\!\bigg(\!\!X^{\mu}_{s^{n}}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s^{n}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\!\!\!Y^{a}_{I,s^{n}}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\Big|_{s^{n}}\!\!\bigg)+\bigg(\!\!\tilde{X}^{\mu}_{s^{n}}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s^{n}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\tilde{Y}^{a}_{I,s^{n}}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\Big|_{s^{n}}\!\!\bigg):=\big(X^{\mu}_{s^{n}}+\tilde{X}^{\mu}_{s^{n}}\big)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s^{n}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!\big(Y^{a}_{I,s^{n}}+\tilde{Y}^{a}_{I,s^{n}}\big)\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\Big|_{s^{n}}.

and similarly for the fiber scalar multiplication.

Remark 4.3

(
Infinite jet tangent bundle as a Fréchet manifold).

Another option would be to show the limit exists as an object of LocProMan,
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{FrMfd}}T(J^{k}_{M}F)

which is suggested in
[
Sau89
]
. Indeed, this is the case and the proof is identical to that for

J^{\infty}_{M}F:=\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{FrMfd}}J^{k}_{M}F
of
[
Ta79
]
[
Sau89
]
. The local model is
\mathbbm{R}^{\infty}\times\mathbbm{R}^{\infty}\in\mathrm{FrMfd}
,
with local charts on
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
taking the form
\big\{x^{\mu},\{u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|},\dot{x}^{\mu},\{\dot{u}^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}
.
However, upon further embedding the resulting Fréchet manifold into
\mathrm{SmthSet}
, this will necessarily coincide with our definition
above as per Rem.
3.4
.
The following discussion could be carried within
\mathrm{FrMfd}
and then embedded in smooth sets. We choose to do this directly in
\mathrm{SmthSet}
, so as to stress
that most of the constructions and definitions appearing depend mostly on universal properties, and rarely on analytical details.

From the plot descriptions, there is an evident smooth projection map

p:T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

as expected intuitively. Naturally, we define vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
as sections of the projection map,
internally to SmoothSet.

Definition 4.4

(
Vector fields on the jet bundle).

The set of smooth vector fields on the infinite jet bundle is defined as smooth sections of its tangent bundle

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\Gamma_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\big(T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)=\Big\{X:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\;|\;p\circ X=\mathrm{id}_{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)}\Big\}\,.

(66)

Let us unwind the definition in more concrete terms. By the limit property for
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

a map
X:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)
corresponds to a family

\{X^{k}:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
such that the diagram

\textstyle{y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}}
\textstyle{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;X^{k-1}}
\textstyle{y\big(T(J^{k-1}_{M}F)\big)}

commutes for all
k\in\mathbbm{N}
. Since
\mathrm{LocProMan}{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{y}}\mathrm{SmthSet}
is fully faithfully,
this is equivalently a family of Fréchet manifold maps
\{X^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}

such that the corresponding diagram commutes, i.e.,
\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\,X^{k}=X^{k-1}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{k-1}_{M}F)
.
Moreover, the section condition of X over
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
interpreted in terms of Fréchet manifolds as above,
corresponds to the condition

X^{k}(s)\in T_{\pi^{\infty}_{k}(s)}J^{k}_{M}F

for each
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
and every

k\in\mathbbm{N}
. By Lem.
3.6
, each
X^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)
is locally
of finite order, i.e., locally around each
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
,

X^{k}|_{\pi^{-1}_{j_{s}}(U_{\pi_{j_{s}}(s)})}=\pi^{*}_{j_{s}}(X^{k}_{j_{s}})\,,

for some
X^{k}_{j_{s}}:J^{j_{s}}_{M}F|_{U_{\pi_{j_{s}}(s)}}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)
. In particular, such a family

\{X^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}

may be represented in a local chart of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
by an infinite (formal) sum

\displaystyle X=X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\,,

(67)

for an infinite list of (locally defined) smooth functions
\big\{X^{\mu},\{Y_{I}^{a}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}\subset C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),
39
39

39

Even though these are defined in some chart
V\subset J^{\infty}_{M}F
, it may be that they only locally
factor through finite order jet bundles around each
x\in V\subset J^{\infty}_{M}F
.

with each
X^{k}
corresponding to the case where the sum is terminated at order
|I|=k
.

Of course, it might be that every smooth map
X^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)
in a compatible family is
globally

of finite order on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, so that
X^{k}=\pi^{*}_{j_{k}}X^{k}_{j_{k}}
for some
X^{k}_{j_{k}}:J^{j_{k}}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)
.
In such a case, the infinite sum representation above has coefficients of fixed orders, i.e., of order up to
j_{k}
when the sum
is terminated at
|I|=k
. We denote the vector subspace of globally finite order vector fields by

\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\subset\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

These are the vector fields that usually appear in the field-theoretic examples, and so
[
An89
]
focuses
on this vector subspace. However, this subset cannot be identified with the full set of smooth sections of some bundle.
For this reason, we allow for locally finite order vector fields as in
[
Ta79
]
[
Sau89
]
.

The fiber-wise
y(\mathbbm{R})
-linear structure on
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)
induces a
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-module
structure on the set of vector fields on
\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\Gamma_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\big(T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)
,
simply by composition along (
64
) and
(
65
). It is easy to see that in the formal coordinate representation above,
this corresponds to the usual formal addition and multiplication, e.g.

\displaystyle f\cdot X=f\cdot X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}f\cdot Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\,,

(68)

for any
f\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. Note, however, the subset of globally finite order vector fields is not
a module of
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
but only of
C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

The following is stated in
[
Ta79
, §2.5]
with “proof left as trivial”. We provide a proof for completeness.

Lemma 4.5

(
Vector fields and derivations on the jet bundle).

Vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
are in 1-1 correspondence with derivations

C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,

\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong{\rm Der}\big(C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\,.

Proof.

Let
X
be a vector field on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
represented by a family
\{X^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
,
as above. Recall any function
f\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is, locally around
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
, the pullback

\pi^{*}_{k_{s}}f_{k_{s}}=f|_{\pi^{-1}_{k_{s}}(U_{\pi_{k_{s}}(s)})}
for some
f_{k_{s}}\in C^{\infty}\big(U_{\pi_{k_{s}}(s)}\subset J^{k_{s}}_{M}F\big)
.
Define
X(f)\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
by

\displaystyle X(f)(s):

\displaystyle=X^{k_{s}}(s)\big(f_{k_{s}}\big),

which is well defined, since for any other local representative
f_{k^{\prime}_{s}}
, without loss of generality
k^{\prime}_{s}\geq k_{s}
, we have

X^{k_{s}}(s)(f_{k_{s}})=\mathrm{d}\pi^{k^{\prime}_{s}}_{k_{s}}X^{k_{s}}(s)(f_{k_{s}})=X^{k^{\prime}_{s}}(s)\big({\pi^{k^{\prime}_{s}}_{k_{s}}}^{*}f_{k_{s}}\big)=X^{k^{\prime}_{s}}(s)(f_{k^{\prime}_{s}})\,.

The derivation property on a product
f\cdot g\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
follows since it holds locally for any
finite order representative. It remains to show that
X(f):J^{\infty}M\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
is indeed smooth.
Since
X^{k}
is also locally of finite order

X^{k_{s}}|_{\pi^{-1}_{j_{s}}(U_{\pi_{j_{s}}(s)})}=\pi^{*}_{j_{s}}X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}\,,

for some
X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}:J^{j_{s}}_{M}F|_{U_{\pi_{j_{s}}(s)}}\rightarrow T(J^{k_{s}}_{M}F)
, without loss of generality

j_{s}\geq k_{s}
, we get that

X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}(f_{k_{s}})\in C^{\infty}\big(U_{\pi_{j_{s}}(s)}\subset J^{j_{s}}_{M}F\big)

given by
X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}\big(f_{k_{s}}\big)(q)=X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}(q)\big(f_{k_{s}}\big).
It follows that

\displaystyle X\big(f\big)(s)

\displaystyle=X^{k_{s}}(s)\big(f_{k_{s}}\big)=\big(\pi^{*}_{j_{s}}X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}\big)(s)\big(f_{k_{s}}\big)

\displaystyle=X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}\big(\pi_{j_{s}}(s)\big)\big(f_{k_{s}}\big)=X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}\big(f_{k_{s}}\big)\big(\pi_{j_{s}}(s)\big)

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}_{j_{s}}\big(X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}(f_{k_{s}})\big)(s)

that is,
X(f)|_{\pi^{-1}_{j_{s}}(U_{\pi_{j_{s}}(s)})}=\pi^{*}_{j_{s}}\big(X^{k_{s}}_{j_{s}}(f_{k_{s}})\big)
around each
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
.
Being locally of finite order,
X(f):J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
is smooth.

It follows similarly that any derivation
\tilde{X}:C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
acts on local finite order representatives as above,
and hence defines a vector field on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
.
∎

The statement and proof restrict to the case of globally finite order vector fields and derivations of globally finite order functions,

\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\mathrm{Der}\big(C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\,.

Similarly, it further restricts to tangent vectors
and germs of smooth functions at any point
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
,

T_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\mathrm{Der}\big(C^{\infty}_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),\mathbbm{R}\big)\,,

which coincides with the definition of tangent vectors of
[
Ta79
]
.

In a local chart for
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, using the formal sum representative

X=X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\,,

the action on a function
f\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
corresponds to

X(f)|_{\pi^{-1}_{k_{s}}(U_{\pi_{k_{s}}(s)})}:=X^{\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{k_{s}}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial f}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\,,

where the formal sum necessarily terminates to the (local) finite order of
f=\pi_{k_{s}}^{*}f_{k_{s}}
around each point
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F

‘since the higher derivatives act trivially’. The derivation point of view allows to define a Lie algebra structure on
\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, as usual by

\displaystyle[X,\tilde{X}](f):=X(\tilde{X}(f))-\tilde{X}\big(X(f)\big)\,,

(69)

which locally may be represented via the usual coordinate formula, now involving an infinite formal sum.
Naturally, it restricts to a Lie algebra structure on the subspace of globally finite order vector fields.

4.2
Horizontal splitting

In finite-dimensional manifolds, the tangent bundle
TE\rightarrow E
of every smooth fiber bundle
E\rightarrow M
splits
noncanonically
into
a vertical and horizontal subbundle
VE\oplus HE\rightarrow E
, with each splitting corresponding to a
choice
of a connection on
E
.
In the context of infinite-dimensional manifolds and smooth sets, such a splitting is not guaranteed to exist.
40
40

40

For
any

chosen notion of a tangent bundle, when it exists.

Nevertheless, in the case of the infinite jet bundle, the tangent bundle

T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
does have a
smooth
horizontal splitting, which is in fact
canonical
.

The total projection
\pi^{\infty}_{M}:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\pi^{\infty}_{0}}y(F)\xrightarrow{\pi_{M}}y(M)
to the base
M
induces
a ‘pushforward projection’ between the tangent bundle smooth sets over
M

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M}:T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(TM)

(70)

which acts on
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots of (
62
) as

\displaystyle\big\{X_{s^{n}}^{k}:\mathbbm{R}^{n}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\;\big|\;\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ X^{k+1}_{s^{n}}=X^{k}_{s^{n}}\big\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{M}\circ X^{k}_{s^{n}}\in y(TM)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})\,,

for any
X^{k}_{s^{n}}
in the representing set of the plot, and
\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{M}:T(J^{k}_{M}F)\rightarrow TM
the pushforward of

\pi^{k}_{M}:J^{k}_{M}F\rightarrow M
. This is well-defined since

\mathrm{d}\pi^{k+1}_{M}\circ X^{k+1}_{s^{n}}=\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{M}\circ\mathrm{d}\pi^{k+1}_{k}\circ X^{k+1}_{s^{n}}=\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{M}\circ X^{k}_{s^{n}}
.
At the level of points and in local coordinates, the map acts on a tangent vector
X_{s}\in T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M})\big)(*)
at
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
by

X_{s}=X^{\mu}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\Big|_{s}\longmapsto X^{\mu}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{\pi_{M}^{\infty}(s)}\,.

Definition 4.6

(
Vertical subbundle).

The (smooth) vertical subbundle of
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)
is defined as the equalizer of
\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M}
and the canonical map

0_{M}:T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\rightarrow y(TM)
,

\textstyle{VJ^{\infty}_{M}F:=\mathrm{eq}\Big(\!T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M}}
\textstyle{y(TM)\!\Big).}

Concretely, the vertical sub-bundle is the sub-object
VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow TJ^{\infty}_{M}F

whose
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots are represented by families

\displaystyle\Big\{X_{s^{n}}^{k}:\mathbbm{R}^{n}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\;\big|\;\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ X^{k+1}_{s^{n}}=X^{k}_{s^{n}}\,,\,\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{M}\circ X^{k}_{s^{n}}=(\pi^{\infty}_{M}\circ s^{n},0)\Big\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,.

(73)

For instance, a vertical tangent vector at
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
,
X_{s}\in VJ^{\infty}_{M}(F)(*)
is represented by
a family of compatible vertical vectors
\{X^{k}_{s}\in V_{\pi^{\infty}_{k}(s)}J^{k}_{M}F\}
in the usual finite-dimensional sense.
In a local coordinate chart for
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, such a family is represented by an infinite formal sum

\displaystyle X_{s}=0+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y_{I}^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{s}\,.

(74)

Example 4.7

(
Prolongated tangent vectors to field space).

Let
\phi_{t}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times M\rightarrow F
be a
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-parametrized section of
F\rightarrow M
, i.e.,
an
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
as in Def.
2.12
.
The jet prolongation (
52
) of the plot
j^{\infty}\phi_{t}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F

defines an
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-parametrized section of
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow M
. For each
x\in M
we get a smooth curve

j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x):\mathbbm{R}^{1}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
, through
j^{\infty}\phi_{0}(x)\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
, which by
Lem.
4.2
defines a
vertical
tangent vector denoted suggestively by

\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}\;\;\in V_{j^{\infty}\phi_{0}(x)}J^{\infty}_{M}F\,.

The verticality follows immediately in terms of the compatible family representation

\big\{\partial_{t}j^{k}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}\;\;\in V_{j^{k}\phi_{0}(x)}J^{k}_{M}F\big\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,,

where each tangent vector is vertical in the usual sense, since each curve
j^{k}\phi_{t}(x):\mathbbm{R}^{1}\rightarrow J^{k}_{M}F
is
contained in the fiber above
x\in M
. Yet equivalently, the action on a smooth function
f\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

via Lem.
4.5
is given by

\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)\big|_{t=0}(f):=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Big|_{t=0}\big((j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x))^{*}f\big)=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Big|_{t=0}\big(f\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)\big)\,,

whereby in a local chart for
J^{\infty}_{M}F
and using the chain rule
41
41

41

The chain rule applies as in
the finite-dimensional manifold case, since around the point
j^{\infty}\phi_{0}(x)\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
,

f
is necessarily of finite order. In particular, the sum in the
formula below terminates.

\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)\big|_{t=0}(f)=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Big|_{t=0}\big(\partial_{I}\phi^{a}_{t}(x)\big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}f\big(j^{\infty}\phi_{0}(x)\big)\,.

Thus in local coordinates,

\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)\big|_{t=0}=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Big|_{t=0}\big(\partial_{I}\phi^{a}_{t}(x)\big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}\phi_{0}(x)}=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi^{a}_{t}|_{t=0}\big)(x)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}\phi_{0}(x)}\,.

Varying over
x\in M
, we get a section
\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}:M\rightarrow VJ^{\infty}F

covering
j^{\infty}\phi_{0}

\textstyle{VJ^{\infty}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\phantom{aa}}
\scriptstyle{{j^{\infty}\phi}_{0}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}F\,.}

By the local coordinate formula, this section depends only on the induced tangent vector
\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\in T\mathcal{F}(*)
on field space.
Thus, since any tangent vector
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}\in T\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(VF)(*)
is represented by such line plots (Lem.
2.17
),
the above defines a ‘jet prolongation’ map which we suggestively
42
42

42

This is not only useful notation. Indeed, it is not hard to see that

VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\cong J^{\infty}_{M}(VF)
as bundles (of smooth sets) over
M
, making the notation consistent with the jet prolongation of a section.
denote by the same symbol

\displaystyle j^{\infty}:T\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}(VF)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbold{\Gamma}(VJ^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}=\mathcal{Z}^{a}_{\phi}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;j^{\infty}\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{a}}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\,,

with a similar form on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of
\mathcal{F}
. For future reference, we note that for each choice of
x\in M
,
composition with evaluation at
x
may be viewed as a (smooth) ‘pushforward’ map

\displaystyle T\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow VJ^{\infty}_{M}F

(75)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}

\displaystyle\longmapsto(j^{\infty}\mathcal{Z}_{\phi})(x)

of tangent vectors on the field space
\mathcal{F}
, to (vertical) tangent vectors on the infinite jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}F
.

Naturally, smooth sections of the vertical sub-bundle
VJ^{\infty}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}F
as in
Def.
4.4
, define the
distribution of
vertical
vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F

\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\Gamma_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}(VJ^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

From the coordinate representation of general vector fields (
67
) and
vertical tangent vectors (
74
), it follows that any vertical vector
field
X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is locally represented by an infinite sum of the form

X=0+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\,,

for arbitrary (local) functions
\{Y^{a}_{I}\}\subset C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.
It follows immediately that
\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is closed under the Lie bracket of
\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

As with finite-dimensional fibrations, the vertical sub-bundle fits into a natural short exact sequence of smooth sets

43
43

43

By which
we mean a short exact sequence of vector spaces of fiber
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots over each
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plot of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
.

over
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle 0_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{y(M)}y(TM)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\,,

(76)

where the fibered product on the right is defined now in SmoothSet, with
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots being pairs of plots that project to the same plot in
M
.
Equivalently, this may be computed as the limit of the finite-dimensional fibered products
J^{k}_{M}F\times_{M}TM
in LocProMan,

J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM:=\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{FrMfd}}(J^{k}_{M}F\times_{M}TM)
,
44
44

44

The coordinate charts on which takes the form

\{x^{\mu},\dot{x}^{\mu},u^{a}_{\mu},u^{a}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}},\cdots\}
with
\{\dot{x}^{\mu}\}
denoting the fiber coordinates of
TM
.

and then embedded via
y:\mathrm{LocProMan}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{SmthSet}
, so that

y(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM)\cong y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{y(M)}y(TM)\,.

The third map is naturally given on
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots by

\displaystyle\{X_{s^{n}}^{k}:\mathbbm{R}^{n}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\,|\,\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ X^{k+1}_{s^{n}}=X^{k}_{s^{n}}\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\longmapsto(s^{n},\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{M}\circ X^{k}_{s^{n}})\,,

extending the usual point-set surjection
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM
.
The crucial property of the infinite jet bundle is that the above sequence has a
canonical splitting

H:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{y(M)}y(TM)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\,,

in contrast to the corresponding sequence of any finite order jet bundle
J^{k}_{M}F
.
That is, there is a canonical connection on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, usually referred to as the
Cartan connection
.
We recall the description of the splitting at the point set level as described in
[
Ta79
]
[
Sau89
]
,
and we show how this extends to a smooth map of bundles over
J^{\infty}_{M}F
in SmoothSet.

Let
j^{k}_{p}\phi\in J^{k}_{M}F
and choose a representative (local) section
\tilde{\phi}:U\subset M\rightarrow F
such that

j^{k}\tilde{\phi}(p)=j^{k}_{p}\phi
. The induced pushforward map

\mathrm{d}(j^{k}\tilde{\phi})_{p}:T_{p}M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{j^{k}_{p}\phi}(J^{k}_{M}F)\,,

is given in local coordinates by

\displaystyle X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{p}\;\longmapsto\;

\displaystyle\;\;X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{j^{k}_{p}\phi}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\tilde{\phi}^{a}(p)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{k}_{p}\phi}\bigg)

\displaystyle=X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{j^{k}_{p}\phi}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{k}u^{a}_{I+\mu}(j^{k+1}_{p}\tilde{\phi})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{k}_{p}\phi}\bigg)\,.

The map depends only on the
(k+1)
-jet at
p\in M
of the chosen representative section
\tilde{\phi}
,
and evidently
smoothly
so. Hence, for each
k\in\mathbbm{N}
the assignment defines a smooth map of bundles over
J^{k}_{M}F

\displaystyle H^{k}:J^{k+1}_{M}F\times_{M}TM

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)

(77)

\displaystyle(j^{k+1}_{p}\phi,X_{p})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathrm{d}(j^{k}\phi)_{p}(X_{p})

where, by slight abuse of notation,
\phi:U\subset M\rightarrow F
on the right-hand side is any representative local section of

j^{k+1}_{p}\phi
. It is easy to see that these fit into the commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
