The intuition behind the smooth horizontal splitting of
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)
is that the above smooth
bundle maps ‘stabilize at the
k\rightarrow\infty
limit’. Indeed, there is an injective
point-set
map

\displaystyle J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)(*)

(84)

\displaystyle(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi\,,\,X_{p})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)_{p}(X_{p}):=\big\{\mathrm{d}(j^{k}\phi)_{p}(X_{p})\big\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\,,

which splits the
*
-plot sequence of (
76
), where
\phi:U\subset M\rightarrow F
is a representative
local section of
j^{\infty}_{p}\phi
. In the coordinate representation (
60
) of tangent vectors
in
T_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}J^{\infty}_{M}F
, the map takes the form

\displaystyle\bigg(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi\,,\,X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{p}\bigg)\;\longmapsto\;X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\bigg)\,,

(85)

as usually written in the existing literature, e.g.
[
Sau89
]
. The tangent vectors on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
in the image are
interpreted as horizontal lifts of tangent vectors on
M
.

For future reference, note that by construction, for any choice of field configuration
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
, this defines a
smooth
pushforward map

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi):TM

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

(86)

\displaystyle X_{p}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)_{p}(X_{p}):=X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\phi^{a}}{\partial x^{I+\mu}}(p)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\bigg)\,,

with a similar action on higher plots of tangent vectors on
M
. Smoothness here follows by the limit property of
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)
,
and since each of the finite jet order maps are by construction smooth.

In fact, more generally, the map of Eq. (
84
) is naturally identified as part of a smooth
splitting of the corresponding
smooth sets.

Proposition 4.8

(
Smooth splitting).

The family of smooth bundle maps
\{H^{k}:J^{k+1}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
determines a map of smooth sets

\displaystyle H:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{y(M)}y(TM)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\,,

(87)

which splits the corresponding exact sequence (
76
).

Proof.

This is essentially an application of (a slight variation of) Prop.
3.7
. More explicitly, by the limit property
of
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)=\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{SmthSet}}y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)
and the fully faithful embedding

y:\mathrm{LocProMan}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{FrMfd}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{SmthSet}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\Big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{y(M)}y(TM)\,,\,T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\Big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM)\,,\,T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{Set}}\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\Big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM)\,,\,y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{Set}}\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{FrMfd}}\big(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\,,\,T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\,.

Hence a smooth map
f:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM)\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F\big)
corresponds to a family of smooth Fréchet maps

\{f^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\}_{k}\in\mathbbm{N}
such that
\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ f^{k}=f^{k-1}
, and vice-versa.

By Lem.
3.6
, the (set theoretic) maps

(\pi_{k+1}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{k+1}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)

are smooth Fréchet maps for each
k\in\mathbbm{N}
,
45
45

45

Lemma
3.6
applies verbatim for any limit in LocProMan, for instance in the case of

J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\in\mathrm{LocProMan}
.
being the pullback of finite order maps (globally in this case). Furthermore, by the
commutativity of diagram (
4.2
) they satisfy

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ\big(\pi_{k+1}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{k}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\pi^{k}_{k-1}\circ H^{k}\circ(\pi_{k+1}\circ\mathrm{id})

\displaystyle=H^{k-1}\circ(\pi^{k+1}_{k}\times\mathrm{id})\circ(\pi_{k+1}\times\mathrm{id})

\displaystyle=H^{k-1}\circ(\pi_{k}\times\mathrm{id})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{k}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{k-1}\,.

Thus the family
\big\{(\pi_{k+1}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\rightarrow T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
uniquely corresponds to a
map
H:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM)\rightarrow T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\,.
The underlying point set map is that of (
84
),
and since this splits the
*
-plot sequence, it follows that the
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plot sequences split too.
∎

In a local coordinate chart for
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, the explicit action on
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots may be seen as

\displaystyle y(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})

\displaystyle\bigg(s^{n}\,,\,X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{\pi_{M}\circ s^{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s^{n}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\circ s^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{s^{n}}\bigg)\,,

where
\{X^{\mu}:\mathbbm{R}^{n}\rightarrow M\}
denote the components of the corresponding plot in
TM
.

Corollary 4.9

(
Canonical horizontal splitting).

There is a canonical isomorphism of smooth sets

\displaystyle VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)}\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{y(M)}y(TM)\big)\xlongrightarrow{\sim}T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)

(88)

over
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. We denote the induced splitting by

T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\cong VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\oplus HJ^{\infty}_{M}F\,,

where the plots of
HJ^{\infty}_{M}F
are given by the image of the map of Prop.
4.8
.

The smooth sub-bundle
HJ^{\infty}_{M}F
is called the
Cartan Distribution
on the infinite jet bundle.
The direct sum here means the fibered product as above, computed in SmoothSet, with the linear structure being fiberwise
for each
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plot over the induced plot of the base
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. This furthermore implies the splitting of

vector spaces
46
46

46

It may naturally be extended to an isomorphism of the corresponding smooth sets of sections.
,
in fact of
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-modules, of smooth vector fields

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\oplus\mathcal{X}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

(89)

where the two components denote smooth sections of the corresponding bundles, as in Def.
4.4
.
Hence, any vector field
X
on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
may be written as
X=X_{V}+X_{H}
, with
X_{V}
and
X_{H}
denoting sections of the
vertical and horizontal smooth sub-bundles, respectively. Taking into account the local coordinate representations for
a general vector field (
67
)

X=X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\,,

and those of vertical (
74
) and horizontal (
85
)
tangent vectors, it follows that the vertical and horizontal component vector fields may locally be represented by

\displaystyle X_{V}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\big(Y_{I}^{a}-X^{\mu}\cdot u^{a}_{I+\mu}\big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}

(90)

\displaystyle X_{H}

\displaystyle=X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\bigg)\,,

reproducing the usual formulas
[
An89
]
[
Sau89
]
. In particular, if
\hat{X}^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\in\Gamma_{M}(TM)

is a vector field on the base, then its horizontal lift is given by
(\pi^{\infty}_{M})^{*}\hat{X}^{\mu}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\big)\in\mathcal{X}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

Example 4.10

(
Horizontal lift of coordinate basis).

It is customary to denote the local basis for
horizontal vector fields, i.e., the horizontal lift of the local basis of coordinate vector fields
\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\}
, by

\displaystyle D_{\mu}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\,.

(91)

If
f:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
is a smooth function then for any smooth section
\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)
, we have

f\circ j^{\infty}\phi\in C^{\infty}(M)
. The lifts
\{D_{\mu}\}
encode the action of
\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\}
on
f\circ j^{\infty}\phi

via the chain rule, i.e.,

\displaystyle D_{\mu}(f)\circ j^{\infty}\phi

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\bigg)\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\frac{\partial\phi^{a}}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\Big(\frac{\partial f}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\big(f\circ j^{\infty}\phi\big)\,.

(92)

Finally, we note that the vertical vector fields
\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
are closed under the Lie bracket (
69
), by construction.
A crucial property of the Cartan connection is that the horizontal vector fields are also closed under the Lie bracket,

\displaystyle[X^{1}_{H},X^{2}_{H}]\,\in\mathcal{X}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

(93)

for all
X^{1}_{H},X^{2}_{H}\in\mathcal{X}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, as can be easily checked in local coordinates. Namely, the Cartan connection is
flat
.
Naturally, the splitting descends on the subspace of global finite order vector fields

\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{glb}}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\oplus\mathcal{X}_{H}^{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

with the same local representation formulas and involutive properties.

4.3
Differential forms

Given that the tangent bundle of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
has a natural fiber-wise linear structure, we will
define differential forms as smooth
y(\mathbbm{R})
-linear maps
T\big(y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
.
This will allow us to recover the differential forms as defined in
[
Ta79
]
[
An89
]
[
Sau89
]
.
Furthermore, as we will explain in
[
GS24
]
, this definition will be in line with the classifying nature

\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{1}
in the extended topos of thickened smooth sets, by abstract arguments; see Rem.
2.33
.
This identification is not completely clear in the current setting of smooth sets, and so we will use a different symbol for definite forms
as fiber-wise linear maps out of the tangent bundle. Nevertheless, we will prove that forms of ‘globally finite order’
on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, in the traditional sense, may be naturally identified as a subalgebra of de Rham forms defined via the classifying space (Lem.
4.15
).

Definition 4.11

(
1-forms infinity jet bundle).

The set of differential 1-forms on the infinite jet bundle is defined as

\displaystyle\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\big(T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,y(\mathbbm{R})\big)

(94)

with respect to the fiber-wise linear structure of (
64
) and (
65
).

As with finite-dimensional manifolds, any fiberwise linear map
\omega:T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
defines a map of
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-modules

\omega:\Gamma\big(T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

by pre-composing as

\big(X:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\longmapsto\big(\omega\circ X:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\big)\,,

where on the right-hand side we identify
\omega\circ X\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{FrMfd}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F,\mathbbm{R})
since

y:\mathrm{FrMfd}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{SmthSet}
is fully faithful. Tracing through the identifications, in a local coordinate chart around

s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
, such a map
\omega
preserving the
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-module structure (
68
)
must take the form

X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\;\longmapsto\;X^{\mu}\cdot\omega\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big)+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\cdot\omega\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big)\,\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

Since the sum on the right-hand side is assumed to be a well-defined smooth function on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
for all vector fields
X
,
it must necessarily terminate at some finite
|I|=k_{s}\in\mathbbm{N}
and so
\omega(\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}})=0
for
|I|>k_{s}
.
Hence, on the chart around
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
, we may represent
\omega
by a
finite sum

\displaystyle\omega=\omega_{\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{k_{s}}\omega_{a}^{I}\mathrm{d}u^{a}_{I}\,,

(95)

where
\big\{\omega_{\mu},\{\omega_{a}^{I}\}_{|I|\leq k_{s}}\big\}\subset C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
are some locally defined smooth functions.
By further restricting to a smaller neighborhood
U_{s}\subset J^{\infty}_{M}F
around
s
, we may assume these functions are also of
finite order and, without loss of generality, of the same order
k_{s}
. Given this local description, we see that the map

\omega:\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is necessarily of the form

\displaystyle\omega(X)(s^{\prime})=\omega_{k_{s}}\big(\pi_{k_{s}}(s^{\prime})\big)\left(\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}\left(X(s^{\prime})\right)\right)

(96)

locally for any
s^{\prime}\in U_{s}\subset J^{\infty}_{M}
for some local form
\omega_{k_{s}}\in\Omega^{1}(J^{k_{s}}_{M}F)
and
k_{s}\in\mathbbm{N}
.
Hence, we may think of 1-forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
as being ‘locally the pullback’ of finite order forms.

More precisely, we see that every 1-form on
\omega
on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is represented by a family

\displaystyle\Big\{\omega^{k_{s}}\in\Omega^{1}(U_{\pi_{k_{s}}(s)})\;\big|\;U_{\pi_{k_{s}}(s)}\subset J^{k_{s}}_{M}F\Big\}_{s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F}

(97)

of compatible locally defined 1-forms on finite order jet bundles. Compatible here means that for any two

s,s^{\prime}\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
with
k_{s^{\prime}}\geq k_{s}
such that
(\pi_{k_{s}}^{k_{s^{\prime}}})^{-1}(U_{k_{s}})\cap U_{k_{s}^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\subset J^{k_{s}}_{M}F
,
we have
(\pi_{k_{s}}^{k_{s^{\prime}}})^{*}\omega_{k_{s}}=\omega_{k_{s^{\prime}}}
.
47
47

47

Two such families
\{\omega^{k_{s}}\}_{s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F},\{\tilde{\omega}^{k_{s}}\}_{s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F}

determine the same 1-form on
J^{\infty}_{M}F

if and only if each pair
(\omega^{k_{s}},\tilde{\omega}^{k_{s}})
of finite degree local forms agree on their common overlap in
J^{k_{s}}_{M}F
.
Formally, such families represent
elements in the limit of the algebras of
germs of
1
-forms
on
J^{k}_{M}F
; see e.g.
[
GMS00
]
.

Formula (
96
) also determines the form of the corresponding map out of the tangent bundle
(Def.
4.11
) on
*
-plots

\displaystyle\omega:T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(*)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

\displaystyle X_{s}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\omega_{k_{s}}\big(\pi_{k_{s}}(s)\big)\big(\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}(X_{s})\big)

for some locally defined form
\omega_{k_{s}}\in\Omega^{1}(J^{k_{s}}_{M}F)
around
\pi_{k_{s}}(s)\in J^{k_{s}}_{M}F
,
and consequently on any
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plot.

Note that any finite order globally defined form
\omega^{k}\in\Omega^{1}(J^{k}_{M}F)
fits the above description and defines a

1
-form on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
by the same formulas. We denote the vector subspace of globally finite order 1-forms by

\displaystyle\Omega^{1}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\subset\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

(98)

in analogy to the case of smooth functions (or 0-forms). The local finite order viewpoint agrees with
[
Ta79
]
,
while both
[
An89
]
[
Sau89
]
define forms as being globally the pullback of finite order forms.
By the discussion above, the former has a natural
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-module structure and corresponds to the
full set of smooth fiber-wise linear maps out of the actual tangent bundle, while it also defines a (petit) sheaf on the topological
space
|J^{\infty}_{M}F|
. The latter is only is a module over
C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\subset C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

and corresponds to a subset of linear maps out of the tangent bundle, and it does
not
define a sheaf on the underlying topological space.

Remark 4.12

(
Infinity jet 1-form as Fréchet map).

Alternatively, if we consider
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
as a locally pro-manifold as per Rem.
4.3
, then by the
fully faithful embedding
y:\mathrm{LocProMan}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{SmthSet}
a 1-form is equivalently a fiber-wise linear Fréchet map

\omega:T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
. By Lem.
3.5
applied to the projective limit
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, such a
smooth map is locally around any
48
48

48

Strictly speaking, this holds around an open neighborhood of any

X_{s}\in T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. The topology of
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and the linearity assumption of
\omega
are crucial to descend to an open
neighborhood
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
.

s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F

\omega=(\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}})^{*}\omega_{k_{s}}\,,

where the pullback is by
\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}:T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow T(J^{k_{s}}_{M}F)
. When interpreted as acting on vector fields,
this reproduces the formula above. Furthermore, the locally pro-manifold
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is paracompact and has partitions of unity
[
Ta79
]
.
By extending tangent vectors to vector fields, via a partition of unity, it follows that any
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-linear map

\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
defines a fiberwise linear smooth map
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
.
As with finite-dimensional manifolds, this is another way to witness the bijection

\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}\big(T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,\mathbbm{R})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrm{-Mod}}\Big(\Gamma\big(\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\Big)\,.

Completely analogously, we may define
m
-forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, with the analogous identifications following verbatim.

Definition 4.13

(
Forms on infinite jet bundle).

The set of differential m-forms on the infinite jet bundle is defined as

\displaystyle\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.an.}}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\big(T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,y(\mathbbm{R})\big)\,,

(99)

i.e., smooth real-valued, fiber-wise linear antisymmetric maps with respect to the fiber-wise linear structure induced by
(
64
) and (
65
), on the
m
-fold fiber product

T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)}\cdots\times_{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)}T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

of the tangent bundle over the infinite jet bundle.

Concretely, the
m
-fold fiber product of the tangent bundle over
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is the smooth set with
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots given by
m
-tuples of
plots covering the same plot in
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,

\displaystyle T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})=\bigcup_{s^{n}\in y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})}\big\{(X^{1}_{s^{n}},\cdots,X^{m}_{s^{n}})\in T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})\times\cdots\times T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})\big\}\,,

(100)

where
p\circ X^{1}_{s^{n}}=\cdots=p\circ X^{m}_{s^{n}}=s^{n}\in y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})
being implicit, with each
X^{i}_{s^{n}}\in T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{n})
as in
(
62
). Equivalently, this may be seen as the limit computed directly in smooth sets

T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\mathrm{lim}_{k}^{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\,y\big(T(J^{k}_{M}F)\times_{J^{k}_{M}F}\cdots\times_{J^{k}_{M}F}T(J^{k}_{M}F)\big)\,.

Repeating the discussion for the case of a single vector field (Def.
4.4
), it follows that sections of the
m
-fold fibered product
above correspond to
m
-tuples of vector fields. Thus, any
m
-form
\omega\in\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
defines an antisymmetric map of
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-modules

\omega:\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times\cdots\times\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

by pre-composing as

\big((X^{1},\cdots,X^{m}):y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\longmapsto\big(\omega\circ(X^{1},\cdots,X^{m}):y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\big)\,.

As with 1-forms, in local coordinates in a neighborhood
U_{s}
around a point
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
we may represent an
m
-form by

\displaystyle\omega=\sum_{p+q=m}\,\sum_{I_{1},\cdots,I_{p}=0}^{k_{s}}\omega_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}a_{1}\cdots a_{q}}^{I_{1}\dots I_{q}}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\wedge\mathrm{d}u^{a_{1}}_{I_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}u^{a_{q}}_{I_{q}}\,,

(101)

where the sum terminates at some finite order
k_{s}
, and the coefficients are locally defined functions on
J^{k_{s}}_{M}F
.
That is, the map
\omega:\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times\cdots\times\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

is necessarily of the form

\displaystyle\omega(X^{1},\cdots,X^{m})(s^{\prime})=\omega_{k_{s}}\big(\pi_{k_{s}}(s^{\prime})\big)\Big(\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}\big(X^{1}(s^{\prime})\big),\cdots,\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}\big(X^{m}(s^{\prime})\big)\Big),

(102)

locally for any
s^{\prime}\in U_{s}\subset J^{\infty}_{M}
for some local form
\omega_{k_{s}}\in\Omega^{m}(J^{k_{s}}_{M}F)
and
k_{s}\in\mathbbm{N}
.
Hence, we may think of m-forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
as being ‘locally the pullback’ of finite order forms, and represent them by
compatible families of locally defined finite order
m
-forms
as in (
97
). The previous formula determines the form of the corresponding
map out of the tangent bundle
(Def.
4.13
) on
*
-plots

\displaystyle\omega:T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\cdots\times_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(*)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

\displaystyle(X^{1}_{s},\cdots,X^{m}_{s})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\omega_{k_{s}}\big(\pi_{k_{s}}(s)\big)\big(\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}(X^{1}_{s}),\cdots,\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}(X^{m}_{s})\big)

for some locally defined form
\omega_{k_{s}}\in\Omega^{1}(J^{k_{s}}_{M}F)
around
\pi_{k_{s}}(s)\in J^{k_{s}}_{M}F
, and consequently on any
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plot.
Thus, as in Rem.
4.12
, there is in fact a bijection

\displaystyle\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.an.}}\big(T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)-\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{antis.}}\big(\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times\cdots\times\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\,.\

(103)

\displaystyle\cong\bigwedge^{m}_{C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)}\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

We denote the vector subspace of globally finite order m-forms, i.e., those determined ‘as a pullback’ of
a single globally defined m-form

\omega^{k}\in\Omega^{m}(J^{k}_{M}F)
, by

\displaystyle\Omega^{m}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\subset\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

(104)

with the same comments applying as in
1
-forms
(
98
)
.

It is straightforward to algebraically define the usual Cartan calculus on
\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbbm{N}}\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,
with all the maps naturally descending to the subspace
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. For instance,
the de Rham differential is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}:\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{m+1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

(105)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega(X^{0},\cdots,X^{m}):=\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{i}\,X^{i}\big(\omega(X^{0},\cdots,\hat{X}^{i},\cdots,X^{m}\big)\big)+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\,\omega\big([X_{i},X_{j}],X_{0},\cdots,\hat{X}^{i},\cdots,\hat{X}^{j},\cdots,X^{m}\big)\,.

Equivalently, in terms of the local finite order representatives (
102
)
around some
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega(X^{0},\cdots,X^{m})(s^{\prime})=\mathrm{d}\omega_{k_{s}}\big(\pi_{k_{s}}(s^{\prime})\big)\Big(\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}\big(X^{0}(s^{\prime})\big),\cdots,\mathrm{d}\pi_{k_{s}}\big(X^{m}(s^{\prime})\big)\Big)\,,

or in terms of the local coordinate representation (
101
), via the usual formula

\mathrm{d}\omega=\bigg(\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{a}_{I}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\bigg)\omega\,,

with the sum necessarily terminating at some finite order
k_{s}
around each
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
, since the coefficients of
\omega
are locally of finite order.
From either representation above, it follows that
\mathrm{d}^{2}=0
and so
\big(\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),\mathrm{d}\big)
defines a cochain complex.
It turns out both the locally
[
Ta79
]
[
GMS00
]
and globally
[
An89
]
finite order de Rham cohomologies agree with the
cohomology the fiber bundle
F\rightarrow M
.

Proposition 4.14

(
Total de Rham cohomology jet bundle).

The projection map
\pi^{\infty}_{0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
induces a quasi-isomorphism

(\pi^{\infty}_{0})^{*}:\Omega^{\bullet}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)_{\mathrm{glb}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

and so isomorphisms on cohomology

\displaystyle H^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong H^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR,glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong H^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(F)\,.

(106)

In particular, any closed
m
-form
\omega
on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
decomposes as
\omega=(\pi_{0}^{\infty})^{*}\tilde{\omega}+\mathrm{d}k
for some

\tilde{\omega}\in\Omega^{m}(F)
and
k\in\Omega^{m-1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

The Lie derivative of a function
f\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
along any vector field
X
is defined by

\mathbb{L}_{X}(f):=X(f)\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. It extends to
m
-forms by
\mathbb{L}_{X}:=[\mathrm{d},\iota_{X}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{X}:\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

(107)

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathrm{d}(\iota_{X}\omega)+\iota_{X}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,,

where
\iota_{X}\omega:=\omega(X,-,\cdots,-)\in\Omega^{m-1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
denotes the contraction map.
As with the de Rham differential, this takes the usual coordinate form around any point
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
,
whereby
\omega
and
X
are of finite order. The usual Cartan calculus identities between the maps
\mathrm{d},\,\mathbb{L}_{X}

and
\iota_{X}
also follow, since they hold locally around every point

s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
for the corresponding finite order representatives.

Relation to de Rham forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
.

Since the infinite jet bundle is a smooth set
y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\in\mathrm{SmthSet}
,
the notion of de Rham
m
-forms (Def.
2.31
) as maps into the classifying space
\mathbold{\Omega}^{m}_{\mathrm{dR}}
applies.
In this case
49
49

49

Notice, such a simple argument cannot be applied to the case of arbitrary differential forms (Def.
2.27
)
and de Rham forms (Def.
2.31
) on the actual field space
\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
. Nevertheless, the above result can be
used to view
local
differential forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
(Def.
7.5
) as de Rham forms (Lem.
7.9
).
,
the relation to the traditional notion of differential
m
-forms as maps out of the tangent bundle
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
(Def.
4.13
)
is immediate, at least for those forms that are of globally finite order (cf. Rem.
2.33
).

Lemma 4.15

(Differential forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
as de Rham forms)
.

The subalgebra
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
of globally finite order differential forms
is canonically identified with a subalgebra of de Rham forms on the infinite jet bundle. That is, there is a canonical DGCA injection

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

Proof.

This follows by the finite dimensional manifold identification of Eq. (
39
). More explicitly,
let
\omega\in\Omega^{m}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
be a differential form of globally finite order, i.e.,

\omega=\pi^{*}_{k}\omega_{k}=\omega_{k}\circ\mathrm{d}\pi_{k}\quad:\quad T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T^{\times m}(J^{k}_{M}F)\xlongrightarrow{\omega_{k}}\mathbbm{R}

for a unique
\omega_{k}\in\Omega^{m}(J^{k}_{M}F)
, where
k
is the minimal such order. In particular,
\omega_{k}
is a differential form on a
finite-dimensional manifold, and so by the Yoneda Lemma
2.4
it corresponds uniquely to a map
(see Eq. (
39
))

\tilde{\omega}_{k}:y(J^{k}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Omega}^{m}_{\mathrm{dR}}\,

into the classifying space, i.e., a de Rham differential form on
y(J^{k}_{M}F)
. Precomposing with the projection

y(\pi_{k}):y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow y(J^{k}_{M}F)
we get

\tilde{\omega}:=\tilde{\omega}_{k}\circ y(\pi_{k})\quad:\quad J^{\infty}_{M}F\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Omega}^{m}_{\mathrm{dR}}\,,

which is the (unique) de Rham
m
-form corresponding to the traditional pullback form
\omega=\omega_{k}\circ\mathrm{d}\pi_{k}
.

It follows similarly that under this identification, the classifying the de Rham differential
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{dR}}
corresponds to the
traditional differential
\mathrm{d}
of (globally finite order) forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, and similarly for the corresponding wedge products.
Thus the DGCA of globally finite order forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
embeds into the de Rham forms (Def.
38
)
on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
defined via the classifying space
\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\bullet}
.
∎

We expect, but do not prove here, that de Rham forms actually exhaust all differential forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
. In other words, the above
inclusion should extend to a canonical bijection of all differential forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
and those defined via the classifying space

50
50

50

We note that this does not follow from a generic categorical argument, in that
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is a
limit
of finite
order jet bundles and not a
colimit
.

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

On the other hand, there is a canonical and all-important splitting of differential forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, into horizontal and
vertical components, which is naturally explained via the traditional tangent bundle picture, and is not at all obvious if one uses the
classifying space picture. This will become clear in the following section.

5
Euler-Lagrange dynamics via the infinite jet bundle

There is a natural bicomplex structure
\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
induced on the differential forms on the infinite jet bundle,
the so-called
variational bicomplex
. The name “variational” is justified, among other reasons, as the vertical differential may be
used to rigorously encode the integration-by-parts algorithm and the explicit algebraic form of the Euler–Lagrange equations via the
“Euler–Lagrange source form” of a Lagrangian. Using the latter, we may express the space of on-shell fields as a smooth set.

Furthermore, the horizontal forms and horizontal differential may be used to prove a version of Stokes’ Theorem on field space, and hence
inducing local (conserved) currents and charges on
\mathcal{F}
by ‘pulling back’ (horizontally) closed forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
.
The latter pullback viewpoint on currents on field space is furthermore useful in showing that local symmetries of a field theory
preserve the on-shell space of fields.

5.1
The variational bicomplex and EL equations

The splitting of the tangent bundle (Cor.
4.9
) induces a splitting on the set of 1-forms

\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

Definition 5.1

(
Horizontal and vertical 1-forms).

Let
\omega:T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
be a differential 1-form on the infinite jet bundle.

(i)

\omega
is called
horizontal
if the following composite is the zero map

\omega|_{{}_{VJ^{\infty}_{M}F}}:VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\;.

(ii)
Similarly, it is called
vertical
if the following composite is the zero map

\omega|_{{}_{HJ^{\infty}_{M}F}}:HJ^{\infty}_{M}F\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\;.

Note that these conditions are equivalent to
\omega
vanishing on all horizontal or vertical
*
-plots (and hence all such
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
-plots), respectively.
That is, vanishing on all horizontal or vertical tangent vectors at each point in
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, respectively. Acting on vector fields,
a
1
-form
\omega
is horizontal or vertical if and only if

\omega(X_{V})=0\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{or}\hskip 28.45274pt\omega(X_{H})=0

for all
X_{V}\in\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,
or all
X_{H}\in\mathcal{X}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, respectively. Since
1
-forms are
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
-linear maps, every 1-form
\omega
uniquely
decomposes as
\omega=\omega_{H}+\omega_{V}
, and so

\displaystyle\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\Omega^{1}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\oplus\Omega^{1}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

(108)

with the subspaces denoting vertical and horizontal 1-forms, respectively. Under the local coordinates representations
(
90
),
(
95
) around a point
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
, horizontal and vertical 1-forms take the form

\displaystyle\omega_{H}=(\omega_{H})_{\mu}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\hskip 28.45274pt\omega_{V}=\sum_{|I|=0}^{k_{s}}(\omega_{V})_{a}^{I}\cdot(\mathrm{d}u^{a}_{I}-u^{a}_{I+\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=:\sum_{|I|=0}^{k_{s}}(\omega_{V})_{a}^{I}\cdot\theta^{a}_{I}\,,

(109)

for some coefficient functions of local finite order, respectively. The locally spanning set of vertical 1-forms denoted by

\big\{\theta^{a}_{I}:=\mathrm{d}u^{a}_{I}-u^{a}_{I+\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\big\}_{|I|\in\mathbbm{N}}

is also referred to as ‘
contact basis forms
’ and the ideal
\Omega_{C}^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

they generate the ‘
contact forms
’, as in e.g.
[
An89
]
. Projecting onto each of the subspaces, the de Rham differential

\mathrm{d}:C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
decomposes as
\mathrm{d}=\mathrm{d}_{H}+\mathrm{d}_{V}

\displaystyle d_{H}+d_{V}:C^{\infty}_{M}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\oplus\Omega^{1}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

(110)

where in local coordinates

\mathrm{d}_{H}(f)=\bigg(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{I=0}^{k_{s}}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\bigg)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}=D_{\mu}(f)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{V}(f)=\sum_{|I|=0}^{k_{s}}\frac{\partial f}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\cdot\theta^{a}_{I}\,.

In particular, the action on local coordinates is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}(x^{\mu})=\mathrm{d}x^{\mu},\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{V}(x^{\mu})=0,\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{H}(u^{a}_{I})=u^{a}_{I+\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu},\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{V}(u^{a}_{I})=\theta^{a}_{I}\,.

(111)

Example 5.2

(
Action of prolongated tangent vector).

The action of the vertical tangent vector
\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}
induced by an
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot

\phi_{t}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)(\mathbbm{R}^{1})
of smooth sections, as in Ex.
4.7
,
may be equivalently given as

\displaystyle{\partial_{t}}|_{t=0}\big(f\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}f\big(\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}\big)=(\mathrm{d}_{H}+\mathrm{d}_{V})(f)\big(\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}\big)

(112)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{V}f\big(\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}\big)=\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{V}f\,,

by the verticality of the tangent vector, or explicitly in coordinates.

Proceeding in a similar manner, the splitting of
1
-forms (
108
) induces a decomposition on
m
-forms

\displaystyle\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\bigoplus_{p+q=m}\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

(113)

via

\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\bigwedge^{m}\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\bigwedge^{m}\big(\Omega^{1}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\oplus\Omega^{1}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)
.
In other words,
\omega\in\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
if and only if

\omega(X_{V}^{1},\cdots,X_{V}^{p},-,\cdots,-)=0\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\omega(X_{H}^{1},\cdots,X_{H}^{q},-\cdots,-)=0

for any vertical
\{X_{V}^{1},\cdots,X_{V}^{p}\}\subset\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and any horizontal
\{X_{H}^{1},\cdots,X_{H}^{q}\}\subset\mathcal{X}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.
Thus there is a bi-grading on the algebra of differential forms

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbbm{N}}\bigoplus_{p+q=m}\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

(114)

where in a local chart around
s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F
a
(p,q)
-form
\omega\in\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
takes the form

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle=\sum_{I_{1},\cdots,I_{p}=0}^{k_{s}}\omega_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}a_{1}\cdots a_{q}}^{I_{1}\dots I_{q}}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\wedge\theta^{a_{1}}_{I_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta^{a_{q}}_{I_{q}}\,,

(115)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I_{1},\cdots,I_{p}=0}^{k_{s}}\omega_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}a_{1}\cdots a_{q}}^{I_{1}\dots I_{q}}\mathrm{d}_{H}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}_{H}x^{\mu_{p}}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a_{1}}_{I_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a_{q}}_{I_{q}}\,.

By the explicit local coordinate description, it is clear that any Lagrangian density map
L:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
of
Def.
3.9
is equivalently
a horizontal
(d,0)
-form
L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F).
More generally, smooth bundle maps
valued in
\wedge^{p}T^{*}M
(over M) are equivalently horizontal
(p,0)
-forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
,

\displaystyle P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\wedge^{p}T^{*}M\hskip 14.22636pt\iff\hskip 14.22636ptP\,\in\,\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

(116)

Definition 5.3

(
Horizontal and vertical differentials).

The horizontal and vertical differentials of degree
(p,q)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}^{p,q}:\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p+1,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),\hskip 56.9055pt\mathrm{d}_{V}^{p,q}:\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p,q+1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

(117)

are defined by

\mathrm{d}_{H}^{p,q}:=\mathsf{pr}_{p+1,q}\circ\mathrm{d}|_{\Omega^{p,q}},\hskip 56.9055pt\mathrm{d}_{V}^{p,q}:=\mathsf{pr}_{p,q+1}\circ\mathrm{d}|_{\Omega^{p,q}}\,,

where
\mathsf{pr}_{p,q}:\Omega^{p+q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
denotes the subspace projection.

We stress that the differential property
\mathrm{d}_{H}^{2}=0,\,\mathrm{d}_{V}^{2}=0
, is non-trivial, and follows from the fact that the total differential
decomposes as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}|_{\Omega^{p,q}}=\mathrm{d}_{H}^{p,q}+\mathrm{d}_{V}^{p,q}\,,

(118)

which in turn hinges upon the involutive properties of the vertical and horizontal sub-bundles (
93
).
Indeed, while the decomposition is true by definition on 0-forms (
110
), on 1-forms one has

\mathrm{d}|_{\Omega^{1,0}}=\mathrm{d}_{H}^{1,0}+\mathrm{d}_{V}^{1,0}+\mathsf{pr}_{0,2}\circ\mathrm{d}|_{\Omega^{1,0}}\,.

Thus it must be the case that
\mathrm{d}\omega_{V}(X_{H}^{1},X_{H}^{2})=0
for all
\omega_{V}\in\Omega^{1}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and any horizontal
vector fields
X^{1}_{H},X^{2}_{H}
. Indeed,

\mathrm{d}\omega_{V}(X^{1}_{H},X^{2}_{H})=X^{1}_{H}\big(\omega_{V}(X_{H}^{2})\big)-X^{2}_{H}\big(\omega_{V}(X_{H}^{1})\big)-\omega_{V}([X^{1}_{H},X^{2}_{H}])=0

since the first two terms vanish by verticality of
\omega_{V}
, and the third similarly since
[X^{1}_{H},X^{2}_{H}]
is also horizontal (
93
).
The same argument shows that
\mathrm{d}\omega|_{\Omega^{0,1}}=\mathrm{d}_{H}^{0,1}+\mathrm{d}_{V}^{0,1}
, since the vertical vector fields are also involutive.
Since the bi-graded algebra
\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is generated by
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),\,\Omega^{1}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and

\Omega^{1}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
it follows that the decomposition (
118
) holds.

As is customary, we omit reference to the
(p,q)
degrees on the differentials, and write simply

\mathrm{d}=\mathrm{d}_{H}+\mathrm{d}_{V}\,,

whereby the differential property
0=\mathrm{d}^{2}=\mathrm{d}_{H}^{2}+\mathrm{d}_{V}^{2}+(\mathrm{d}_{H}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V}+\mathrm{d}_{V}\circ\mathrm{d}_{H})
implies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}^{2}=0,\hskip 42.67912pt\mathrm{d}_{V}^{2}=0,\hskip 42.67912pt\mathrm{d}_{H}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V}=-\mathrm{d}_{V}\circ\mathrm{d}_{H}\,,

(119)

since each of the terms maps into distinct subspaces of the bi-graded algebra
\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.
The anti-commutation relations and (
111
) determine the action on the local basis of horizontal and vertical 1-forms

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=0,\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{V}(\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=0,\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I})=-\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu},\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{V}(\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I})=0\,,

and hence on any
(p,q)
-form (
115
), by derivation property of the differentials and their action of
functions (
110
).

Definition 5.4

(
Bundle variational bi-complex).

The
variational bi-complex
of a fiber bundle
F\rightarrow M
is the bi-complex

\displaystyle\big(\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),\,\mathrm{d}_{H},\,\mathrm{d}_{V}\big)\,,

(120)

depicted graphically as

\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\Omega^{0,2}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1,2}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{2,2}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{d,2}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{0,1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1,1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{2,1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{d,1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{2,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\,.}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}

The vertical and horizontal cohomology of the variational bicomplex above is described in detail in
[
Ta79
]
, and in
[
An89
]

for the subcomplex of globally finite order forms, with both cohomologies agreeing on abstract grounds
[
GMS00
]
.
In particular, they prove the following important and useful result.

Proposition 5.5

(
Takens’ acyclicity theorem).

The horizontal rows
\big(\Omega^{\bullet,q\geq 1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\mathrm{d}_{H}\big)
are exact, apart from degrees
(d,q)
. Similarly the vertical columns
\big(\Omega^{p,\bullet}\,,\mathrm{d}_{V}\big)
, apart from degrees
(p,q\leq f)
, where
f
is the dimension of the fiber,
\mathrm{dim}(F)=f+d
.

This means that any potentially non-vanishing cohomology is concentrated in a finite portion of the diagram. For the purposes of classical field theory, most of the useful information is in the bottom row and right-most column of the diagram. Indeed, it is convenient to extend the bi-complex to the right by the column
of
functional forms
, which in turn will allow for combining the bottom row and right-most column in a single cochain complex.

Remark 5.6

(
Globally vs. locally finite order).

The definitions and results that follow apply for both cases of locally finite order
\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and globally finite order

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
on the infinite jet bundle, as in
[
Ta79
]
[
An89
]
[
GMS00
]
.
Therefore, we will suppress mentioning the distinction between the cases.

Definition 5.7

(
Source forms).

For
q\geq 1
, the
source forms
of degree
(d,q)
are defined as the subspace

\displaystyle\Omega^{d,q}_{s}(J^{\infty}F):=\Omega^{d,q-1}(J^{\infty}F)\wedge\Omega^{1}_{V,0}(J^{\infty}F)\,,

(121)

where

\Omega^{1}_{V,0}(J^{\infty}F):=\mathsf{pr}_{0,1}\big((\pi^{\infty}_{0})^{*}\Omega^{1}(F)\big)\subset\Omega^{1}_{V}(J^{\infty}F)\,

is the space of vertical 1-forms that arise by pulling back 1-forms on
F
.

In particular, a source form
P\in\Omega^{d,q}_{s}(J^{\infty}F)
always has a ‘leg along
\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}
’, i.e.,

P=P_{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}
,
for some
\{P_{a}\}\subset\Omega^{d,q-1}(J^{\infty}F)
.

Lemma 5.8

(
Differential operator of source form).

(i)
Any
(d,1)
-source form
P\in\Omega_{s}^{d,1}(J^{\infty}F)
naturally defines a smooth bundle map

P:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
over
F
, and so in turn over
M
,

51
51

51

We note that
V^{*}F\rightarrow F\rightarrow M
is a
vector
bundle over
F
, but in general only a
fiber
over
M
.
Hence, the tensor product of bundles
\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
is a shorthand for the tensor product of vector bundles over
F
, i.e.,

\pi_{F}^{*}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)\otimes_{F}V^{*}F\rightarrow F\rightarrow M
.
and hence a differential operator

\mathcal{P}:\Gamma_{M}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}\big(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,,

where
V^{*}F\rightarrow F\rightarrow M
is the dual vector bundle to the vertical tangent bundle
VF\hookrightarrow F\rightarrow M
.

(ii)
Furthermore, by Lem.
3.15
, it is (uniquely) extended to a map of smooth sets

\mathcal{P}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,.

Proof.

Let
P
be
a
(d,1)
-source form, locally of the form
P=P_{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}
. Since each
P_{a}\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is horizontal top-form, we have

P=P_{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}=P_{a}\wedge(\mathrm{d}u^{a}-u^{a}_{\mu}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=P_{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}u^{a}+0\,.

The local coordinate
1
-forms
\{\mathrm{d}u^{a}=\mathrm{d}_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}u^{a}\}
are the pullback of the differential of fiber coordinates on
F
,

\mathrm{d}u^{a}=\mathrm{d}(\pi^{*}_{0}u^{a})=\pi^{*}_{0}(\mathrm{d}_{F}u^{a})\,,

which locally span the fibers of
V^{*}F
. Thus
P
is equivalently a bundle map over
F
(and so over M),

P:J^{\infty}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\,.

By Lem.
2.32
, the corresponding differential operator is defined by precomposing
with the jet prolongation of any given section

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:\Gamma_{M}(F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow P\circ j^{\infty}\phi\,.

and similarly for its smooth extension on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots. In local coordinates,

\phi^{b}(x^{\mu})\mapstochar\rightarrow P_{a}(x^{\mu},\phi^{b},\partial_{\mu}\phi^{b},\cdots)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{F}u^{a}
.
∎

Note that the result
does not extend
to higher order source forms as stated. There is a special subspace of source forms,

the functional forms

[
An89
]
, defined as the image of a natural projection map, which parametrizes a class of
currents on
\mathcal{F}
via contraction with (vertical) vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
(see Rem.
6.5
below
and
[
An89
]
for more details).

Definition 5.9

(
Interior Euler operator).

(i)
For any
q\geq 1
, the
interior Euler operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}:\Omega^{d,q}(J^{\infty}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d,q}(J^{\infty}F)

(122)

defined by the formula

\omega\;\longmapsto\;\frac{1}{q}\mathrm{d}_{v}u^{a}\wedge\bigg(\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|I|}\mathbb{L}_{D_{I}}\Big(\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}}\omega\Big)\!\!\bigg)

where
\mathbb{L}_{D_{I}}=(\mathbb{L}_{D_{1}})^{I_{1}}\circ(\mathbb{L}_{D_{2}})^{I_{2}}\circ\cdots\circ(\mathbb{L}_{D_{d}})^{I_{d}}
denotes the
composition of the Lie derivatives (
107
) with respect to the lifts of the
coordinate vector fields (
91
), for every multi-index
I=(I_{1},\cdots,I_{d})
.

(ii)
The set of
functional forms
is defined as

\displaystyle\Omega^{d,q}_{\mathrm{f}}(J^{\infty}F):=\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{I})\subset\Omega^{d,q}_{s}(J^{\infty})\,.

The interior Euler operator appears in
[
An89
]
in a more coordinate-dependent fashion,
while the above form – as far as we know – appears in

[
Blo23
]
.

Proposition 5.10

(
Properties of the interior operator).

[
An89
, Thm 2.12]

(i)

\mathcal{I}
is a projection operator

\mathcal{I}\circ\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}\,.

(ii)
The composition
\mathcal{I}\circ\mathrm{d}_{H}:\Omega^{d-1,q}(J^{\infty}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d,q}(J^{\infty}F)

vanishes and furthermore

\mathrm{ker}(\mathcal{I})=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{d}_{H})\,.

(iii)
The map
\mathcal{I}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V}:\Omega^{d,q}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d,q+1}
is nilpotent

(\mathcal{I}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V})\circ(\mathcal{I}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V})=0\,.

(iv)

The
(d,1)
-functional forms coincide with
(d,1)
-source forms

\Omega^{d,1}_{\mathrm{f}}(J^{\infty}F):=\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{I})\cong\Omega^{d,1}_{S}(J^{\infty}F)\,.

The first two items are the non-trivial part of the proposition, the third follows from the first two, while the fourth
follows directly from the defining formula in Def.
5.9
. Note that the first two items imply that

\Omega^{d,q}(J^{\infty}F)\cong\Omega^{d,q}_{f}(J^{\infty}F)\oplus\mathrm{d}_{H}\Omega^{d-1,q}(J^{\infty}F)\,,

and so in particular functional forms parametrize
(d,q)
-forms modulo horizontally exact forms

\displaystyle\Omega^{d,q}_{f}(J^{\infty}F)\cong\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}F)/\mathrm{d}_{H}\Omega^{d-1,q}(J^{\infty}F)\,.

The map defined on functional forms

\displaystyle\delta_{V}:=\mathcal{I}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V}:\Omega^{d,q}_{f}(J^{\infty}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d,q+1}_{f}(J^{\infty}F)

(123)

is called the (higher) Euler operator. Since it squares to zero, it follows that
the variational complex may be
augmented
to the right

\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\Omega^{0,2}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1,2}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\mathcal{I}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{d,1}_{f}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\Omega^{d,1}_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Omega^{0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{H}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{2,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{V}}

where, by the second property of the above proposition, all the rows except the bottom are still horizontally exact.
The bottom row and right-most column connect into a single complex.

Definition 5.11

(
Euler-Lagrange complex).

The
Euler–Lagrange
complex
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{EL}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is defined as

\displaystyle\Omega^{0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{d}_{H}\;}\Omega^{1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{d}_{H}\;}\cdots\xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{d}_{H}\;}\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\;\delta_{V}\;}\Omega^{d,1}_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\;\delta_{V}\;}\Omega^{d,2}_{f}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\;\delta_{V}\;}\cdots\,,

(124)

where the notation implies
\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{EL}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
for
0\leq p\leq d

and
\Omega^{d+q}_{\mathrm{EL}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\Omega^{d,q}_{f}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
for
1\leq q
.

In particular, if
L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is a Lagrangian density given in local coordinates by

L=\bar{L}\big(x^{\mu},\{u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}
, then the ‘integration by parts’
algorithm (with differentials and derivatives taken on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
) gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{V}L

\displaystyle=\sum\frac{\partial\bar{L}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\cdot\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum(-1)^{|I|}D_{I}\bigg(\!\frac{\partial\bar{L}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\!\bigg)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{n}+\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}

(125)

\displaystyle=\delta_{V}L+\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}\,,

for some
\theta_{L}\in\Omega^{d-1,1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, defined up to the addition of a horizontally closed
(d-1,1)
-form
(and hence exact by Prop.
5.5
). This can be checked directly in coordinates using Def.
5.9

and explicit formulas for arbitrary order Lagrangians can be found, for instance, in

[
De18
]
[
Blo23
]
. For the simple (but generic) case where the Lagrangian is globally of order
k=1
, we have

\displaystyle\theta_{L}=-\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}\wedge\mathbb{L}_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{\mu}}}\Big(\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}}L\Big)\,.

(126)

Generally, the interpretation is that
\theta
absorbs the dependence on the vertical forms
\{\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}\}_{|I|\geq 1}
.
Abstractly, this is implied by the exactness of the second row in the augmented bicomplex. Equivalently, this reads

\delta_{V}L=\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{d}_{V}L)=\mathrm{d}_{V}L-\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}

and so the projection operator
\mathcal{I}
acts locally via partial integration. Indeed, this is exactly the purpose of
the defining formula (Def.
5.9
). For reasons that will become apparent below, we denote the
component of
\delta_{V}=\mathcal{I}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V}
acting on Lagrangians by

E:=\delta_{V}\quad:\quad\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d,1}_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

and call source forms in its image
Euler–Lagrange source forms
. The map
E
itself is often called the

Euler–Lagrange differential
on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
. Note that when the Lagrangian density happens to be
exact
L=\mathrm{d}_{H}T
, then the induced Euler–Lagrange source form is trivial

\displaystyle EL=\delta_{V}L=\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{d}_{V}\mathrm{d}_{H}T)=\mathcal{I}(\mathrm{d}_{H}\mathrm{d}_{V}T)=0\,.

(127)

For this reason, horizontally exact Lagrangian densities are known as
trivial
Lagrangians.

5.2
On-shell space of fields and conserved currents

Using the explicit expression of the Euler–Lagrange form above, there is an immediate and natural description of the on-shell spaces of fields.
A useful intermediate step is to define the smooth subspace of the infinite jet bundle where the Euler–Lagrange sources form vanishes.

Definition 5.12

(
Shell of Lagrangian).

The
shell
of a Lagrangian
L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is defined to be the smooth subspace of the jet bundle

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{L}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

on which the Euler–Lagrange source form vanishes. Equivalently, the shell is the pullback/intersection of smooth sets

\textstyle{S_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{EL}
\textstyle{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0^{\infty}_{F}}
\textstyle{\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\,,}

where the Euler–Lagrange source form
EL
is considered as a bundle map, via Lem.
5.8
,
and
0^{\infty}_{F}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
is the (non-constant) fiberwise 0-bundle map over
F
.

52
52

52

This is induced by pulling back the zero section
0_{F}
of the vector-bundle
\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\rightarrow F

via
\pi_{0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
.

Yet equivalently, the shell and the above diagram may be reinterpreted as the intersection of the zero section and the section
induced by the
EL
-source form, of the induced pullback bundle
(\pi_{0})^{*}\big(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\big)
over
J^{\infty}_{M}F
itself.
Explicitly, the shell is the smooth space with points

S_{L}(*)=\{s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F\,|\,EL(s)=0_{\pi_{0}(s)}\}

and more generally
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{L}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})=\big\{s^{k}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F\;\big|\;EL\big(s^{k}(x)\big)=0_{\pi_{0}(s^{k}(x))}\quad\forall x\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}\big\}\,.

According to Lem.
5.8
, for any Lagrangian
L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, the resulting
Euler–Lagrange source form
EL=\delta_{V}L\in\Omega^{d,1}_{s}(J^{\infty}F)
defines a differential operator and, in particular, a smooth map

\displaystyle\mathcal{EL}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)

(128)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k}\,.

By (
4.10
), in local coordinates this takes the usual form of
the Euler–Lagrange operator acting on a field
\phi
,

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|I|}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\bigg(\frac{\partial\bar{L}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\bigg)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{F}u^{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{n}\,,

whose coefficients, by abuse of notation, are usually expressed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)=EL_{a}\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|I|}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\bigg(\frac{\delta\bar{L}\big(x^{\mu},\{\partial_{J}\phi^{b}\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{a})}\bigg)\,.

(129)

Remark 5.13

(
Treating partial derivatives as independent).

For the above textbook form of the Euler–Lagrange equations (Eq. (
129
)) to make sense computationally,
one implicitly treats the functions
\{\partial_{I}\phi^{a}\}_{|I|\leq k}
on spacetime
M
as independent variables – which is factually not the case – and
acts formally via the corresponding partial differentiation. The rigorous justification for this treatment is given by the proper definition of the
differential Euler–Lagrange operator (map (
128
)) and its well-defined local coordinate formula,
whereby the coordinates
\{u^{a}_{I}\}_{|I|\leq k}
are truly independent.

Definition 5.14

(
On-shell smooth space of fields).

(i)

A field configuration
\phi
is said to be
on-shell
for a local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
if it satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equation

\mathcal{EL}(\phi)=EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi=0_{\phi}\in\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,,

where
0_{\phi}:=0_{F}\circ\phi:M\rightarrow F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
is the canonical section covering

\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)
, given by postcomposing with the zero-section of
\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\rightarrow F
.

(ii)
Similarly, an
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plot of fields
\phi^{k}
is on-shell if it satisfies
\mathcal{EL}(\phi^{k})=EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k}=0_{\phi^{k}}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
.

(iii)
The on-shell
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of fields define the

smooth space of on-shell fields
, i.e., the smooth subspace of the smooth field space

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}):=\big\{\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,\big|\,\mathcal{EL}(\phi^{k})=EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k}=0_{\phi^{k}}\big\}\,.

Equivalently, an
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plot of fields
\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
is on-shell if and only if its prolongation

j^{\infty}\phi^{k}:y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times M)\rightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

factors through the shell of L

j^{\infty}\phi^{k}:y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{S}_{L}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

In
section

5.3
, we will show how the on-shell space of fields is
identified with the smooth critical subset of the
corresponding action functional (or smooth Lagrangian), in an appropriate sense of the criticality condition. As we will make explicit therein
(Cor.
5.32
), the above definition is in fact a universal construction within smooth sets – i.e., pullback/intersection
similar to that of the shell (Def.
5.12
) within the infinite jet bundle.

Example 5.15

(
O(
n
)-model on-shell fields).

Recall the O(
n
)-model Lagrangian from Ex.
3.10
.
We will consider the particular case of
c_{4}=0
for the sake of brevity.
The corresponding Euler–Lagrange operator is given by

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=-\mathrm{d}_{M}\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi+\star\phi\quad\in\quad\,\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes W)\,,

where the fibers of
V^{*}(W\times M)\cong W^{*}\times W\times M
are implicitly identified with
W\times W
via the Euclidean product on
W
. Equivalently as usually expressed, by application of a further Hodge dual isomorphism

\star\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=-\Delta\phi+\phi\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(M,W)\,,

where
\Delta=\mathrm{d}_{M}\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\star
is the Laplace–Beltrami operator. Thus, the smooth set of on-shell fields is
comprised of (plots of) fields satisfying

\Delta(\phi)=\phi\,.

The corresponding source form is easily read in coordinates. In terms of the decomposition at the level of the jet bundle we have

\delta L=EL+\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}
where

\displaystyle\theta_{L}=-\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}\wedge\mathbb{L}_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{\mu}}}\Big(\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}}L\Big)=-\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}\wedge g^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu\,a}\cdot\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}=:-\langle\mathrm{d}_{V}u\,,\star\mathrm{d}_{H}u\rangle_{g}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1,1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

(130)

Although the shell
S_{L}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
(Def.
5.12
) serves as a good stepping stone
towards the actual on-shell field space
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
(Def.
5.14
), it is not quite
its correct avatar within the infinite jet bundle. Indeed, a solution
\phi
of a PDE such as the Euler–Lagrange equation

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=0
of a field theory automatically satisfies an infinite list of implied differential equations. Intuitively,
locally in coordinates and trivializations of the corresponding bundles, these are ‘generated’ by applying arbitrary derivatives
on the original Euler–Lagrange differential condition (
129
)

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)=0\quad\implies\quad\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)=0\,.

These implied conditions, however, are not reflected in the shell
S_{L}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
. In particular,
for an Euler–Lagrange source form
EL=\pi_{k}^{*}EL_{k}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{k}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F

of global order
k
, an arbitrary point
s=j^{\infty}_{p}\phi\in S_{L}
is such that
EL(s)=EL_{k}\circ\pi_{k}(s)=EL_{k}\big(j^{k}(\phi)\big)=0
,
i.e., with conditions on the jet components up to order
k
. Crucially, there are no conditions on the higher jets, such as those induced
by the implied differential equations. Said otherwise, in local coordinates
\{x^{\mu},\{u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|}\}
for
J^{\infty}_{M}F
,
the vanishing of the Euler–Lagrange form imposes conditions on the coordinates up to order
|I|=k
, but leaves the rest of the coordinates free.

Taking into account the global structure of the field bundle
F\rightarrow M
and the corresponding Euler–Lagrange differential
operator
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}:\Gamma_{M}(F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
, this translates to fact that

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=0_{\phi}\,\in\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,\quad\implies\quad j^{\infty}\big(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)=0_{j^{\infty}(\phi)}\,\in\Gamma_{M}\big(J^{\infty}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\big)\,.

Expanding the induced condition on the right and recalling Eq. (
56
), the (locally defined) implied
conditions may be equivalently
encoded as

53
53

53

For the reverse implication, notice that for any
linear
differential
operator
\mathcal{K}:\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(G)
, with underlying vector bundle map

K:J^{\infty}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\rightarrow G
valued in an arbitrary vector bundle
G
over
F
(and hence over M),
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=0_{\phi}\,\in\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
implies

\mathcal{K}\circ\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=K\circ j^{\infty}\circ EL\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)=K\circ\mathsf{pr}EL(j^{\infty}\phi)=0\quad\in\quad\Gamma_{M}(G)\,.

\displaystyle j^{\infty}\circ\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=\mathsf{pr}EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi=0_{j^{\infty}(\phi)}\quad\in\quad\Gamma_{M}\big(J^{\infty}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\big)\,.

In other words, the prolongation
j^{\infty}\phi
of a solution
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
does not only factor through the shell
S_{L}
, i.e.,
zero locus of
EL:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
, but furthermore through the ‘prolongated shell’ – the zero
locus of the prolongated Euler–Lagrange bundle map
\mathsf{pr}EL:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
.

Definition 5.16

(Prolongated Shell of Lagrangian)
.

The
prolongated shell
of a Lagrangian
L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is defined to be the smooth subspace of the jet bundle

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}^{\infty}_{L}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

on which the prolongated Euler–Lagrange source form
\mathsf{pr}EL
vanishes. That is, the prolongated shell is the pullback/intersection of smooth sets

\textstyle{S^{\infty}_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{pr}EL}
\textstyle{y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{pr}0^{\infty}_{F}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,,}

where the
\mathsf{pr}EL
is the prolongation (Def.
3.16
) of the Euler–Lagrange source form
EL
viewed as a
bundle map, via Lem.
5.8
, and
\mathsf{pr}0^{\infty}_{F}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)

is the (non-constant) fiberwise 0-bundle map over
F
.

Yet equivalently, the prolongated shell and the above diagram may be reinterpreted as the intersection of the zero section and the section induced by
the prolongated
EL
-source form, of the induced pullback bundle
(\pi_{0})^{*}\big(J^{\infty}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\big)
over
J^{\infty}_{M}F
itself.
Explicitly, the prolongated shell is the smooth space with points

S_{L}(*)=\big\{s\in J^{\infty}_{M}F\;|\;\mathsf{pr}EL(s)=0_{\pi_{0}(s)}\big\}
and
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots along the same lines.

Remark 5.17

(
Manifold structure and nomenclature of the prolongated shell).

(i)

Even though the ambient infinite jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is a Fréchet manifold, it is not guaranteed that (the set of points of) the prolongated shell
S_{L}^{\infty}

can be supplied with a Fréchet submanifold structure. This is apparent even in the globally finite order Lagrangian case, where the corresponding
Euler–Lagrange form is (the pullback of) a globally finite order map, say
EL^{k}:J^{k}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
. In this case, even the
question of the (finite order) shell
S_{L,k}\hookrightarrow J^{k}_{M}F
having a smooth (finite-dimensional) manifold structure demands the transversality
of the maps
EL^{k}
and
0_{F}
. This is not always guaranteed and depends on the explicit form of the Lagrangian and the induced Euler–Lagrange form.

(ii)

Even if this is the case,
it might still be the case that they become non-transversal upon any finite order prolongation
54
54

54

These are defined analogously, so that

\mathsf{pr}^{q}EL^{k}(j^{k+q}_{p}\phi):=j^{q}_{p}\big(P\circ j^{k}\phi\big)
for some (local) representative section
\phi
. Equivalently, they may be
defined directly via the
q
-order prolongation of
EL:=(\pi_{k})^{*}EL^{k}
on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
as

\mathsf{pr}^{q}EL(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi):=j^{q}_{p}\big(EL\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)\big)=j^{q}_{p}(EL^{k}\circ j^{k}\phi)=\mathsf{pr}^{q}EL^{k}\circ\pi_{q+k}(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)
.

\mathsf{pr}^{q}EL^{k}:J^{k+q}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{q}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
, so that their zero locus intersection
S^{q}_{L,k}\hookrightarrow J^{k+q}_{M}F
is not a smooth submanifold. Nevertheless, there is a smooth set
structure on each finite order prolonged shell, and the prolonged shell
S^{\infty}_{L}
as their (projective) limit, since all limits exist in the category of smooth sets
(see also Ex.
5.34
). More intricate details about the potential analytic nature required by constructions in
local field theory will be noted in Rem.
7.15
.

(iii)

We note that the description of the ‘avatar’ of an arbitrary partial differential equation, as the zero locus of the corresponding prolongated
bundle map inside the infinite jet bundle, was first introduced by Vinogradov in
[
Vin81
]
[
Vin84b
]
and goes under the name
diffiety
.
Its geometrical properties and relation to the actual solutions of PDEs have been much studied since (see
[
Vin13
]
for a modern review).
Our nomenclature as the (prolonged) shell in the specific case of Euler–Lagrange operators is intended to make contact with the
“on-shell fields” nomenclature from the physics literature, thus being those fields that factor through the (prolonged) shell.

Given the exactness properties of the variational bi-complex, along with the identification of the total cohomologies of Prop.
4.14

and Prop.
5.5
,
together with enough diagram chasing, one can arrive at the following result on the cohomology of the Euler–Lagrange complex

[
Ta79
]
[
An89
]
[
GMS00
]
.

Proposition 5.18

(
Euler–Lagrange cohomology).

The projection map
\pi^{\infty}_{0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
along with the projections
\mathsf{pr}_{p,0}
and
\mathcal{I}\circ\mathsf{pr}_{d,q}
, onto horizontal
and
(d,q)
-source forms respectively, induce a quasi-isomorphism

\Omega^{\bullet}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{EL}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

and so isomorphisms on the corresponding cohomologies.

In particular, this implies that
H^{n}_{\mathrm{EL}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)=0
for
n>\mathrm{dim}(F)
. Furthermore, any
\mathrm{d}_{H}
-closed horizontal form form
