\omega_{p,0}\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
for
0\leq p<d
decomposes as

\omega_{p,0}=\mathsf{pr}_{p,0}\circ(\pi^{\infty}_{0})^{*}(\tilde{\omega}_{p})+\mathrm{d}_{H}k_{p-1,0}\,,

for some closed
\tilde{\omega}_{p}\in\Omega^{p}(F)
and
k_{p-1,0}\in\Omega^{p-1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, while the same holds for any density

L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
which is
\delta_{V}
-closed
instead. Since
\delta_{V}L=EL=0
means the Lagrangian has trivial
Euler–Lagrange derivatives as per Eq. (
127
), the cohomology
H^{d}_{\mathrm{dR}}(F)
parametrizes variationally trivial Lagrangian densities.

Remark 5.19

(
The (global) inverse problem of the calculus of variations).

Given a (smooth) differential
operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;P\circ j^{\infty}\phi\,,

for some
(d,1)
-source form
P\in\Omega^{d,1}_{s}(J^{\infty}F)
, does there exist a Lagrangian
(d,0)
-form such that
P=\delta_{V}L
?
In words, is the “
source equation
”

\mathcal{P}=0

“variational”
? A necessary condition is that
\delta_{V}P=0
. This condition guarantees the
existence of a Lagrangian
locally
on

J^{\infty}_{M}F

[
To69
]
. Globally, the obstruction to be variational lies in the
(d+1)
-cohomology of the Euler–Lagrange
complex (Def.
5.11
), and hence by Prop.
5.18
, in the
(d+1)
de Rham cohomology
H^{d+1}_{\mathrm{dR}}
(F) of the underlying field bundle. This problem and its reduction to the cohomological question is historically one of the main reasons for the introduction of the Euler–Lagrange complex.

Forms on the infinite jet bundle may be pulled back to forms on spacetime, along any prolongation of a field configuration
j^{\infty}\phi:M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
, in a manner that is compatible with the horizontal differential
\mathrm{d}_{H}
on

J^{\infty}_{M}F
and the de Rham differential
\mathrm{d}_{M}
of the base
M
.

Definition 5.20

(
Pullback via prolongated section).

Let
\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)
be a smooth section and
j^{\infty}\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(J^{\infty}F)
its prolongation.
Define the pullback morphism of
differential forms

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}:\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(M)

as follows: For any
m
-form

\omega\in\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, let
(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\omega\in\Omega^{m}(M)
denote the
m
-form on the base
M
given by

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\omega(X_{p}):=\omega_{j^{\infty}\phi(p)}\big(\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)_{p}X_{p}\big)\,,

for all
X_{p}\in T_{p}M
,
p\in M
, where
d(j^{\infty}\phi)_{p}
denotes the pushforward map from (
86
).

Lemma 5.21

(
Horizontal differential and base de Rham compatibility).

The pullback
(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}:\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
respects the horizontal differential

\displaystyle(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}\omega=\mathrm{d}_{M}(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\omega\,,

(131)

and vanishes on forms of non-zero vertical degree

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\omega_{p,q\geq 1}=0\,.

Proof.

It suffices to check the relations on
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and the local basis of
\Omega^{1,1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,
since these generate
\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. In local coordinates, recall by
(
85
),

\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)_{p}\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{p}\Big)=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\,.

On the local coordinate functions
\{x^{\mu}\}
for
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, we have

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}x^{\mu}=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}=\mathrm{d}_{M}(j^{\infty}\phi^{*}x^{\mu})=\mathrm{d}_{M}x^{\mu}\,

where the first equality is due to (
111
),
the second by acting on
\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}|_{p}
, and the last equality due to the section condition
of
j^{\infty}\phi
over M. Similarly, for any smooth function
f\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, we have

\displaystyle(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}f

\displaystyle=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\big(D_{\mu}(f)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=\big(D_{\mu}(f)\circ j^{\infty}\phi\big)\cdot(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}(f\circ j^{\infty}\phi)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{M}x^{\mu}

with the last equality being the chain rule as in (
4.10
). On the local basis of horizontal 1-forms

\{\mathrm{d}_{H}x^{\mu}=\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\}
it is immediate that

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathrm{d}_{H}x^{\mu})=0=\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathrm{d}_{M}x^{\mu})=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big((j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}x^{\mu}\big)\,,

while on the vertical
1
-forms
\{\theta^{a}_{I}=\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}\}

\displaystyle(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I})

\displaystyle=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}(-\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=0\,,

since
\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}
vanishes when applied to two horizontal vectors, such as the horizontal lifts

\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)_{p}(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}|_{p})
. Similarly,
\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}
vanishes on horizontal vectors by
definition, and so

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}=0
which also implies

\mathrm{d}_{M}\big((j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}\big)=0=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I})\,.

The above proves that
(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}=\mathrm{d}_{M}(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}
, while the fact that

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}=0

furthermore implies the vanishing result
(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\omega_{p,q\geq 1}=0
by expression (
115
).
∎

By a straightforward
calculation along the lines of the proof above, and recalling the identification of (
116
),
the currents of Def.
3.17
induced by horizontal forms
P\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
are then equivalently given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:\Gamma_{M}(F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}(M)

(132)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\longmapsto P\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}P\,,

where the pullback denotes that of forms in the sense of Def.
5.20
.

Corollary 5.22

(
Stokes’ Theorem on field space).

Consider a horizontal
(p,0)
-form
P\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and its horizontal differential
(p+1,0)
-form

\mathrm{d}_{H}P\in\Omega^{p+1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

(i)
For any compact oriented submanifold
B^{p+1}\hookrightarrow M
with boundary
\partial B^{p+1}=\Sigma^{p}
, we have

\int_{B^{p+1}}\mathrm{d}_{H}P\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}\phi\,,

for any field
\phi\in\mathcal{F}=\Gamma_{M}(F)
.

(ii)
Similarly, the same formulas hold for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of fields, valued in
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k},\mathbbm{R})
.
That is, the induced charges defined by
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}:=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}_{M}
and

\int_{B^{p+1}}\mathrm{d}_{H}P\circ j^{\infty}_{M}
agree as smooth real-valued functions

\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

on field space.

Proof.

By Stokes’ Theorem,

\displaystyle\int_{B^{p+1}}\mathrm{d}_{H}P\circ j^{\infty}\phi

\displaystyle=\int_{B^{p+1}}(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\mathrm{d}_{H}P=\int_{B^{p+1}}\mathrm{d}_{M}(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}P

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}P=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}\phi\,,

and similarly for plots of fields.
∎

The above corollary naturally leads to the notion of conserved local currents. In particular, if
P
is horizontally closed

\mathrm{d}_{H}P=0
, then

\displaystyle 0=\mathrm{d}_{M}\circ\mathcal{P}:\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p+1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

(133)

and the so current is ‘conserved’. In particular
\mathcal{P}_{\partial B^{p+1}}(\phi)=0
, i.e., the total flux/current passing through

any bounding

submanifold
\partial B^{p+1}\hookrightarrow M
is zero, for any field configuration
\phi
(and similarly plots of fields).

Definition 5.23

(
Conserved currents and charges).

A current
\mathcal{P}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
induced by a horizontally closed
(p,0)
-form
P\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

is called a
conserved current
. The induced charges are called
conserved charges
.

The intuition for a conserved charge is the usual one. If
\Sigma^{p}=\Sigma^{p}_{-}\mathop{\text{\smash{\raisebox{-9.03763pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\prod$}}}\vphantom{$\prod$}}}\Sigma^{p}_{+}

is the disjoint union of two cobordant submanifolds,
\Sigma^{p}=\partial B^{p+1}
,
then
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}=0=\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}_{+}}-\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}_{-}}
and so

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}_{+}}=\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}_{-}}:\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,,

(134)

i.e., the charge is conserved along the cobordism
B^{p+1}
.

Remark 5.24

(
Spacetime cohomology and triviality of currents).

Let
\mathcal{P}=P\circ j^{\infty}
be a conserved local current on
\mathcal{F}
, i.e.,
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}=(\mathrm{d}_{H}P)\circ j^{\infty}=0
as a smooth map on
\mathcal{F}
.
If the de Rham cohomology
H^{p}_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
vanishes, then for any fixed field configuration
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
there exists
some
T_{\phi}\in\Omega^{p-1}(M)
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(\phi)=\mathrm{d}_{M}T_{\phi}\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636pt\Omega^{p}(M)\,.

However, the dependence of
T_{\phi}
on
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
is by no means guaranteed to be
local
or
smooth
.

If
\mathcal{P}(\phi)=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\phi}
happens to be exact,
then its charges are identically zero along
any
closed submanifold, and one says
\mathcal{P}
is a trivial current at
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
.
Of course, it may still have non-trivial charges over submanifolds with boundary, but the nomenclature is standard.

Definition 5.25

(
Trivial local currents and charges).

A local current
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
induced by a horizontally exact
(p,0)
-form

\mathrm{d}_{H}T\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is called a
trivial current
.

It follows that the cohomology
H^{\bullet<d}_{\mathrm{EL}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
of Euler–Lagrange complex (Def.
5.11
)
up to degree
d-1
, and so equivalently by Prop.
5.18
the de Rham cohomology

H^{\bullet<d}_{\mathrm{dR}}(F)
, parametrizes the classes of non-trivial (off-shell) conserved local currents on the
field space
\mathcal{F}
. In particular, if the degree
p
-cohomology of
F\rightarrow M
vanishes, then every conserved

p
-form local current on
\mathcal{F}
is trivial (in a local manner, in contrast with Rem.
5.24
).
For instance, if the field bundle is a vector bundle over a contractible spacetime, e.g.

W\times\mathbbm{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{d}
as in the case of vector-valued field theory (Ex.
3.10
)
on Minkowski spacetime, then all of its cohomology vanishes and hence all
conserved (off-shell) charges are necessarily trivial. In particular, the currents from Ex.
3.18
(i), (ii)
are easily seen to be conserved off-shell, by a direct application of the de Rham differential.

In classical field theory, the currents of most interest are only preserved on the smooth subspace
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
of on-shell fields.

Definition 5.26

(
On-shell conserved currents and charges).

A
(p,0)
-form
P
is ‘
horizontally closed on the (prolongated) shell of

L
’ if
\mathrm{d}_{H}P\circ\iota_{\mathrm{S}^{\infty}_{L}}=0

as a bundle map

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}^{\infty}_{L}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\wedge^{p}T^{*}M)\,

over
M
. The induced current
\mathcal{P}
and charges are called
on-shell conserved current and charges
.

It follows by Stokes’ Theorem on field space, Cor.
5.22
, that

\displaystyle 0=\mathrm{d}_{M}\circ\mathcal{P}:\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p+1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,,

and so is indeed ‘conserved on-shell’. Similarly

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}_{+}}=\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}_{-}}:\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R}),

for the corresponding charges for any
\Sigma^{p}=\Sigma^{p}_{-}\mathop{\text{\smash{\raisebox{-9.03763pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\prod$}}}\vphantom{$\prod$}}}\Sigma^{p}_{+}
bounding a submanifold

B^{p+1}
. Of course, such conserved currents arise when (infinitesimal) local symmetries exist, via Noether’s First Theorem (Prop.
6.14
).
In particular, the O(
n
)-model local
(d-1)
-currents from Ex.
3.18
(iii) are not off-shell conserved,
but can be checked to be on-shell conserved when the vector fields employed are infinitesimal symmetries.

Remark 5.27

(
EL-cohomology and on-shell currents).

Crucially, the cohomology
H^{\bullet<d}_{\mathrm{dR}}(F)
of the field bundle
does not
parametrize on-shell
conserved currents. Indeed, even if the cohomology vanishes, and hence all off-shell currents are trivial, there
can still be non-trivial on-shell conserved currents. We will come back to the distinction of off- and on-shell
conserved currents in the form of Noether’s first and second theorems in the following section.

We close off this section by proving that a local symmetry of a Lagrangian field theory
(Def.
3.23
) preserves the smooth subspace of on-shell fields,
justifying its definition.

Proposition 5.28

(
Local symmetries preserve the on-shell space of fields).

Any local symmetry
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
of a Lagrangian field theory such that
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}
,
where
\mathcal{K}=K\circ j^{\infty}
for some
K\in\Omega^{d-1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, preserves the smooth subspace

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
of on-shell fields. Namely, the local diffeomorphism
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

restricts to a diffeomorphism

\mathcal{D}|_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}}:\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\,.

Proof.

By Lem.
5.21
, the trivial smooth Lagrangian
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}
is equivalently
(\mathrm{d}_{H}K)\circ j^{\infty}
.
So, by Eq. (
127
),
its corresponding smooth Euler–Lagrange operator vanishes. Hence,

\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D})=\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K})=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}+\mathcal{E}(\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K})=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\,,

i.e., the corresponding smooth Euler–Lagrange operators of
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{L}
coincide. It follows they define the same on-shell space of fields.

Next, we note that the Euler–Lagrange operator of the local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}
is further equivalently given
by
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D})=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}
. To see this, first note that by relation (
56
)

\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}=L\circ j^{\infty}\circ D\circ j^{\infty}=L\circ\mathsf{pr}D\circ j^{\infty}=(\mathsf{pr}D^{*}L)\circ j^{\infty}\,,

where
\mathsf{pr}D:J^{\infty}_{M}F\xrightarrow{\sim}J^{\infty}_{M}F
is the prolongated bundle map (Def.
3.16
),
and the final equation is the pullback in terms of horizontal forms
\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. It is tedious but straightforward
to check that the pullback via a prolongated bundle map as above commutes with the Euler operator on
\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

and so
E(\mathsf{pr}D^{*}L)=\mathsf{pr}D^{*}EL
(e.g. in local coordinates, see Prop. 1.6 and Cor 3.22 of
[
An89
]

55
55

55

The statement
of Prop 1.6 therein is for a prolongation of a bundle map
F\rightarrow F
, but the proof can be directly extended to include prolongations
of bundle maps
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
.
). Precomposing with the jet prolongation
j^{\infty}\phi
of any field configuration gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D})(\phi):

\displaystyle=E(\mathsf{pr}D^{*}L)\circ j^{\infty}\phi=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\,\big(\mathsf{pr}D^{*}EL\big)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{pr}D\circ j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}EL=EL\circ(\mathsf{pr}D\circ j^{\infty}\phi)

\displaystyle=EL\circ\big(j^{\infty}\circ\mathcal{D}(\phi)\big)=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}(\phi)\,.

It follows that if
\phi

is an on-shell field for
\mathcal{L}
, then
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=0
implies
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(\mathcal{D}(\phi)\big)=0
and similarly for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of on-shell
fields. In other words ‘the image’
of
\mathcal{D}
sits inside
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
, and since
\mathcal{D}
is by assumption invertible, the result follows.
∎

Note that the result of this proposition can be interpreted colloquially as the statement that a local symmetry of an action/Lagrangian is also
a symmetry of the corresponding Euler–Lagrange equations. The proof here relies crucially on the fact that
E(\mathsf{pr}D^{*}L)=\mathsf{pr}D^{*}EL
,
for any prolongation of a bundle map
D:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
over
M
. This is also true (using identical arguments)
for prolongations of bundle maps
D_{f}:J^{\infty}F\rightarrow F
covering diffeomorphisms
f:M\rightarrow M
. Overall, this shows
that the Euler–Lagrange differential operators
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
are
‘covariant’ under the action of the induced diffeomorphisms on field space
\mathcal{D}_{f}:=D_{f}\circ j^{\infty}(-)\circ f^{-1}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{f}):=E(\mathsf{pr}D_{f}^{*}L)\circ j^{\infty}\equiv\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{f}\,.

(135)

In Prop.
5.42
, we will use the critical locus characterization of the on-shell space of fields to
show that any (spacetime covariant or local) symmetry descends to a diffeomorphism on the on-shell space of fields. We note
also that the proof of Prop.
5.42
does
not
explicitly rely on the covariance of the Euler–Lagrange operators.

Example 5.29

(
O(
n
)-model symmetries vs. on-shell fields).

Consider the O(
n
)-model on-shell fields from Ex.
5.15
, i.e.,
\phi\in C^{\infty}(M,W)
such that

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=-\Delta\phi+\phi=-\star\mathrm{d}\star\mathrm{d}\phi+\phi=0\,.

For the local finite symmetries of
\mathcal{D}:[M,W]\rightarrow[M,W]
of the Lagrangian from Ex.
3.24
,
given by
\mathcal{D}(\phi)=g\cdot\phi=g^{a}_{\,b}\cdot\phi^{b}\cdot e_{b}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(\mathcal{D}(\phi)\big)

\displaystyle=-\Delta(g\cdot\phi)+g\cdot\phi

\displaystyle=-g\cdot\big(\Delta(\phi)+\phi)

\displaystyle=0\,,

since
g
is constant when viewed as a function on M
g:M\rightarrow O(n)
, and hence commutes with the de Rham differential and Hodge dual.
For the spacetime covariant symmetry
\mathcal{D}:[M,W]\rightarrow[M,W]
, given by pulling back along an isometry
\mathcal{D}(\phi)=f^{*}\phi
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(\mathcal{D}(\phi)\big)

\displaystyle=-\Delta(f^{*}\phi)+f^{*}\phi=-f^{*}\big(\star\mathrm{d}\star\mathrm{d}\phi+\phi\big)

\displaystyle=-f^{*}\big(\Delta(\phi)+\phi)

\displaystyle=0\,,

since pullbacks commute with the de Rham differential, and further since the pullback by an isometry commutes with the Hodge dual.

5.3
On-shell fields as a smooth critical set

In this section, we show how the usual variation algorithm of local action functionals, or more generally local Lagrangians, appearing
in the physics literature may be rigorously interpreted in smooth sets. This completely avoids having to deal with any functional
analytic and infinite-dimensional manifold technicalities in the actual field space. The resulting space of classical solutions, i.e.,
the space of “extrema of the action functional”, naturally forms a smooth set and coincides with the space of on-shell fields of
Def.
5.14
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a smooth set and
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
a smooth map, e.g. a field space and an action functional.
Evaluating on any
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot
\phi_{t}
, the quantity
S(\phi_{t})
is an element of
y(\mathbbm{R})(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})=C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t},\mathbbm{R})

and so we may compute its derivative with respect to
t\in\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

(136)

\displaystyle\phi_{t}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\partial_{t}S(\phi_{t})\,.

Denote by
\mathcal{F}_{\phi}(\mathbbm{R}^{0}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})
the subset of paths

\phi_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\cong\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{0}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})
such that
\phi_{t=0}:=\iota^{*}_{0}\phi_{t}=\phi
, where

\iota_{0}:\{0\}\cong\mathbbm{R}^{0}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{1}
. We say
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{0})
is a
critical point

of
S
if the map (of sets)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi}(\mathbbm{R}^{0}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\partial_{t}S\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\xlongrightarrow{\;\;\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}\;\;}\mathbbm{R}

\displaystyle\phi_{t}

\displaystyle\quad\longmapsto\quad{\partial_{t}}S(\phi_{t})\quad\longmapsto\;\;{\partial_{t}}S(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}\,\,

vanishes.
56
56

56

It is easy to check that the analogous point-criticality condition for variations with higher dimensional paths,
e.g.
\phi_{(t_{1},t_{2})}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{2})
with
\phi_{(t_{1},t_{2})=0}=\phi^{0}
, is redundant since it is implied by the 1-parameter
variation condition.

The interpretation of the formulas above is the intuitive one, we have a notion of paths through a point in our space of fields
and we differentiate at the given point.

Smooth sets allow us to consider more than the bare set

\mathrm{Crit}(S)(*)\subset\mathcal{F}(*)
of critical points. Denoting similarly by

\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{1}}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{2})
the ‘surfaces’
\phi^{1}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{2})
such that

\phi^{1}_{t=0}:=\iota_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\{0\}}^{*}\phi^{1}_{t}=\phi^{1}
, where

\iota_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\{0\}}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\cong\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{2}
.
Indeed, we say
\phi^{1}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})
is a
critical line
or critical
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot of S if the map (of sets)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{1}}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\partial_{t}S\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\xlongrightarrow{\;\;\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}\;\;}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\phi_{t}^{1}

\displaystyle\quad\longmapsto\qquad{\partial_{t}}S(\phi_{t}^{1})\qquad\;\;\longmapsto\;\;{\partial_{t}}S(\phi_{t}^{1})|_{t=0}\,

vanishes. Note that the first map takes only derivative with respect to
t\in\mathbbm{R}^{1}
. Naturally, we may consider more than the
set of critical lines of
S
by
\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{1})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})
. Inducting the above formula to higher dimensional plots
and their 1-parameter variations, we have the following definition.

Definition 5.30

(
Critical plots of smooth map).

Let
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
be a smooth map. The
critical
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots
of
S
is the subset of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}):=\Big\{\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\;\big|\;\partial_{t}S(\phi^{k}_{t})|_{t=0}=0,\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\,\phi^{k}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{k}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\Big\},

(137)

where
\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{k}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})=\big\{\phi^{k}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\,|\,\phi^{k}_{t=0}=\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\big\}
.

Hence, there is an assignment of sets of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-critical plots

\mathbbm{R}^{k}\longmapsto\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,,

for each
k\in\mathbbm{N}
. A priori, if
\mathcal{F}
and
S
are an arbitrary smooth set and smooth map respectively, this assignment
might not be functorial under maps
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}
. Even if it does, it might still not satisfy the sheaf condition.
That is, it
may not
define a smooth set. Categorically, this is due to the
fact that the above definition is not - in general - identified with a universal construction in SmoothSet, e.g.
a limit of some sort.
57
57

57

The culprit is
not
simply the fact that, generally, the quantity

\partial_{t}S(\phi^{1}_{t})|_{t=0}
does not depend only on ‘
\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}
’ as in Rem.
2.18
.
Indeed, this condition alone would not be sufficient for functoriality.

Nevertheless, the structure of local action functionals on field spaces guarantees the functoriality of the critical set.

Proposition 5.31

(
Functoriality of the critical set).

Let
M
be a compact manifold without boundary,
F\rightarrow M
a fiber bundle,
\mathcal{F}
its smooth set of sections and

S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
a (smooth) action functional given by

\displaystyle S=\int_{M}\circ\mathcal{L}

for some local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
.
Then the assignment

\displaystyle\mathbbm{R}^{k}\longmapsto\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})

defines a smooth set, which coincides with the on-shell space of fields
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

The smooth map
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
is
defined as

S=\int_{M}\circ\mathcal{L}\;:\;\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

by composing the smooth Lagrangian density map
\mathcal{L}
of Prop.
3.11
corresponding to

L:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
, with integration along
M
. Intuitively, derivatives only act along
M
,
and for each
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plot of fields the
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-dependence is carried along by multiplication of functions on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
.
The standard integration by parts calculation may now be expressed rigorously through the infinity jet prolongation as follows.
Firstly, by viewing the Lagrangian density bundle map locally as
L=\bar{L}(x^{\mu},u^{a}_{I})\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}
, and working
along the lines of Ex.
6.10
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(L\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=(j^{\infty}\phi_{0})^{*}\big(\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{V}L)=(j^{\infty}\phi_{0})^{*}\Big(\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\delta_{V}L+\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}\Big)

with the latter equality being Eq. (
5.1
). Next, following the description
of Lem.
5.8
, the first term coincides with the natural pairing of the corresponding Euler–Lagrange
differential operator
\mathrm{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(\phi_{0})\in\Gamma_{M}(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)
evaluated at
\phi_{0}
, with the tangent vector

\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\in\Gamma_{M}(VF)=T\mathcal{F}(*)
at
\phi_{0}\in\mathcal{F}(*)
as in Def.
2.19
.
That is,

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\big(\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\delta_{V}L\big)=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\rangle\in\Omega^{d}(M)\,.

Further, the commutation relation
[\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}},\mathrm{d}_{H}]=0
may be easily checked, for instance
in local coordinates. Along with the compatibility result of Lem.
5.21
,
the second term then becomes an exact form on
M

(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\big(\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L})\big)=-(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\big(\mathrm{d}_{H}\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\theta_{L}\big)=-\mathrm{d}_{M}\big((j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\theta_{L}\big)\in\Omega^{d}(M)\,.

Let us now make the useful, for the moment only notational, shorthand

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big):=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big((j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\theta_{L}\big)\,,

(138)

whose underlying mathematical meaning will be made explicit in
section

7
.

With this at hand, we arrive at the more familiar form

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\rangle-\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big)\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d}(M)\,,

(139)

which in coordinates reads as the usual integration by parts algorithm. Note that here it is globally justified for arbitrary
non-trivial fiber bundles and local Lagrangians. Composing with the integral,
the variation of the action functional is thus given by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}S(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle\;\;-\;\;\int_{M}\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle\,,

where the second term vanishes due to the compactness and boundary-less assumption on
M
.

All the steps above follow through similarly on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of sections, and so

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big(L\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k}_{t}\big)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle-\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big)\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\big)\,

(140)

taking value in

\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\big)\cong\Omega^{d}(M)\hat{\otimes}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
, with

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}\big(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\big)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\cong\Gamma_{M}\big(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\big)\hat{\otimes}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})

as in (
128
) and similarly
\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\in T\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(VF)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\cong\Gamma_{M}(VF)\hat{\otimes}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})

as in Def.
2.19
. The pairing
\langle-,-\rangle
is extended linearly by multiplying
the
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
components.
58
58

58

In other words, it defines a smooth map
\langle-,-\rangle:{\mathbold{\Gamma}}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\times{\mathbold{\Gamma}}_{M}(VF)\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
.

Composing with integration, the second term once again vanishes, thus

\partial_{t}S(\phi^{k}_{t})|_{t=0}=\int_{M}\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\rangle\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,,

taking values in
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
. By Lem.
2.17

and its application to higher plots (see relation (
31
) and its footnote), the equation
holds for all tangent vectors at
\phi^{k}_{0}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
. By the fundamental lemma
of the calculus of variations
(see, e.g.,
[
JLJ99
]
), the pairing

\int_{M}\langle-,-\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t
is non-degenerate and so

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{k}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\iff\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})=0_{\phi^{k}_{0}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\hat{\otimes}\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,.

(141)

By verticality of
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
, the latter condition is functorial (invariant) under pullbacks along maps

f:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
of plots, and so
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(\phi^{k}_{0}\big)=0_{\phi^{k}_{0}}\implies\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(f^{*}\phi^{k}_{0}\big)=0_{f^{*}\phi^{k}_{0}}
.
Hence if

\phi^{k}_{0}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
, then
f^{*}\phi^{k}_{0}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})

for all
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
and the result follows.
∎

The criticality condition being equivalent to the Euler–Lagrange equations (
141
) implies a universal construction of the smooth
critical set of a local Lagrangian field theory. To that end, recall that
\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
really stands
for the tensor product
\pi_{F}^{*}\big(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\big)\otimes_{F}V^{*}F
of the pullback top exterior bundle over
F
, and hence
has a zero-section
0_{F}:F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
. Postcomposition of (plots of) sections of
F
, i.e., (plots of)
fields in
\mathcal{F}
, with the zero section
0_{F}
yields the (non-constant) smooth map

\displaystyle 0_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)

(142)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0_{F}\circ\phi^{k}\,.

Corollary 5.32

(
Critical smooth set as a pullback).

(i)
The bare set of critical points
\mathrm{Crit}(S)(*)
is given equivalently by the pullback/intersection set

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}(S)(*)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{M}(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{M}(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{\Gamma_{M}(F)}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,.}

(ii)
The smooth critical set
\mathrm{Crit}(S)
coincides with the incarnation of this
diagram in the topos of smooth sets, i.e.,

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,.}

Proof.

The first statement can easily be checked via (
141
), while the second
follows using the (unique) smooth extension of the Euler–Lagrange differential operator from (
128
).
∎

Remark 5.33

(
Subspace diffeology).

We should note that for the concrete, i.e., diffeological, field spaces appearing in
bosonic
field theory,
the smooth structure on
\mathrm{Crit}(S)
we have defined coincides with the so-called
subspace diffeology
. This point of view, however,
does not generalize to non-concrete field spaces - such as those appearing in
fermionic
field theory. On the contrary,
the smooth set viewpoint we have laid out will directly generalize when considering infinitesimally thickened smooth bosonic field spaces
and even smooth supersets of fermions and bosons, while at the same time paralleling the discussions of fermionic on-shell spaces in the
physics literature
[
GS24
]
.

Example 5.34

(
Field theory in zero dimension).

Consider the case of zero-dimensional field theory, i.e.,
M=*
and
F=N\times*
. Then
\mathcal{F}\cong y(N)
, and the above calculation
reduces to the condition

\partial_{t}S(\phi^{k}_{t})\big|_{t=0}=\;\mathrm{d}^{N}_{\phi^{k}_{0}}\,S\big(\dot{\phi}^{k}(0)\big)=0\;,

where the right-hand side is interpreted, for each
x\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, as the pushforward of a tangent vector

\dot{\phi}^{k}(0,x)\in T_{\phi^{k}_{0}(x)}N
under the differential of
S
at the point
\phi^{k}_{0}(x)\in N
.
Equivalently, this is the condition that the 1-form
\mathrm{d}_{N}S\in\Omega^{1}(N)\cong\Gamma_{N}(T^{*}N)
,
viewed as a section of the cotangent bundle, vanishes at the point
\phi^{k}_{0}(x)\in N
, for every
x\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}
.
Since this condition holds for each point in the image of the
\phi^{k}_{0}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\rightarrow N
, for all tangent vectors
at each point, it follows it is invariant under pullbacks along
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
. Yet equivalently and
more concisely, the critical smooth set of
S:y(M)\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
is the intersection/pullback diagram in
\mathrm{SmthSet}
,

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}_{N}S}
\textstyle{y(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{y(T^{*}N)\,.}

Note that the above critical locus might not have a finite-dimensional smooth manifold structure
59
59

59

That is, in the case where
the intersection is non-transversal.
– i.e., the pullback diagram might not exist in
\mathrm{SmthMfd}
– but it does have a natural
smooth set structure since
any
such intersection/pullback diagram exists in
\mathrm{SmthSet}
.
60
60

60

Depending on one’s goals,
this smooth structure might not be sufficient, and one might have to choose a different category to compute the intersection.
For instance, appropriate choices include thickened smooth sets
[
GS24
]
, which do detect intersection multiplicities in
contrast to smooth sets, or more generally their homotopical versions as in derived geometry (see
[
Ca23
]
).

Comparing with the finite-dimensional example above, we see that the smooth set

\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
serves as a substitute of the cotangent bundle
for the field space
\mathcal{F}
and
local
action functionals.

Definition 5.35

(
Variational cotangent bundle).

The
local or variational cotangent bundle

\pi_{\mathcal{F}}:T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
of a smooth field space

\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\in\mathrm{SmthSet}
is defined as the smooth vector bundle

\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\,,}

where the projection
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}
is given by postcomposition of (plots of) sections of
\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F

with the (manifold) vector bundle projection
\pi_{F}:\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\rightarrow F
.

In this sense, the map
0_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T^{*}\mathcal{F}
of Eq. (
142
) is really the canonical zero-section
of the smooth set vector bundle
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}:T^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
over the off-shell space of fields. Similarly the Euler–Lagrange
operator
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T^{*}\mathcal{F}
is another such section, i.e.,

\pi_{\mathcal{F}}\circ\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=\pi_{F}\circ EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\pi_{0}\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\phi
as

EL:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
is a bundle map over
F
(Lem.
5.8
).

Remark 5.36

(
Non-variational field theories).

Recall, by Lem.
5.8
, that
there exist differential operators

P:\Gamma_{M}(F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
, inducing smooth sections

\mathcal{P}:\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
of the variational cotangent bundle, which arise from source forms

\Omega^{d,1}_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
which cannot be variational according to Lem.
5.18
.
In other words, differential operators of this form cannot express the criticality of some action functional, and so represent local
field theories which are
non-variational
.
Nevertheless, we may still consider the smooth set of solutions of the partial differential equation
\mathcal{P}=0
,
exactly as the pullback of Cor.
5.32
.

In the case of a
noncompact spacetime

M
the action is not necessarily defined on the whole of field space
\mathcal{F}
, since fields of
noncompact support may yield noncompactly supported densities under
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
– which are
not

integrable. One could restrict to compactly supported fields
\mathcal{F}_{c}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
, in which case the action is well defined
and the criticality condition along with the functoriality result follow verbatim.
However, fields of non-compact support are considered physically viable field configurations – and so this option is too restrictive.

The proper formulation of the criticality condition for a generic local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
on a noncompact spacetime
M
is
defined
by
the joint criticality condition of all its charges (Def.
3.19
). Indeed, notice that in the previously examined case of
a compact spacetime without boundary, the well-defined action
S=\int_{M}\circ\mathcal{L}
may be thought of as the charge
\mathcal{L}_{M}
of the local
d
-form
current
\mathcal{L}
, over the compact submanifold
M
. For a general spacetime, the integral does not exist over the whole of
M
,
but it does exist over compact
d
-dimensional submanifolds - i.e., the charges

S_{\Sigma^{d}}\equiv\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma^{d}}:=\int_{\Sigma^{d}}\circ\,\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

exist for all compact submanifolds
\Sigma^{d}\rightarrow M
. The criticality condition (Def.
5.30
) may be consistently
defined for each of the charges, with an appropriate modification arising that takes care of the potential boundary of the submanifold.
That is, we only consider
1
-parameter variations that fix the value of the jet of the (plots of) field at the boundary – viewed as
a ‘boundary condition’ for the charge.

Definition 5.37

(
Critical plots of charge).

Let
S_{\Sigma^{d}}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
be the charge/action of a local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
over a
d
-dimensional compact
submanifold
\Sigma^{d}\hookrightarrow M
. The
critical
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots
of
S_{\Sigma^{d}}
is the subset of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}(S_{\Sigma^{d}})(\mathbbm{R}^{k}):=\Big\{\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\;\big|\;\partial_{t}S_{\Sigma^{d}}(\phi^{k}_{t})|_{t=0}=0,\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\,\phi^{k}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{k},\,\partial\Sigma^{d}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\Big\},

(143)

where

\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{k},\,\partial\Sigma^{d}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})=\Big\{\phi^{k}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\,\big|\,\phi^{k}_{t=0}=\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,\,\,\mathrm{and}\,\,\,j^{\infty}\phi^{k}_{t=t_{0}}|_{\partial\Sigma^{d}}=j^{\infty}\phi^{k}|_{\partial\Sigma^{d}},\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\,t_{0}\in\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t}\Big\}

is the set of
1
-parameter families of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of fields that start at
\phi^{k}
and have constant jet along the boundary
of
\Sigma^{d}
.

In less technical terms, the criticality condition for the charges employs 1-parameter variations which keep all the ‘derivatives’
of the field fixed along the boundary of the given submanifold.
61
61

61

In other words, the value of the field is fixed in an ‘infinitesimal
neighborhood’ around
\partial\Sigma^{d}
. This will be made into a rigorous definition in
[
GS24
]
.
Such variations include,
in particular, variations that are constant in some open neighborhood of the boundary (i.e., in germs around the boundary),
as most often used in the rigorous (point-set) description of the action principle (see
[
Chr00
]
).
Our definition will necessarily yield the same critical set, but is more suited to make contact with the notion of boundary
conditions in the case of a spacetime with boundary (see Remark
5.40
below).

Definition 5.38

(
Critical
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of Lagrangian).

Let
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
be a local Lagrangian over a (potentially) non-compact d-dimensional spacetime
M

without boundary. The
critical
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots
of
\mathcal{L}
is the subset of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})(\mathbbm{R}^{k}):=\Big\{\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\;\big|\;\phi^{k}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S_{\Sigma^{d}})(\mathbbm{R}^{k}),\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\;\mathrm{compact\hskip 2.84544ptsubmanifolds\hskip 2.84544pt}\Sigma^{d}\hookrightarrow M\Big\}.

(144)

With these definitions at hand, and by the (petit) sheaf theoretic nature of the fields space (Rem.
2.14
),
the result of Prop.
5.31
readily extends to the case of non-compact spacetimes
M
.

Proposition 5.39

(
Crit(
\mathcal{L}
) functoriality).

Let
M
be a noncompact manifold without boundary,
F\rightarrow M
a fiber bundle,
\mathcal{F}
its smooth set
of sections and
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
a (smooth) local Lagrangian density given by

\mathcal{L}=L\circ j^{\infty}

for some Lagrangian density bundle map
L:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
. Then the
assignment
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\mapstochar\rightarrow\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
defines a smooth set, which is equivalently given by the pullback diagram of smooth sets

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}\,,}

where
\mathcal{EL}
is the (smooth) Euler–Lagrange differential operator (
128
) of
\mathcal{L}
.

Proof.

The proof follows exactly as in Prop.
5.31
up to Eq. (
140
)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big(L\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k}_{t}\big)\big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle\;-\;\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big)\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\big)\,.

The criticality condition is obtained by demanding the criticality of each of the charges of
\mathcal{L}
.
Integrating over any compact
\Sigma^{d}\hookrightarrow M
, and for any variation
\phi_{t}
as in Def.
5.37
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}S_{\Sigma^{d}}(\phi^{k}_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma^{d}}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle\;-\;\int_{\Sigma^{d}}\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma^{d}}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle\;-\;\int_{\partial\Sigma^{d}}(j^{\infty}\phi^{k}_{0})^{*}\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}}\theta_{L}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma^{d}}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle

where the boundary term in this case vanishes since
j^{\infty}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{\partial\Sigma^{d}}
is constant in
t
by assumption,
and hence the corresponding prolongated tangent vector
\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}:M\rightarrow VJ^{\infty}F
vanishes
at the boundary
\partial\Sigma^{d}
. By Lem.
2.17
and its application to higher plots
(see Eq. (
31
) and its footnote),
this holds for all tangent vectors
\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}=X_{\phi_{0}^{k},\Sigma^{d}}\in T_{\phi^{k}_{0}}\mathcal{F}

whose support lies in the interior of
\Sigma^{d}
.

62
62

62

In more detail, this entails that the tangent vector
X_{\phi_{0},\Sigma^{d}}:M\rightarrow VF
at
\phi_{0}\in\mathcal{F}(*)
is such that

X_{\phi_{0},\Sigma^{d}}(p)=0\in V_{\phi_{0}(p)}F
if
p\notin\mathrm{int}(\Sigma^{d})
, and similarly for tangent vectors over
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots.

Since this holds for
all
such tangent vectors, it follows by the Fundamental
Lemma of variational calculus that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{k}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S_{\Sigma^{d}})(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\quad\iff\quad\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})\big|_{\mathrm{int}(\Sigma^{d})}=0_{\phi^{k}_{0}}\big|_{\mathrm{int}(\Sigma^{d})}\;\;\in\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\hat{\otimes}\Gamma_{\mathrm{int}(\Sigma^{d})}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,,

i.e.,
\phi_{0}^{k}
is a critical plot of the charge over
\Sigma^{d}
if and only if it satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equations on
the interior of
\Sigma^{d}
.

Now a plot
\phi^{k}_{0}
is a critical plot for
\mathcal{L}
(Def.
5.38
) if it is a critical plot of the
corresponding charge
for all
such
\Sigma^{d}\hookrightarrow M
, and hence if and only if

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0}^{k})\big|_{\mathrm{int}(\Sigma^{d})}=0\big|_{\mathrm{int}(\Sigma^{d})}

for all
compact submanifolds
\Sigma^{d}\hookrightarrow M
. Taking the collection of (closed) balls
\bar{B}_{p}^{d}\hookrightarrow M

around any point
p\in M
, we may cover the spacetime
M
with compact submanifolds. Hence
\phi^{k}_{0}
is a critical plot of
\mathcal{L}

if any only if it satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equations on an open neighborhood
B_{p}^{d}\hookrightarrow M
of every point.
Since the Euler–Lagrange differential operator
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
is furthermore a map of sheaves in the petit sense of
Rem.
2.14
,
\phi^{k}_{0}
satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equations
locally
if and only if
it satisfies them
globally
. In other words

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{k}\in\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\quad\iff\quad\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k}_{0})=0_{\phi^{k}_{0}}\;\;\in\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\hat{\otimes}\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\,,

and so the rest of the claim follows as in Cor.
5.32
.
∎

Remark 5.40

(
Criticality on spacetimes with boundary).

Field theories on spacetimes with boundary require further care, both in the above mathematical treatment and the corresponding physical interpretation.
We briefly summarize the relevant cases at the level of fields (with general plots following similarly), without expanding on the fine details here.

(i)
Consider first the case of a local field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
on a compact spacetime
M
with (connected) boundary

\partial M\neq\emptyset
. In this case, one may consistently compose with integration over
M
over the full field space, and
the local action functional is well-defined

S=\int_{M}\circ\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
. Naively, one has two similar but different definitions for the criticality of the action functional.
As we will see, each of these corresponds to a different choice of
boundary conditions
.

(a)
We may use Def.
5.30
which has no restrictions on the variations along the boundary
\partial M
, in which case the criticality is equivalent to the vanishing of
both
the Euler–Lagrange equations
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}=0_{\phi}|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}
on the interior of
M

and
of the integral

\int_{\partial M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big):=\int_{\partial M}(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\;\theta_{L}\,

along the boundary, for all 1-parameter variations. The vanishing of the latter is equivalent to a set of conditions relating the field
and its jets along the boundary – which depend on the explicit form of Lagrangian and in particular the ‘boundary term’
\theta_{L}
.
Since this term is defined up to the addition of
\mathrm{d}_{H}
-exact
(d-1,1)
-forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}
, this means that
in the presence of a boundary

its
choice
in the decomposition
\mathrm{d}_{V}L=EL+\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta
should actually be considered as data entering the definition of the field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
.
Such boundary conditions are called “
natural
” or “
Neumann
” boundary conditions, and may also be expressed as the
vanishing of a smooth map
\mathrm{Nm}(\phi|_{\partial M})=0
. The critical smooth set is then again a pullback, given by the intersection
of those fields that satisfy the Euler–Lagrange equations on the interior
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}=0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}

and those that satisfy the Neumann boundary conditions
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{Nm}\circ|_{\partial M}=0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
,

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}^{\mathrm{Nm}}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{Nm}\circ|_{\partial M}=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\,.}

We note that each of the smooth subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}=0}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{Nm}\circ|_{\partial M}=0}
are
themselves a pullback, respectively, given by the vanishing of the corresponding smooth maps.

(b)
We may instead use Def.
5.37
applied on
M
itself, viewed tautologically as a compact submanifold of itself,
which restricts the (jets of) variations to vanish along the boundary. As we will see, this is in fact a special case of Def.
5.30
,
applied however to a smooth subspace of the smooth field space
\mathcal{F}
. The interpretation in this case is that one has made a
choice
of
boundary values for the dynamical fields
\phi
and their derivatives.
63
63

63

More precisely if the resulting Euler–Lagrange operator is of order
k
,
the boundary jet values are fixed only up to order
k-1
. This is to avoid overdetermining the resulting PDE, and (hopefully) resulting
in a well-posed boundary value problem. The corresponding differences in the following discussion are simply in notation.

Indeed, let
\psi:\partial M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
be a smooth section over the boundary (i.e., covering
\partial M\hookrightarrow M
),
thought of as the fixed values of the (jets) of the fields we consider (see also Def.
6.28
).
Such boundary conditions on the fields are called “
imposed
” or “
Dirichlet
” boundary conditions. In mathematical terms,
this means we are not actually considering the smooth action functional
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
on the full space of fields,
but instead its restriction on the smooth subspace

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}_{\psi}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

with
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of constant infinite jet value

j^{\infty}\psi
on the boundary,

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}_{\psi}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})=\big\{\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\;\big|\;j^{\infty}\phi^{k}|_{\partial M}(x,-)=\psi(-)\hskip 5.69046pt\forall x\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}\big\}\,.

It is now immediate that applying the general Def.
5.30
on the smooth action functional
S:\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}_{\psi}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

immediately recovers exactly that of Def.
5.37
applied on
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
as a charge on
M
. In this case,
the boundary term appearing in the variation of the functional vanishes by
definition
, and so criticality is equivalent to the vanishing
of the Euler–Lagrange on the interior
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}=0_{\phi}|_{\mathrm{int}(\Sigma^{d})}
. The critical smooth set is yet again given by a pullback,

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}^{\mathrm{Dir}}_{\psi}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}_{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}_{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{\mathcal{F}}\circ|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}\,.}

One can consider all possible Dirichlet boundary values simultaneously, and hence demand instead the criticality over the smooth subspace

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

with
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of constant value on the boundary
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})=\underset{\psi:\partial M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F}{\mathop{\text{\smash{\raisebox{-9.03763pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\prod$}}}\vphantom{$\prod$}}}}\mathcal{F}_{\psi}^{\mathrm{Dir}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
. This is a smooth subspace of the space of field
\mathcal{F}
which has the
same points

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}(*)=\mathcal{F}(*)
, but
different higher
plots (and in particular less of them). The critical smooth set is given analogously by

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}^{\mathrm{Dir}}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{\mathcal{F}}\circ|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}|_{\mathrm{int}(M)}\,,}

by which it also follows that
\mathrm{Crit}^{\mathrm{Dir}}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})=\underset{\psi}{\mathop{\text{\smash{\raisebox{-9.03763pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\prod$}}}\vphantom{$\prod$}}}}\mathrm{Crit}_{\psi}^{\mathrm{Dir}}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
.
We note that each of the smooth subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{\psi}^{\mathrm{Dir}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
and
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Dir}}
may also be seen as pullbacks, respectively.

(ii)
If
M
is noncompact with (connected) boundary
\partial M\neq\emptyset
, the criticality condition
is defined locally by considering compact submanifolds as in Def.
5.38
. However, there is
the following important distinction due to the appearance of the
ambient
boundary. For submanifolds contained
in the
interior
of
M
, one uses Def.
5.37
, while for submanifolds intersecting the

ambient
boundary
\partial M
, there is a choice of boundary conditions – as in the compact case. The resulting
smooth critical sets follow the above description, analogously. One way to think of the distinction between the ambient
and interior boundaries is as follows: For any point
p
of a boundary
\partial\Sigma^{d}\subset\mathrm{int}(M)
lying
in the interior of the spacetime, there necessarily exists a closed ball
\bar{B}_{p}^{d}
around it with
B_{p}^{d}\subset\mathrm{int}(M)
.
For a given field configuration
\phi
, the criticality of the action on
\bar{B}_{p}^{d}
implies that
\phi
satisfies the
Euler–Lagrange equations at
p
. This condition is then to be viewed as a Dirichlet boundary condition for the criticality
of the charge over
\Sigma^{d}
, hence the restriction of the (jets of) the variations Def.
5.37
.
Crucially, this line of thought breaks down for points in the ambient boundary
p\in\partial M
, allowing for
a
choice
of boundary condition as outlined above.

(iii)
If the boundary has several connected components
\partial M=\mathop{\text{\smash{\raisebox{-9.03763pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\prod$}}}\vphantom{$\prod$}}}_{i\in I}N_{i}
, one may choose different
boundary conditions independently for each component, and the description of the smooth critical sets is modified accordingly.

(iv)
For any of the cases above, and any choice of boundary condition, it is not guaranteed that the resulting boundary
value problem will have a solution, and even if it does, it may not be unique. Such questions are within the realm of analysis of PDEs,
which falls outside the scope of this manuscript.

Remark 5.41

(
Criticality via moduli space of 1-forms).

We may reformulate the discussion of criticality condition (Def.
5.30
) in a slightly more functorial way, by employing
smooth 1-forms on
\mathcal{F}
in the sense of Def.
2.31
. Unfortunately, the situation is more convoluted than the naive
interpretation of the notation suggests. Recall (Def.
2.32
)
the de Rham differential 1-form
\mathrm{d}S\in\Omega^{1}(\mathcal{F})
of a smooth map
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

may be defined as the composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}S:\mathcal{F}\xlongrightarrow{\;\;S\;\;}y(\mathbbm{R})\cong\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{0}\xlongrightarrow{\;\;\mathrm{d}\;\;}\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{1}\,.

In particular, when evaluating on
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plots, this takes the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})

\displaystyle\phi_{t}

\displaystyle\longmapsto{\partial_{t}}S(\phi_{t})\mathrm{d}t,

naturally encoding the partial derivative of (
136
). This is naturally extended
to a smooth map out of the path space
\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})=[\mathbbm{R}^{1},\mathcal{F}]
of Ex.
2.22

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{t}S:\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447ptS\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR,\mathrm{Vert}}}^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\xlongrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\mathrm{d}_{t}\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR},\mathrm{Vert}}^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

\displaystyle\phi^{k}_{t}

\displaystyle\quad\longmapsto\quad S(\phi^{k}_{t})\qquad\quad\longmapsto\quad\partial_{t}S(\phi^{k}_{t})\cdot\mathrm{d}t\,,

where we have used

[\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t},\mathbbm{R}]\cong y(\mathbbm{R})\hat{\otimes}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\cong\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR},\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})
.
The smooth (vertical) de Rham differential

\mathrm{d}_{t}:\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{Vert},\mathrm{dR}}^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\rightarrow\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{Vert},\mathrm{dR}}^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\cong y(\mathbbm{R})\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

here coincides with that of (
45
). This composition naturally encodes all the partial derivatives along 1-parameter
families of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots appearing in Def.
5.30
. However, this is somewhat misleading since neither of these maps is
exactly what is denoted as the variation ‘
\delta S
’ in the physics literature. Indeed :

(i)
The ‘variation
\delta S
’ of the smooth function
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
, viewed as acting on paths of (plots) of fields,
may be encoded by further composing with the smooth evaluation map

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}:\Omega^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\otimes(T^{*}_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{1})\cong y(\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle f_{t}^{k}\cdot\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\longmapsto f_{t=0}^{k}\cdot\mathrm{d}t|_{t=0}\,,

and so via the smooth map

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}\circ\mathrm{d}_{t}S:\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\otimes T^{*}_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{1}\cong y(\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\phi^{k}_{t}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\partial_{t}S(\phi^{k}_{t})|_{t=0}\cdot\mathrm{d}t|_{t=0}\,.

(ii)
Comparison with Def.
5.30
shows that the critical
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots are
not
given by those that vanish
under
\mathrm{d}S
,
\mathrm{d}_{t}S
or
\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}\circ\mathrm{d}_{t}
. The latter of the three encodes the variation
\delta S
, but the criticality
vanishing condition is on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of the actual field space
\mathcal{F}
and
not
of
\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})
. Direct inspection gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\cong\Big\{\phi^{k}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\;\big|\;\delta_{\phi^{k}_{t}}S=\mathrm{d}_{t}S(\phi^{k}_{t})|_{t=0}=0,\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\,\phi^{k}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{k}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})\Big\}\,.

(iii)
For a generic smooth map
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
, by Ex.
2.18
, the composed map

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}\circ\mathrm{d}_{t}S:\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

may not necessarily depend
only
on the tangent vector at
\phi^{k}
corresponding to each
\phi^{k}_{t}
, i.e., does not necessarily factor through

\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}
of Ex.
2.22
. For a local function S, e.g.

S=\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
, for which the above derivative does depend only on the
corresponding tangent vector (Ex.
6.10
), then the variation does (uniquely) smoothly
factor through the tangent bundle

\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}\circ\mathrm{d}_{t}S:\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})\,,

in which case it can be thought of as a
1
-form in the traditional sense of a real-valued map out of
T\mathcal{F}
. Indeed, this can be made much more explicit
via the use of the local bicomplex of
section

7
(see Lem.
7.9
and
Eq. (
200
)). In the synthetic setting of
[
GS24
]
,
this factorization will be guaranteed,
64
64

64

This will be essentially
by definition
of (infinitesimally) thickened smooth sets,
whereby smooth maps are such that they ‘preserve the infinitesimal structure’.
a fact related to the classifying nature of

\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{1}
(see Rem.
2.33
).

(iv)
It is still not guaranteed that the criticality condition is functorial for an arbitrary local function. Intuitively, this is
due to the fact that in general, a smooth fiber-wise linear map
T\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})
is not necessarily given by a section of some ‘cotangent bundle’.
If the local function in question is a smooth action functional
S=\int_{M}\circ\mathcal{L}
on a compact manifold
M
, for some local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
,
then Prop.
5.31
shows that the induced variation map on the tangent bundle
is given by

\displaystyle T\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\int_{M}\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k})\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k}_{t}|_{t=0}\rangle

which makes the criticality condition functorial. Cor.
5.32
amplifies this by noting that the map out of the tangent
bundle is equivalently determined via the section of the
variational
cotangent bundle (Def.
5.35
) over
\mathcal{F}

\textstyle{T^{*}_{\mathrm{Var}}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\,.}

We stress that this description fails for generic local functions and the induced map
T\mathcal{F}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

obtained via point
(iii)
.

(v)
The above discussion applies in all cases of spacetimes, compact or noncompact, with or without boundary,
by demanding the local criticality of charges and encoding the potential boundary condition choices in
\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})
.

The critical set description may be employed to show that
all
notions of (finite) symmetries of a Lagrangian field
theory
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{F})
(Def.
3.23
) do in fact preserve the on-shell space of fields,
extending the result of Prop.
5.28
.

Proposition 5.42

(
Symmetries preserve on-shell space).

Any (spacetime covariant) symmetry
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
of a Lagrangian
field theory such that
\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}=f^{*}\circ(\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K})
, where
\mathcal{K}=K\circ j^{\infty}
for some

K\in\Omega^{d-1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
and
f:M\xrightarrow{\sim}M
, preserves the smooth critical subspace

\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
of on-shell fields. That is, the diffeomorphism

\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
restricts to a diffeomorphism

\mathcal{D}|_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})}:\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\,.

Proof.

For the sake of being concise, we prove this in the case of a compact spacetime with boundary, with the noncompact
case being completely analogous using the criticality of local charges instead. The following holds for either Neumann
or Dirichlet boundary conditions, and so we do not indicate the choice in the notation.
Let
\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{D}}:=\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
, then for any plot
\phi_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{D}}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\big(f^{*}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t})+f^{*}\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}(\phi_{t})\big)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=f^{*}\big(\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}\big)

\displaystyle=f^{*}\Big(\big\langle\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K})(\phi_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle-\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=f^{*}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle-f^{*}\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big)

where in the second line we used that
f^{*}
and
\partial_{t}
commute as operators on
\Omega^{d}(M)\hat{\otimes}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})
,
in the third we employ Eq. (
139
) for the local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}
and in the
fourth we use the fact that exact Lagrangians have trivial Euler–Lagrange operators via Eq. (
127
).

Since
f:M\rightarrow M
is a diffeomorphism, it preserves the integral of top-forms (up to a sign) and so

\displaystyle\partial_{t}S^{\mathcal{D}}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{D}}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}=\int_{M}f^{*}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle-\int_{M}f^{*}\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big)

\displaystyle=\pm\int_{M}\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle-(\pm)\int_{M}\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big)

\displaystyle=\pm\partial_{t}S(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}\,.

It follows
\phi_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
is a critical point for
S^{\mathcal{D}}
if and only if it is so for
S
. The argument follows
identically for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots, and hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{D}})\cong\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\cong\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=0}\,.

(145)

Next, let
\phi_{0}\in\mathcal{F}(*)
and define
\psi_{0}:=\mathcal{D}(\phi_{0})\in\mathcal{F}(*)
. Since
\mathcal{P}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
is a diffeomorphism, any
line plot
\psi_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})
starting at
\psi_{0}
is given (uniquely) by
\mathcal{D}(\phi_{t})
for some
\phi_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t})
.
By Eq. (
139
), it is again the case that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{t})|_{t=0}=\big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{0}),\,\partial_{t}\psi_{t}|_{t=0}\big\rangle\;-\;\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\big)\,,

i.e.,
\psi_{0}=\mathcal{D}(\phi_{0})
is a critical point for
\mathcal{L}
if and only if it satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equations of
\mathcal{L}
and the chosen boundary conditions.
On the other hand, by definition

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{t})|_{t=0}:

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\big(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}(\phi_{t})\big)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{D}}(\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

whose integral vanishes for all
1
-parameter variations if and only if
\phi_{0}\in\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})
, by Eq. (
145
).
That is,
\phi_{0}
is an on-shell field for
\mathcal{L}
if and only if
\mathcal{D}(\phi_{0})
is an on-shell field for
\mathcal{L}
,

\phi_{0}\in\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\hskip 34.14322pt\iff\hskip 34.14322pt\mathcal{D}(\phi_{0})\in\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\,.

The discussion follows identically for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots, and hence the result follows.
∎

6
Infinitesimal symmetries

6.1
Local infinitesimal symmetries and Noether’s First Theorem

Having introduced local symmetries (Def.
3.23
) of a Lagrangian field theory, we now
proceed to define their infinitesimal version.
These are
local
vector fields on field space that preserve the Lagrangian up to an exact local Lagrangian,
and whose interplay with a certain subspace of
vertical vector fields on the infinite jet bundle immediately yields Noether’s First and Second theorems.
As Lem.
6.17
will show, local vector fields do in fact capture the infinitesimal
version of spacetime covariant symmetries as well, thus justifying the focus solely
local
vector fields.

Definition 6.1

(
Local vector fields).

A smooth vector field
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F})=\Gamma_{\mathcal{F}}(T\mathcal{F})
on field space is
local
if it is given by

\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}

for some bundle map

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\infty}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{Z}
\textstyle{VF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

over the total space
F
of the field bundle
F\rightarrow M
, via Lem.
3.15
.
The subset of local vector fields is denoted by
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\subset\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F})
.

As usual, the name is justified since for each
\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)

the value of the tangent vector
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}
at each
x\in M
depends only ‘locally’ on
\phi
, via its jet

j^{\infty}_{x}\phi
at
x\in M
. By Lem.
3.5
, such bundle maps are locally represented by (finite) sums

\displaystyle Z=Z^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\,,

(146)

where
\{Z^{a}\}\subset C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
are (locally defined) smooth functions on the infinite jet bundle, and

\{\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\}
is the local coordinate basis for vertical tangent vectors on
F
. Thus in the physics literature, by abuse of notation
as in Ex.
2.24
and
2.25
,
such a local vector field
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F})
is usually denoted by

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}^{a}(\phi)\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}=Z^{a}\big(\phi,\{\partial_{I}\phi\}_{|I|\leq k}\big)\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{or}\hskip 28.45274pt\delta_{Z}\phi^{a}=\mathcal{Z}^{a}(\phi)=Z^{a}\big(\phi,\{\partial_{I}\phi\}_{|I|\leq k}\big)\,,

(147)

with the latter thought of as a smooth (and local) ‘infinitesimal transformation of the field’. The latter notation will be fully
justified in
section

7
and Lem.
7.7
. Recall, we have already introduced examples
of such local vector fields in Ex.
2.24
and Ex.
2.25
,
where the corresponding bundle maps factor globally through
J^{0}_{M}F
and
J^{1}_{M}F
, respectively.
Given their importance, the bundle maps inducing local vector fields are given a special name.

Definition 6.2

(
Evolutionary vector field).

The set of
evolutionary vector fields

\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is the set of smooth bundle maps

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\infty}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{Z}
\textstyle{VF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

over the total space
F
of the field bundle
F\rightarrow M
.

Example 6.3

(
Differentiating finite local diffeomorphisms).

Consider a smooth 1-parameter family of
local
diffeomorphisms
\mathcal{P}_{t}\in[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}](\mathbbm{R}^{1})
starting at the identity
\mathcal{P}_{0}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{F}}
, via Ex.
2.23
, and so such that
\mathcal{P}_{t}:=P_{t}\circ j^{\infty}
for some smooth 1-parameter family of bundle maps

\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
