\scriptstyle{P_{t}}
\textstyle{F\;,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

with
P_{t=0}=\pi^{\infty}_{0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
. Running through the differentiation of Ex.
2.23

(also carried out explicitly in the particular cases of Ex.
2.24
and Ex.
2.25
),
the interested reader may verify that its
infinitesimal version, i.e., the induced vector field
\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{t}|_{t=0}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F})
is
local
in the sense of Def.
6.1
.
In particular,

\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{t}|_{t=0}=Z\circ j^{\infty}\,,

where
Z=\partial_{t}P_{t}|_{t=0}
is the induced evolutionary vector field

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\infty}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{Z}
\textstyle{VF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\,.}
\textstyle{F}

In other words, local vector fields are indeed the infinitesimal version of 1-parameter local diffeomorphisms.

Strictly speaking, evolutionary vector fields are
not
vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, since they do not take values in
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.
Nevertheless, they may be uniquely ‘prolongated’ to a Lie sub-algebra of vertical vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
whose explicit form and properties
rigorously justify several formulas appearing in field theory. For evolutionary vector fields arising by differentiating
1
-parameter families of
bundle maps
P_{t}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow F
over M as in Ex.
6.3
, the corresponding vector fields on

J^{\infty}_{M}F
arise by differentiating the corresponding prolongated families
\mathsf{pr}P_{t}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F

from Def.
3.16
. More generally, we have the following result
[
An89
]
.

Proposition 6.4

(
Prolongated evolutionary vector fields).

(i)

For any
Z\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, there exists a unique ‘prolongated’ vertical vector field

\mathsf{pr}Z:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow VJ^{\infty}_{M}F
, i.e.,
such that

(a)

it projects to
Z

\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ{\rm pr}Z=Z\,,

(b)

it commutes with the horizontal differential

[\iota_{{\rm pr}Z},\mathrm{d}_{H}]=0\,.

(ii)
Conversely, any vertical vector field
\hat{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
such that

[\iota_{\hat{Z}},\mathrm{d}_{H}]=0
defines an evolutionary vector field via

\mathrm{d}\pi_{0}^{\infty}\circ Z:J^{\infty}_{M}F\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow VF\,.

(iii)
The two processes are inverse to each other.

In local coordinates, the coefficients of any vertical vector field

\hat{Z}=0+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\hat{Y}^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}
such that

[\iota_{\hat{Z}},\mathrm{d}_{H}]=0
are necessarily of the form
\hat{Y}^{a}_{I}=D_{I}(\hat{Y}^{a})
, where

\hat{Y}^{a}=\hat{Y}^{a}_{0}
are the coefficients of

\{\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\}
. This can be checked by evaluating the condition

[\iota_{\hat{Z}},\mathrm{d}_{H}]=0
inductively on the local
‘generators’
\big\{x^{\mu},\,\{u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|},\,\mathrm{d}x^{\mu},\,\{\mathrm{d}_{H}u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|},\,\{\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}

of the variational bi-complex. That is,

\hat{Z}=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}D_{I}(\hat{Y}^{a})\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\,,

and so if
Z=Z^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is an evolutionary vector field,
then its prolongation is given locally by

\displaystyle\mathsf{pr}Z=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}D_{I}(Z^{a})\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\,.

(148)

Remark 6.5

(
On the nomenclature of functional forms).

Let
\omega^{p,q}\in\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
be any
(p,q)
-form. Then for each q-tuple of evolutionary vector
fields
Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{q}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, there is an induced horizontal
p
-form

\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}}\cdots\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{q}}\omega^{p,q}=\omega^{p,q}(\mathsf{pr}Z_{1},\cdots,\mathsf{pr}Z_{q})\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

and hence an induced smooth current

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vect}}(M)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}

\displaystyle\longmapsto(j^{\infty}\phi^{k})^{*}\omega^{p,q}(\mathsf{pr}Z_{1},\cdots,\mathsf{pr}Z_{q})\,.

For the case of
p=d
, the decomposition

\Omega^{d,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\Omega^{d,q}_{f}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\oplus\mathrm{d}_{H}\Omega^{d-1,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
of Prop.
5.10

says that the non-trivial part of the above current is the functional form component of
\omega^{d,q}
. Similarly, the value
of the corresponding charges defined over compact submanifolds without boundary is completely determined by the functional form component.
In other words, the resulting charges (functionals) on field space are completely determined by the subspace of functional
forms, justifying the name given. Thus, each functional form
\omega^{d,q}\in\Omega^{d,q}_{f}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
may be thought of as defining
a family of Lagrangian densities, (smoothly) parametrized by
q
-tuples of evolutionary vector fields.

Corollary 6.6

(
Cartan calculus for evolutionary vector fields).

The evolutionary vector fields
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
may be naturally identified
with a Lie sub-algebra of
\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

(i)
The Lie derivative along an
(prolongated) evolutionary vector field is given by

\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}=[\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z},\mathrm{d}_{V}]

and satisfies

\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{H}=\mathrm{d}_{H}\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\,,\hskip 56.9055pt\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{V}=\mathrm{d}_{V}\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\,.

(ii)
The Lie bracket of any two (vertical)
evolutionary vector fields is vertical

[{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}},\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}]\in\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

(iii)

For any two evolutionary vector fields

\iota_{[{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}},\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}]}\mathrm{d}_{H}=\mathrm{d}_{H}\iota_{[{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}},\mathsf{pr}X_{Z}]}\,.

That is,
[{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}},\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}]
is (the prolongation) of some evolutionary vector field
Z_{3}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}F)
.

(iv)
In local coordinates,

[{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}},\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}]=\mathsf{pr}Z_{3}

where
Z_{3}=\Big(D_{I}Z_{1}^{b}\cdot\frac{\partial Z_{2}^{a}}{\partial u^{b}_{I}}-D_{I}Z_{2}^{b}\cdot\frac{\partial Z_{1}^{a}}{\partial u^{b}_{I}}\Big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}.

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows from the commutation
[\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z},\mathrm{d}_{H}]=0
, part
(ii)

from vertical vector fields being involutive, part
(iii)
by the general Cartan Calculus formula

\iota_{[{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}},\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}]}=[L_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}},\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}}]
, and part
(iv)
by direct checking.
∎

The corollary implies the existence of an induced Lie algebra structure on the set of local vector fields. Via Ex.
6.3
,
it may be checked that this is the infinitesimal version of the group structure of the subgroup of local diffeomorphisms

\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Diff}(\mathcal{F})
.

Definition 6.7

(
Lie algebra of local vector fields).

(i)
The Lie bracket of two local vector fields,
\mathcal{Z}_{1}=Z_{1}\circ j^{\infty},\;\mathcal{Z}_{2}=Z_{2}\circ j^{\infty}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
, is given by

[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]:=Z_{3}\circ j^{\infty}\,,

where
Z_{3}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is the evolutionary vector field corresponding to
[\mathsf{pr}Z_{1},\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}]\in\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,
as in Cor.
6.6
​(iii).

(ii)
In local coordinates, by Eq. (
4.10
)
and Cor.
6.6
​(iv), this is locally given by

\mathcal{Z}_{3}(\phi)=[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}](\phi)=\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\big(Z_{1}^{b}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\partial Z_{2}^{a}}{\partial u^{b}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\Big)\;-\;\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\big(Z_{2}^{b}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\partial Z_{1}^{a}}{\partial u^{b}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\Big)\!\bigg)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\,,

or abusively as in Eq. (
147
) and Rem.
5.13
by

\mathcal{Z}_{3}(\phi)=\bigg(\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}^{b}_{1}(\phi)}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\delta Z^{a}_{2}(\phi,\{\partial_{J}\phi\}_{|J|\leq k_{2}})}{\delta\phi^{b}_{I}}\;\;-\;\;\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}^{a}_{2}(\phi)}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\delta Z^{b}_{1}(\phi,\{\partial_{J}\phi\}_{|J|\leq k_{1}})}{\delta\phi^{b}_{I}}\bigg)\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}\,.

Note that the action of a (prolongated) evolutionary vector field on horizontal forms
P\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
simplifies further

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P

\displaystyle=[\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z},\mathrm{d}_{V}]=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{V}P\,,

since
\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P=0
as
\mathsf{pr}Z
is vertical. Locally,
if
P=P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}(x,\{u_{I}^{a}\}_{|I|\leq k})\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}
the action takes
the familiar form

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\bigg(\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\cdot\mathrm{d}_{v}u^{a}_{I}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\!\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}D_{I}(Z^{a})\cdot\frac{\partial P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}

(149)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{pr}Z(P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}})\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\,.

As we will demonstrate, this encodes the textbook presentation of the action of local vector fields on local currents and functions on field space.
To see this, note that by (
4.10
) and for any field

\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)
, the local formula for
the prolonganted vector field (
148
) gives

\mathsf{pr}Z\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\big(Z^{a}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\big)\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}

as a section of
VJ^{\infty}F
over
M
. That is, as a section of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(VJ^{\infty}F)\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)

we may abusively denote the (prolongated) vector field
\mathsf{pr}\mathcal{Z}:=\mathsf{pr}Z\circ j^{\infty}
on
\mathcal{F}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{pr}\mathcal{Z}(\phi)=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}^{a}(\phi)}{\partial x^{I}}\frac{\delta}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{a})}\,,

(150)

similar to (
147
).

Analogously, by the local formula
(
6.1
) of
\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P
, the value of the induced
differential operator
\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P\circ j^{\infty}_{M}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
on a field
\phi
is given by

\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial(Z^{a}\circ j^{\infty}\phi)}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\partial P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\,,

which is presented, abusing notation as in Rem.
5.13
, as

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)=

\displaystyle=\mathsf{pr}\mathcal{Z}(\phi)\Big(P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}\big(\phi,\{\partial_{J}\phi\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)\!\Big)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}^{a}(\phi)}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\delta P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}\big(\phi,\{\partial_{J}\phi\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{a})}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\,.

(151)

Definition 6.8

(
Action of local vector fields on currents).

The Lie algebra of local vector fields
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
(Def.
6.1
) acts on
local currents (Def.
3.17
) via the
prolongation of the corresponding evolutionary vector field. Explicitly:

(i)
If
Z\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is an evolutionary vector
field with induced local vector field
\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
and
P\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is a horizontal

(p,0)
-form with induced current
\mathcal{P}=P\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P}):=\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}(P)\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,.

(152)

(ii)
Locally, the value of the resulting current on a field
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P})(\phi)=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial(Z^{a}\circ j^{\infty}\phi)}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\partial P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}

and similarly on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots of fields.

(iii)
Via the abuse of notation (
150
) and (
6.1
),
the value on a field
\phi
may be equivalently calculated as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P})(\phi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P\circ j^{\infty}\phi=\mathsf{pr}\mathcal{Z}(\phi)\Big(P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}\big(\phi,\{\partial_{J}\phi\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)\!\Big)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}^{a}(\phi)}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\delta P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}\big(\phi,\{\partial_{J}\phi\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{a})}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\,,

by treating
\{\partial_{I}\phi^{a}\}_{|I|\leq k}
as independent and computing the corresponding partial derivatives,
via Rem.
5.13
.

(iv)
Using further the ‘infinitesimal transformation of the field’ notation, it may also be written as

\displaystyle\delta_{Z}\mathcal{P}(\phi)=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial(\delta_{Z}\phi^{a})}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\frac{\delta P_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}\big(\phi,\{\partial_{J}\phi\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{a})}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}

recovering the formulas implicitly used in the physics literature. The latter will be fully justified in
section

7

and Lem.
7.7
.

By construction and Cor.
6.6
,
this is a Lie algebra action via derivations with respect to the (graded) algebra structure of currents. In
section

7
,
this action will be identified with the “Lie derivative along
\mathcal{Z}
”, in an appropriate sense. Integrating the resulting currents along submanifolds,
we get an induced action on charges and hence on the algebra of local functions on field space.

Definition 6.9

(
Action of local vector fields on local functions).

The Lie algebra of local vector fields
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
acts on charges, the generators of smooth local functions
C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})

of Def.
3.20
via the action on the corresponding current, and is
extended as a derivation to all of
C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
.

(i)
If
X\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is an evolutionary vector field with induced local
vector field
\mathcal{Z}=X\circ j^{\infty}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
and
P\in\Omega^{p,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is a horizontal

(p,0)
-form with induced charge
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}P\circ j^{\infty}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
,
then

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}):=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P})=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}(P)\circ j^{\infty}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,.

(153)

(ii)
If

P^{\prime}\in\Omega^{p^{\prime},0}(J^{\infty}_{M})
is an other horizontal
(p^{\prime},0)
form with induced charge
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{p^{\prime}}}
, then

\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}\cdot\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{p^{\prime}}}):=\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}})\cdot\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{p^{\prime}}}+\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}\cdot\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{p^{\prime}}})\,.

Remark 6.10

(
Tangent vectors and derivations of local functionals).

Let

Z_{\phi}=\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\in T_{\phi}(\mathcal{F})=\Gamma_{M}(VF)
be a tangent vector at
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
, represented by
an
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot
\phi_{t}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)(\mathbbm{R}^{1})
.

(i)
Recall, as in Rem.
2.18
, there is an induced
\mathbbm{R}
-valued derivation of local functionals

\displaystyle C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\partial_{t}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}\circ\phi_{t})|_{t=0}\,.

(ii)
Contrary to the case of general smooth functions on
\mathcal{F}
as in
Rem.
2.18
, the derivation on local functionals is clearly independent of the representative
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot.
To see this, notice that for each
x\in M

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(P\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x))|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}(x)|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{V}P|_{j^{\infty}\phi_{0}(x)}

as in Eq. (
112
), where the latter ‘prolongated’ tangent vector

\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\in\Gamma_{M}(VJ^{\infty}F)
depends only on
\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\in\Gamma_{M}(VF)
, as can be seen explicitly in
Ex.
4.7
. Notice that varying pointwise in
x\in M
this contraction
only
partially
defines a‘
(p,0)
-form on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
’, that is only along the image of
j^{\infty}\phi
.
However, it does define a top-form on the base
M
, denoted by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(P\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\big(\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{V}P)

where the pullback form acts as in Lem.
5.21
. Thus, on the induced local function

\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}
we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}\circ\phi_{t})\big|_{t=0}=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}\partial_{t}(P\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t})\big|_{t=0}=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{V}P\,,

which manifestly depends only on
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
and
Z_{\phi}=\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\in T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}
.

(iii)
This observation may be used as an alternative definition of the action of local vector fields.
Indeed, for a local vector field
\mathcal{Z}
we may instead define
\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}})
by its value on field configurations as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}})(\phi):=\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}(\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}})=\int_{\Sigma^{p}}\partial_{t}(P\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t})|_{t=0}\,,

(154)

where
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}=Z\circ j^{\infty}_{M}(\phi)\in T_{\phi}(\mathcal{F})
,
is be represented as

\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}=\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\,,

for some smooth
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot of fields
\phi_{t}
(Lem.
2.17
).
This recovers the expression of Def.
6.9

since

\partial_{t}(P\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t})|_{t=0}=(j^{\infty}\phi)^{*}\iota_{\partial_{t}j^{\infty}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}}\mathrm{d}_{V}P=(\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{V})\circ j^{\infty}\phi=L_{\mathsf{pr}Z}P\circ j^{\infty}\phi\;.

(iv)
The same statements apply for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots.
Note that this is in contrast with a general vector field, which might not necessarily define an action on smooth (or local)
functionals, as described in (
33
). From this description, the derivation extension of
Def.
6.9
is deduced as a property instead.

(v)
For a local vector field

\mathcal{Z}=\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{t}|_{t=0}
arising from a 1-parameter family of local diffeomorphisms as in
Ex.
6.3
, the Lie algebra action corresponds to the differentiation of the pullback map

\mathcal{P}_{t}^{*}:C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\mathcal{F})\,,

and hence is the infinitesimal version of the
\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
action on
C^{\infty}(\mathcal{F})
.

At this point, we are well-equipped to define the notion of an infinitesimal local symmetry of a local Lagrangian field theory.
With this notion at hand, the statement and proof of Noether’s First and Second theorems follow easily, as a result on the
bicomplex of the infinite
jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, which naturally pulls back to the field space as a statement about local currents and their charges.

Definition 6.11

(
Infinitesimal local symmetry of Lagrangian field theory).

An evolutionary vector field
Z\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is an
infinitesimal symmetry
of
a Lagrangian density
L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
if

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}L=\mathrm{d}_{H}K_{Z}

(155)

for some
K_{Z}\in\Omega^{d-1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
. Equivalently,
by Lem.
5.21
and Def.
6.8
,
the corresponding local vector field
\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}_{M}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
is an
infinitesimal local symmetry

of the corresponding Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
if

\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{L})=\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathcal{K}_{Z})\,,

i.e., if it preserves the Lagrangian up to a trivial local Lagrangian.

It is easy to see that the Lie bracket
[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]
(Def.
6.7
) of any two infinitesimal local
symmetries
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
of a local field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
is also a symmetry. This follows since

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{[\mathsf{pr}Z_{1},\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}]}L

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}}\,(\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}}L)-\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}}L)=\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}}(\mathrm{d}_{H}K_{Z_{2}})-\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}}(\mathrm{d}_{H}K_{Z_{1}})

(156)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{1}}K_{Z_{2}}-\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z_{2}}K_{Z_{1}})\,,

where we used symmetry assumption of
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}
, and then the Cartan calculus for evolutionary vector fields of Cor.
6.6
.
That is, the subspace
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
of local vector fields consisting of local symmetries of a field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
is a Lie subalgebra

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F}),\,[-,-]\big)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\big(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}),\,[-,-]\big)\,,

(157)

which is the infinitesimal version of the (smooth) subgroup inclusion
\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathrm{loc}}
from part
(c)
of Rem.
3.25
.

There is a class of infinitesimal local symmetries that exist for
every
local Lagrangian field theory. For reasons that will become apparent shortly,
these are called
trivial
infinitesimal symmetries.

Example 6.12

(
Trivial infinitesimal symmetries).

Let
L\in\Omega^{d,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
be an arbitrary Lagrangian density. For any bundle map

T:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{2}VF\otimes\wedge^{d}TM
over
F
, there is an induced evolutionary vector field

T\cdot\mathrm{EL}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
given by the composition of bundle maps over
F

\displaystyle T\cdot\mathrm{EL}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447ptT\otimes\mathrm{EL}\hskip 8.19447pt}\wedge^{2}VF\otimes\wedge^{d}TM\otimes V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\xlongrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}(\wedge^{2}VF\otimes V^{*}F)\otimes(\wedge^{d}TM\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow VF

where the second map is the isomorphism swapping the order of the fibers, while the last map combines the fiberwise contractions

\iota_{(-)}(-):\wedge^{2}VF\otimes V^{*}F\rightarrow VF
and
\iota_{(-)}(-):\wedge^{d}TM\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\xrightarrow{\sim}M\times\mathbbm{R}
.
In local coordinates, we have

T=T^{[ab]}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{b}}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{d}}

for some collection of (local) functions
T^{[ab]}\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
antisymmetric in the indices, and similarly

\mathrm{EL}=\mathrm{EL}_{a}\cdot\mathrm{d}_{F}u^{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}
, thus the induced ‘trivial’ evolutionary vector field is locally

\displaystyle T\cdot\mathrm{EL}=T^{[ab]}\cdot\mathrm{EL}_{b}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\,,

which is the form that often appears in the physics literature.
65
65

65

As customary, the global structure of
M
,
F\rightarrow M
and
induced bundles is often ignored – hence defining a trivial symmetry simply by a collection of functions
K^{[ab]}
antisymmetric in the indices.
This is not sufficient to define a
global
evolutionary vector field on non-trivial field bundles.

By construction, this is ‘trivially’ a symmetry of the density
L

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}(T\cdot\mathrm{EL})}L=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}(T\cdot\mathrm{EL})}\mathrm{d}_{V}L=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}(T\cdot\mathrm{EL})}\mathrm{EL}+\iota_{\mathsf{pr}(T\cdot\mathrm{EL})}\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}=0+\mathrm{d}_{H}\big(-\iota_{\mathsf{pr}(T\cdot\mathrm{EL})}\theta_{L}\big)

where the first term vanishes by its local coordinate expression (see also proof of Prop.
6.14
),

\iota_{\mathsf{pr}(T\cdot\mathrm{EL})}\mathrm{EL}=T^{[ab]}\cdot EL_{a}\cdot EL_{b}
and using symmetry and antisymmetry of the indices,
while the second becomes horizontally exact by the commutation relation for evolutionary vector fields. Lastly, notice that any
such evolutionary vector field vanishes on the shell
S_{L}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
of the Lagrangian (Def.
5.12
).
Equivalently, the corresponding local vector field
\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}:=(K\cdot EL)\circ j^{\infty}=(K\circ j^{\infty})\cdot(EL\circ j^{\infty})

is an infinitesimal symmetry of the field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{T}^{[ab]}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{b}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})\,,

which vanishes on the smooth subspace of on-shell fields
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}.
It is easy to see that the Lie bracket (Def.
6.7
) of any two trivial infinitesimal symmetries is again trivial. In other words, the set of all trivial
infinitesimal local symmetries is a further Lie subalgebra of (
157
), denoted by

\displaystyle(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L},\mathrm{triv}}(\mathcal{F}),\,[-,-])\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F}),\,[-,-])\,.

The integrated (finite) version of this fact further justifies the name ‘trivial’.

Example 6.13

(
Differentiating finite local symmetries).

Consider the case where
\mathcal{D}_{t}=D_{t}\circ j^{\infty}
is a 1-parameter (finite) local symmetry of a Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
as in Def.
3.23
, starting at the identity, and so

\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{t}=\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{t}

for some
\mathcal{K}_{t}=K_{t}\circ j^{\infty}
, where
K_{t}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
is a 1-parameter family of smooth
bundle maps over
M
, with
K_{0}=0_{M}:J^{\infty}_{M}\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
the zero bundle map.
Then the interested reader may verify, proceeding as in Ex.
6.3
, that the differentiated version
of the relation recovers exactly that
of Def.
6.11

\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{L})=\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}})\,,

with
66
66

66

Strictly speaking,
\partial_{t}K_{t}|_{t=0}
is a map

J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow V(\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M)\cong\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M\times_{M}\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
which covers
K_{0}=0_{M}
via

\mathsf{pr}_{1}:\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M\times_{M}\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
. Thus it is completely determined by the projection to
the section factor, which is the one we tacitly mean above.

K_{Z}=\partial_{t}K_{t}|_{t=0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}(T^{*}M)
and

\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}
, for
Z=\partial_{t}D_{t}|_{t=0}
. In other words, local
infinitesimal symmetries of a Lagrangian field theory are indeed the infinitesimal version of finite local symmetries. In particular,
if a trivial infinitesimal local symmetry
\mathcal{T}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
of Ex.
6.12
is integrable, then the
corresponding symmetry
\mathcal{P}_{t}
is necessarily the identity
67
67

67

Strictly, only for
t\in(-\epsilon,\epsilon)\subset\mathbbm{R}^{1}
for some

\epsilon\in\mathbbm{R}
.
on the subspace on-shell fields
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
, since
\mathcal{T}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=\partial_{t}\mathcal{D}_{t}|_{t=0}
vanishes on

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

The main and most famous application of infinitesimal local symmetries is via Noether’s First Theorem, by which each symmetry produces a conserved current.

Proposition 6.14

(
Noether’s First Theorem).

[
No18
]

Let
Z\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
be an infinitesimal symmetry with
\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}L=\mathrm{d}_{H}K_{Z}
.

(i)
Then the
(d-1,0)
-form

\displaystyle P_{Z}:=K_{Z}+\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\theta_{L}

(158)

satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}P_{Z}=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}EL=\langle EL,Z\rangle\,,

where
\mathrm{d}_{V}L=EL+\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}
as in (
5.1
), and the
latter pairing is the duality bundle map

\langle-,-\rangle:(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)\otimes VF\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
over
M
.
In particular,
P_{Z}
is horizontally closed on the shell of
\mathrm{S}_{L}\hookrightarrow y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
.

(ii)
It follows the induced current

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{Z}:=\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}+\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\equiv\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}+(\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\theta_{L})\circ j^{\infty}

satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},\mathcal{Z}\rangle

and so it is conserved on the space of on-shell fields
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{EL}=0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}(P_{Z})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{H}K_{Z}+\mathrm{d}_{H}\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\theta_{L}=\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}L-\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{V}L-\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}EL+\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}-\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}EL\,,

where the first line follows by the symmetry assumption and the second by the commutation relation
[\mathrm{d}_{H},\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\theta_{L}]=0

for evolutionary vector fields. The identification
\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}EL=\langle EL,Z\rangle
follows since
EL
is a source form,
and in particular corresponds to a bundle map
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M
over F,
according to Lem.
5.8
. Explicitly,
EL=EL^{a}\cdot\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{d}

locally, and hence only the first component
of
\mathsf{pr}Z=Z^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}+\sum_{|I|\geq 1}^{\infty}D_{I}(Z^{a})\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}
contributes
to the contraction, i.e., the component of its evolutionary vector field
Z
. The induced current
\mathcal{P}_{Z}=P_{Z}\circ j^{\infty}
is
on-shell conserved by Stokes’ Theorem
5.22
. In particular the formula for
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}
follows
immediately since
\langle EL,Z\rangle\circ j^{\infty}_{M}=\langle EL\circ j^{\infty},Z\circ j^{\infty}\rangle=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},\mathcal{Z}\rangle.

∎

As with Eq. (
138
), we have used the notation for the pullback of the contraction
\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\theta_{L}
to field space
\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}:=(\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\theta_{L})\circ j^{\infty}\,.

(159)

The underlying mathematical meaning of this notation will become apparent in
section

7
, as an actual contraction
of the local vector field
\mathcal{Z}:=Z\circ j^{\infty}
and the ‘local form
\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
’ on
\mathcal{F}\times M
(see Eq. (
7.1
)).
For the moment, we will only need it and treat it as a useful notation.

The calculation may be read in reverse, implying the converse statement: Suppose there exists a current
\mathcal{P}_{Z}
which is
on-shell preserved, in such a way that
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},\mathcal{Z}\rangle
for some local vector field
\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}_{M}
.
Then the vector field is an infinitesimal symmetry of the local Lagrangian with

\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{L})=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\mathcal{P}_{Z}-(\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\theta_{L}\circ j^{\infty}_{M})\big)
. An immediate ‘trivial’ application of Noether’s First Theorem
is on the trivial symmetries from Ex.
6.12
, whereby
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{T}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=-(\iota_{\mathsf{pr}T\cdot EL}\theta_{L})

and hence
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=0
identically. Strictly speaking, an infinitesimal symmetry
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})

actually defines a family of conserved currents: One may add an arbitrary horizontally closed

(d-1,0)
-form
K_{Z}
and similarly
\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\mathrm{d}_{M}T^{\prime}
for an arbitrary horizontally (closed and hence) exact
(d-1,1)
-form.

Example 6.15

(
O(
n
)-model conserved currents).

Consider the case of the O(n)-model Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
from Ex.
3.10
, and the vector fields

\mathcal{Z}^{A}(\phi)=A^{a}_{\,b}\cdot\phi^{b}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}
from Ex.
2.24

and
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}(\phi)=\mathbb{L}_{\nu}(\phi)^{a}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}=\nu^{\mu}\cdot\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}

from Ex.
2.25
.
These are local with corresponding the evolutionary vector fields
Z^{A}=A^{a}_{\,b}\cdot u^{b}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}

and
Z^{\nu}(\phi)=\nu^{\mu}\cdot u^{a}_{\mu}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}
, respectively.

(i)
Choosing
A=(A^{a}_{\,b})\in\mathfrak{o}(n)
, it is immediate that
\mathcal{Z}^{A}(\mathcal{L})(\phi):=\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z^{A}}(L)\big(j^{\infty}\phi)=0

due to the
O(n)
-invariance of the Euclidean product
\langle-,-\rangle
on the target
W
. Recalling the variational decomposition

\delta L=EL+\mathrm{d}_{H}\theta_{L}
with
\theta_{L}=-\langle\mathrm{d}_{V}u,\star\mathrm{d}_{H}u\rangle_{g}
from Eq. (
130
),
the corresponding on-shell conserved current is

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}^{A}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=0+\iota_{\mathcal{Z}^{A}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}(\phi):=(\iota_{\mathsf{pr}}\theta_{L})(j^{\infty}\phi)

\displaystyle=-\big(Z^{A,a}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{H}u_{a}\big)\circ j^{\infty}\phi=-\mathcal{Z}^{A}(\phi)^{a}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi

\displaystyle=-A^{a}_{\,b}\cdot\phi^{b}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi_{a}\;.

(ii)
Choosing
\nu=\nu^{\mu}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\in\mathcal{X}(M)
to be a Killing vector field of the metric
g
,
\mathbb{L}_{\nu}(g)=0
,
it follows that
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}(\mathcal{L})(\phi):=\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z^{\nu}}(L)\big(j^{\infty}\phi)=\mathbb{L}_{\nu}\big(\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)=\mathrm{d}_{M}\iota_{\nu}\big(\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)
.
The corresponding on-shell conserved current is

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\iota_{\nu}\big(\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)+\iota_{\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\iota_{\nu}\big(\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)-\mathbb{L}_{\nu}(\phi)^{a}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi_{a}\,,

which may be further expanded in local coordinates as in Ex.
3.10
.
In the case of Minkowski spacetime with its Killing Lie algebra being the Poincaré Lie algebra, these currents comprise the energy-momentum tensor.

Remark 6.16

(
Integrating infinitesimal local symmetries).

Although every 1-parameter local diffeomorphism induces a local vector field on field space (Ex.
6.3
),
it is not necessarily the case that every local vector field integrates to a 1-parameter local diffeomorphism. Indeed, even in the case where

\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}
for some
Z
that globally factors through a finite order jet
J^{k}_{M}F
, it generally only integrates to a local
flow on
J^{k}_{M}F
, which is not enough to define a diffeomorphism on the full field space
\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
. Moreover, it might
be that a local vector field integrates to a spacetime covariant diffeomorphism on
\mathcal{F}
instead (see Lem.
6.17
).
Nevertheless, we stress that Noether’s First theorem (and Second, see Prop.
6.23
) applies for
any
infinitesimal
local symmetries, and the existence of corresponding conserved charges is independent of the integrability properties.

Let us close off this subsection by proving how the infinitesimal versions of finite spacetime covariant symmetries
(Def.
3.23
) are in fact
local
infinitesimal symmetries, justifying our focus – and generally
of the physics literature – on the latter.

Proposition 6.17

(
Infinitesimal spacetime covariant symmetries are local).

Let
\mathcal{D}_{t}
be a smooth 1-parameter family of spacetime covariant symmetries of a classical field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
starting at the identity.
That is,

\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{t}=f_{t}^{*}\circ\mathcal{L}+f^{*}_{t}\circ\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{t}

where
\mathcal{D}_{t}=D_{t}\circ j^{\infty}(-)\circ(\mathrm{id},f^{-1}_{t})\in[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}](\mathbbm{R}^{1})
is induced by a smooth 1-parameter family of bundle maps

\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{t}}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{t}}
\textstyle{M\,.}

covering a 1-parameter family of diffeomorphisms
f_{t}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times M\rightarrow M
, for some
\mathcal{K}_{t}=K_{t}\circ j^{\infty}
where

K_{t}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
is a 1-parameter family of smooth bundle maps over
M
. Then:

(i)
The vector field
\partial_{t}\mathcal{D}_{t}|_{t=0}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}
is local

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{D}_{t}|_{t=0}=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}=Z_{\mathrm{ev}}\circ j^{\infty}\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,,

for an evolutionary vector field
Z_{\mathrm{ev}}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow VF
over F.

(ii)
The induced infinitesimal action is an infinitesimal local symmetry of
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{t})|_{t=0}\equiv\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathcal{L})=\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathcal{K}_{Z_{\mathrm{ev}}})

for some
K_{Z_{\mathrm{ev}}}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
over M.

Proof.

We show the calculation at the level of
*
-plots of fields, with that of higher
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots being analogous.
Running through the differentiation of Ex.
2.23

(see also Ex.
6.3
), it follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{D}_{t}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}D_{t}|_{t=0}\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)\,\circ\,\mathrm{id}_{M}+\partial_{t}\big(\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\,\circ\,f_{t}^{-1}\big)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\big(\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ\mathsf{pr}D_{t})|_{t=0}\,\circ\,j^{\infty}\phi+\mathrm{d}(\pi^{\infty}_{0}\,\circ\,j^{\infty}\phi)\circ\big(\partial_{t}f^{-1}_{t}|_{t=0}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\,\circ\,(\partial_{t}\mathsf{pr}D_{t}|_{t=0})\,\circ\,j^{\infty}\phi+\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\,\circ\,\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)\big(\partial_{t}f^{-1}_{t}|_{t=0}\big)

\displaystyle=:\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\,\circ\,\mathsf{pr}Z\circ j^{\infty}\phi-\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\,\circ\,H(X)\circ j^{\infty}\phi\,,

where the first line follows by the product rule, the second by the definition (Def.
3.16
) of the prolongation

\mathsf{pr}D_{t}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
and the chain rule, while the third again by the chain rule. The last line follows by
defining the vector field
\mathsf{pr}Z:=\partial_{t}\mathsf{pr}D_{t}|_{t=0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
and recalling
68
68

68

We use

H(X)_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}=H_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\big(X(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)\big)
as a shorthand for

H\big((\pi^{\infty}_{M})^{*}X\big)(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)=H_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\big((\pi^{\infty}_{M})^{*}X(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)\big)
, since the splitting is really
a map
H:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\rightarrow TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
.
that
H_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\big(X(j^{\infty}_{p}\phi)\big):=\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)_{p}X_{p}

defines the horizontal lift of tangent vectors on
M
to tangent vectors on
J^{\infty}_{M}F

(
see Eq. (
85
),
Prop.
4.8
)
, where we define
X:=\partial_{t}f_{t}|_{t=0}:M\rightarrow TM
, hence that
\partial_{t}f^{-1}_{t}|_{t=0}=-X
.

We highlight that the vector field
\mathsf{pr}Z:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
is
not
vertical and in particular not the prolongation of some evolutionary vector
field (unless
f_{t}=\mathrm{id}_{M}
). However, the horizontal splitting of
TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
decomposes the vector field as
\mathsf{pr}Z=(\mathsf{pr}Z)_{V}+H(X)
, with the horizontal component
being necessarily the lift of
X
since
Z
covers
X
– by construction. Working in local coordinates (see (
90
)) and
using the local characterization of prolongated evolutionary vector fields (
148
), one sees that the vertical component is
in fact necessarily the prolongation
(\mathsf{pr}Z)_{V}=\mathsf{pr}Z_{\mathrm{ev}}
of an evolutionary vector field
Z_{\mathrm{ev}}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow VF
over
F

(see also
[
An89
, Prop. 1.20]
for detailed coordinate formulas). Thus

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{D}_{t}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ\mathsf{pr}Z_{\mathrm{ev}}\circ j^{\infty}+\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ H(X)\circ j^{\infty}-\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{0}\circ H(X)\circ j^{\infty}

\displaystyle=Z_{\mathrm{ev}}\circ j^{\infty}=:\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}

is in fact a
local
vector field
\mathcal{F}
. Completely analogously, it follows that the infinitesimal action
69
69

69

A priori, the left-hand side is defined only through Rem.
154
.
is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{t}\big)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{pr}Z_{\mathrm{ev}}(L)\circ j^{\infty}+\mathbb{L}_{X}(\mathcal{L})-\mathbb{L}_{X}\circ(\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathcal{L})\,.

with the latter action being that of Def.
6.8
.

Assuming further that
\mathcal{D}_{t}
is a 1-parameter covariant symmetry of the Lagrangian, we furthermore get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{t}\big)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\big(f^{*}_{t}\circ\mathcal{L}+f^{*}_{t}\circ\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{t})|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}_{X}(\mathcal{L})+\mathbb{L}_{X}(\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{0})+\mathrm{d}_{M}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}_{t}|_{t=0})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\iota_{X}\mathcal{L}+\iota_{X}\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{0}+\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\big)\,,

where in the first line we used the product rule, then the Cartan calculus on
M
and by defining

\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{0}=\dot{K}_{0}\circ j^{\infty}
where
\dot{K}_{0}:=\partial_{t}K_{t}|_{t=0}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M

(see Ex.
6.13
). Lastly, it follows immediately in coordinates that

\iota_{X}\mathcal{L}(\phi):=\iota_{X}(L\circ j^{\infty}\phi)=(\iota_{H(X)}L)\circ j^{\infty}\phi
since the Lagrangian density
L
is a horizontal form, and similarly for the second term.
Combining this with the compatibility of Lem.
5.21
, we arrive at

\partial_{t}\big(\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{D}_{t}\big)|_{t=0}=\mathrm{d}_{M}(K_{Z_{\mathrm{ev}}}\circ j^{\infty})

where
K_{Z_{\mathrm{ev}}}=\iota_{H(X)}\big(L+\mathrm{d}_{H}K_{0}\big)+\dot{K}_{0}\,\in\,\Omega^{d-1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, which completes the proof.
∎

The above, somewhat abstract, statement applies in virtually all local field theories with spacetime covariant symmetries (General relativity,
Yang–Mills, Chern–Simons). It is considerably easier to interpret the calculation in explicit examples.

Example 6.18

(
Differentiating spacetime symmetry of O(
n
)-model).

Recall the O(
n
)-model field theory of Ex.
3.10
, with field space
\mathcal{F}=[M,W]
and local Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}(\phi)=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle\phi,\phi\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\cdot(\langle\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}\,,

where
g
is a background metric on
M
and
\langle-,-\rangle
an inner product on
W
.
By Ex.
3.24
, any isometry
f:M\rightarrow M
induces a spacetime covariant symmetry via
the pullback action
\mathcal{D}=f^{*}:[M,W]\rightarrow[M,W]

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\circ f^{*}(\phi)=f^{*}\circ\mathcal{L}(\phi)\,.

(160)

Consider any 1-parameter family of isometries
f_{t}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times M\rightarrow M
starting at the identity, with induced Killing vector field
v=\partial_{t}f_{t}|_{t=0}\in\mathcal{X}(M)
on spacetime. Differentiating the corresponding 1-parameter family action on
\mathcal{L}
, i.e., the left-hand side of Eq. (
160
), it immediately follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(f^{*}_{t}\phi)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathbb{L}_{v}\phi)\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}(\phi)\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle\mathbb{L}_{v}\phi,\phi\rangle+c_{4}\cdot(\langle\mathbb{L}_{v}\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}\,.

Next recall the vector field on field space
\mathcal{Z}^{v}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}
from Ex.
2.25
corresponding to
f_{t}^{*}:\mathbbm{R}^{1}\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
. It is immediately seen to be
local
as

\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}(\phi)=\mathbb{L}_{v}(\phi^{a})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}=Z^{\nu}\circ j^{\infty}(\phi)

for
Z^{\nu}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow VF
defined locally by
\nu^{\mu}\cdot u^{a}_{\mu}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}
. It follows by the explicit formula above that the infinitesimal action the diffeomorphism
f^{*}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
on the Lagrangian is equivalently given by the action of the corresponding local vector field (Def.
6.8
)

\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(f^{*}_{t}\phi)|_{t=0}=\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}(\mathcal{L})(\phi)=\big(\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathbb{L}_{v}\phi)\,,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}(\phi)\rangle_{g}+c_{2}\cdot\langle\mathbb{L}_{v}\phi,\phi\rangle+c_{4}\cdot(\langle\mathbb{L}_{v}\phi,\phi\rangle)^{2}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}\,.

At this point, either by using the fact that
\nu\in\mathcal{X}(M)
is a Killing vector field or by differentiating the right-hand side of Eq. (
160
), it furthermore follows that the infinitesimal action satisfies

\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}(f^{*}_{t}\phi)|_{t=0}=\mathcal{Z}^{\nu}(\mathcal{L})(\phi)=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\iota_{\nu}\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)\,,

i.e., an infinitesimal local symmetry as per Lem.
6.17
.

6.2
Infinitesimal gauge symmetries and Noether’s Second Theorem

There is a class of infinitesimal local symmetries that induce redundancies in the physical interpretation of classical
field theories, in the sense that they obstruct (Prop.
6.33
) the existence of a ‘
Cauchy surface
’ (Def.
6.30
), i.e., a well-defined set of initial conditions which induce a
unique
evolution of fields via the Euler–Lagrange
equations.
These are symmetries that can be freely parametrized by ‘gauge’ parameters which are functions, or more generally sections
of vector bundles, on the spacetime
M
. Such symmetries induce currents that are conserved off-shell,

70
70

70

In general, this holds up to a trivial current that vanishes on-shell (Cor.
6.24
).

which may
equivalently be interpreted as inducing interrelations between the components of the Euler–Lagrange differential operator.

Lemma 6.19

(
Motivating case of a gauge symmetry).

Let
\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F})
be a local vector field. Suppose the local vector field

\mathcal{Z}_{f}=Z_{f}\circ j^{\infty}:=(f\cdot Z)\circ j^{\infty}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F})
is an infinitesimal symmetry of the smooth
Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
, for any
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
. That is,

\mathcal{Z}_{f}(\mathcal{L})=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{Z_{f}}\,,

for some
K_{Z_{f}}\in\Omega^{d-1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, for every
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
.

(i)

Then the
(d-1)
-form current
\mathcal{P}_{Z}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
corresponding to
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F})
is conserved off-shell

\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}=0\,:\,\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,,

and not only on the smooth space of on-shell fields
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

(ii)
Equivalently, there exist the off-shell relation between the Euler-Lagrange
differential operators of
\mathcal{L}

\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},\mathcal{Z}\rangle=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\mathcal{Z}^{a}=0\,.

Proof.

Since
(f\cdot Z)\circ j^{\infty}
is a symmetry of
\mathcal{L}
, by Noether’s First Theorem

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{f\cdot Z}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},(f\cdot Z)\circ j^{\infty}\rangle=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},Z\circ j^{\infty}\rangle\cdot f

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}\cdot f

for any
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
. In other words, for any field configuration
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
(and any higher plot),

\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}(\phi)\cdot f\in\Omega^{d}(M)
is an exact top-form for all
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
.
Integrating over closed balls
\bar{B}^{d}_{p}\subset M
around any point
p\in M
in the base manifold,

\int_{\bar{B}^{d}_{p}}\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}(\phi)\cdot f=0

for all
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
, which implies that
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}(\phi)|_{B^{d}_{p}}=0
in the interior. Thus,
\mathcal{P}_{Z}(\phi)
is
locally - and hence globally closed for any field configuration
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
.
71
71

71

Strictly speaking, this only shows that
\mathcal{P}_{Z}(\phi)

is closed in the
interior

\mathrm{int}(M)\subset M
.
It follows that, as a smooth current on field space,

0=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}:\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,,

and so
\mathcal{P}_{Z}
is conserved off-shell. By the formula
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{Z}=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},\mathcal{Z}\rangle
, being conserved off-shell is
equivalent to the identities

\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L},\mathcal{Z}\rangle=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\ \mathcal{Z}^{a}=0

between the components of the smooth Euler–Lagrange operator.
∎

The illustration above captures the main features of gauge symmetries. The ‘gauge’ parameters

f\in C^{\infty}(M)\cong\Gamma_{M}(M\times\mathbbm{R})
parametrize an (infinite-dimensional) family of symmetries

\{\mathcal{Z}_{f}:=(f\cdot Z)\circ j^{\infty}\}\subset\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
, whose dependence on
f
is
local
.
In the above example,
\mathcal{Z}_{f}
depends on f via its value at points, i.e., the zero-jet of
f
. These induce non-trivial interrelations between the components of the Euler–Lagrange operator, and the resulting current is conserved off-shell.

More generally, an infinitesimal local gauge symmetry is an infinite-dimensional subspace

\{\mathcal{R}_{e}:=R_{e}\circ j^{\infty}\}\subset\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
parametrized by ‘
gauge parameters
’, sections

e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
of a gauge parameter vector bundle
E\rightarrow M
, such that the dependence of each
\mathcal{R}_{e}
on
e
is
linear and local, i.e.,
via the infinite jet prolongation of
e
. The resulting currents satisfy similar off-shell properties, which are equivalent
to a set of identities relating the components of the Euler–Lagrange operator. We now turn to make this intuition precise.

Definition 6.20

(
Infinitesimal gauge symmetry).

A (parametrized) collection of
infinitesimal local gauge symmetries
of a Lagrangian
density
L
is an infinite-dimensional subspace of evolutionary vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
that may be identified with
the image of a
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map

\displaystyle R_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{ev}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,

\displaystyle e

\displaystyle\longmapsto R\circ(j^{\infty}e,\mathrm{id}_{J^{\infty}_{M}F})

where
E\rightarrow M
is a “
gauge parameter
” vector bundle over M, and

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}E\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 22.76228ptR}
\textstyle{VF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

is a smooth bundle map linear in the
J^{\infty}_{M}E
fibers,
72
72

72

The fiber product
J^{\infty}_{M}G\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F

is taken in
\mathrm{LocProMan}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{FrMfd}
. Equivalently, it is the infinite jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}(G\times_{M}F)

on the fiber product of the bundles
G
and
F
, and so manifestly a locally pro-manifold.

such that each evolutionary vector field

R_{e}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\cong M\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F\xlongrightarrow{\;\;\big(j^{\infty}e,\;\mathrm{id}_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\big)\;\;}J^{\infty}_{M}E\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F\xlongrightarrow{\;R\;}VF\,

is a symmetry of the Lagrangian density

\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}R_{e}}L=\mathrm{d}_{H}K_{R_{e}}\,,

for some
K_{R_{e}}\in\Omega^{d-1,0}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
with local dependence on
e
, i.e., given by a bundle map

K_{R}:J^{\infty}_{M}E\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d-1}T^{*}M
over
M
.

Composing with the jet
prolongation along
F
, this corresponds to a subspace
of local vector fields of
\mathcal{F}
, given by the image of the (smooth)
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map

\mathcal{R}_{(-)}:=R_{(-)}\circ j^{\infty}:\Gamma_{M}(E)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,,

such that for each
e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
it defines a local symmetry of the local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}

\mathcal{R}_{e}(\mathcal{L})=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{R_{e}}\,.

Let us expand the definition in local coordinates, where it appears in a more familiar form. Let
\{x^{\mu},c^{\beta}\}
be a compatible
coordinate chart for
E\rightarrow M
with induced coordinates
\big\{x^{\mu},\{c^{\beta}_{K}\}_{0\leq|K|}\big\}
for
J^{\infty}_{M}E\rightarrow M
.
Then by the linearity assumption, the gauge parameter bundle map
R:J^{\infty}_{M}E\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow VF
is locally of the form

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=\sum_{|K|=0}^{\infty}c^{\beta}_{K}\cdot R^{aK}_{\beta}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}

(161)

\displaystyle=\Big(c^{\beta}\cdot R_{\beta}^{a}+c^{\beta}_{\mu}\cdot{R}_{\beta}^{a\mu}+c^{\beta}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\cdot R_{\beta}^{a\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}+\cdots\Big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}

with the sum necessarily (locally) terminating, where each of the coefficients
\{R^{a}_{\beta},R^{a\mu}_{\beta},R^{a\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}_{\beta},\cdots\}

is a smooth function on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, and hence also of (locally) finite order in the coordinates
\big\{x^{\mu},\{u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}
.
Thus, for a gauge parameter
e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
the corresponding evolutionary vector field takes the form

\displaystyle R_{e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|K|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{K}}\cdot R^{aK}_{\beta}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}

\displaystyle=\Big(e^{\beta}\cdot R_{\beta}^{a}+\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\mu}}\cdot{R}_{\beta}^{a\mu}+\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\mu_{1}}\partial x^{\mu_{2}}}\cdot R_{\beta}^{a\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}+\cdots\Big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\,.

By abuse of notation, the corresponding local vector field on
\mathcal{F}
is often denoted by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{e}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|K|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{K}}\cdot\mathcal{R}^{aK}_{\beta}(\phi)\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}=\sum_{|K|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{K}}\cdot R^{aK}_{\beta}(\phi,\{\partial_{I}\phi\}_{|I|\leq k})\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}

\displaystyle=\Big(e^{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a}(\phi)+\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\mu}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a\mu}(\phi)+\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\mu_{1}}\partial x^{\mu_{2}}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(\phi)+\cdots\Big)\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}\,,

or even as an ‘infinitesimal transformation of the field’

\delta_{R_{e}}\phi^{a}=e^{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a}(\phi)+\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\mu}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a\mu}(\phi)+\frac{\partial e^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\mu_{1}}\partial x^{\mu_{2}}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(\phi)+\cdots\,,

exactly matching the formulae appearing in the physics literature (see e.g.
[
HT92
]
).

Example 6.21

(
Electromagnetic gauge symmetry).

The archetypical example of a gauge symmetry is that of pure electromagnetism, expressed in terms of gauge potentials.
Consider spacetime
(M,g)
as a (pseudo)-Riemannian
d
-dimensional manifold, and field bundle as the cotangent bundle

F=T^{*}M
with induced coordinates
\{x^{\mu},u_{\mu}\}
. The smooth set of electromagnetic fields is

\mathcal{F}=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(T^{*}M)\cong\Omega^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
, and the local Lagrangian of pure electromagnetism is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(A)

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot F_{A}\wedge\star F_{A}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle F_{A},F_{A}\rangle_{g}\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}A,\mathrm{d}_{M}A\rangle_{g}\cdot\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma\rho}\cdot\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}\cdot\partial_{\rho}A_{\nu}\cdot\sqrt{|g|}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}\,,

where
F_{A}:=\mathrm{d}_{M}A
is the field strength
73
73

73

For a spacetime of the form
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t}\times N
,
the traditional electric and magnetic field strengths are recovered via the decomposition

F=E\wedge\mathrm{d}t+B=E_{it}\mathrm{d}x^{i}\wedge\mathrm{d}t+B_{ij}\mathrm{d}x^{i}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{j}
.
(curvature) of the
gauge potential (connection)
A\in\Omega^{1}(M)
. Since
F=T^{*}M
is a vector bundle it follows that
VF\cong T^{*}M\times_{M}T^{*}M
, and so
T\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{F}
(see also Rem.
2.16
). Hence,
for each function
e\in C^{\infty}(M)\cong\Gamma_{M}(M\times\mathbbm{R})
on spacetime there exists the
constant
vector field

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{e}:\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\longmapsto(A,\mathrm{d}_{M}e)\,,

which in local coordinates may be represented by

\mathcal{R}_{e}(A):=\partial_{\mu}e\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta A_{\mu}}=\partial_{\mu}e\cdot\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta A_{\nu}}\quad\in\quad\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,.

Note that the dependence on the chosen function
e\in\Gamma_{M}(M\times\mathbbm{R})
is through
J^{1}_{M}(M\times\mathbbm{R})
. It is infinitesimal symmetry of the Lagrangian
as can be checked in coordinates via Def.
6.8
, or directly by the nilpotency of the de Rham differential

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{e}(\mathcal{L})(A)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathrm{d}_{M}e)\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A+\mathrm{d}_{M}A\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathrm{d}_{M}e)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}^{2}e\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A=0\,.

(162)

Thus, it defines a parametrized gauge symmetry, with gauge parameter bundle
E=M\times\mathbbm{R}
and coordinates
\{x^{\mu},c\}
, and the corresponding
bundle map
R:J^{\infty}_{M}(M\times\mathbbm{R})\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}(T^{*}M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T^{*}M\times_{M}T^{*}M\cong V(T^{*}M)
over
T^{*}M
given locally by

c_{\mu}\cdot\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{\mu}}\,.

For completeness, let us note the electromagnetism Euler–Lagrange operator, i.e., the (free) Maxwell equations take the form

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A)=\mathrm{d}_{M}\star F_{A}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1}(M),

(163)

where
\Omega^{d-1}(M)\cong\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\times_{M}TM)\cong\Gamma_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
, as expected.
The current corresponding to each infinitesimal symmetry
\mathcal{R}_{e}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}(A)=\mathrm{d}_{M}e\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A\,,

(164)

which is obviously conserved on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
.

Remark 6.22

(
The Lie algebra of all infinitesimal gauge symmetries).

For the sake of completeness,
we comment on some abstract aspects of gauge symmetries, defined via arbitrary parametrizations as above. Some of these points are explicitly
mentioned and further expanded in
[
HT92
]
, while others are implicitly necessary and slightly supplement the
(local coordinate) description of gauge symmetries therein.

(i)
The
actual
gauge symmetries
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{F})\subset\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})

are identified by the
image
of
some
local
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map
\mathcal{R}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
as in Def.
6.20
. In particular, the gauge parameter
E\rightarrow M
bundle and corresponding parametrization bundle map
R
are
not unique
.
74
74

74

For instance, let
E^{\prime}\rightarrow M
be any other vector bundle and consider the map
\tilde{\mathcal{R}}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E\times_{M}E^{\prime})\cong\Gamma_{M}(E)\oplus\Gamma_{M}(E^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
acting via
\mathcal{R}_{(-)}
on the first component and trivially on the second.
Furthermore, it might be that some gauge symmetries are in the image of some parametrization
\mathcal{R}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
, but
not
in the image of another
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(\hat{E})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
.
75
75

75

For instance, take
\hat{E}\rightarrow M
to be a proper subbundle of
E\rightarrow M
for an injective
\mathcal{R}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
. Then the restricted parametrization
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{(-)}:=\mathcal{R}_{(-)}|_{\hat{E}}:\Gamma_{M}(\hat{E})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
identifies strictly less gauge symmetries via its image.

(ii)
By Eq. (
157
), it follows that for any two gauge parameters
e_{1},e_{2}\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
of a (parametrized) gauge symmetry
R
, the commutator
[\mathcal{R}_{e_{1}},\mathcal{R}_{e_{2}}]
is also a local symmetry of
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
. Crucially, however, it is not necessarily possible to express
[\mathcal{R}_{e_{1}},\mathcal{R}_{e_{2}}]
as
R_{e_{3}}
for some other gauge parameter

e_{3}\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
.
76
76

76

This is possible for all gauge parameters if there exists a local ‘bracket map’
[-,-]^{\mathcal{R}}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E)\times\Gamma_{M}(E)\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(J^{\infty}E)
such that
\mathcal{R}_{[e_{1},e_{2}]^{\mathcal{R}}_{(\phi)}}(\phi)=[\mathcal{R}_{e_{1}}(\phi),\mathcal{R}_{e_{1}}(\phi)]_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})}
. The bracket generally might depend on the dynamical fields (in a local manner), and so the composition
\Gamma_{M}(E)\times\Gamma_{M}(E)\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(J^{\infty}E)\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}
defines a bundle map over
\mathcal{F}
, and so (generally) a
Lie algebroid
structure, rather than a Lie algebra. Nevertheless, for many physical examples (e.g. General Relativity, Yang-Mills, Chern-Simons theories) this happens to be an actual Lie algebra map.
Nevertheless, commutators of (parametrized) gauge symmetries are also considered to be
gauge
symmetries.

(iii)
We have defined an infinite-dimensional family of local symmetries, the
trivial
symmetries
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L},\mathrm{triv}}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
of Ex.
6.12
. Strictly speaking, these are not parametrized by sections of some vector bundle over
M
, but instead by vector bundle maps out
K:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{2}VF\otimes\wedge^{d}TM
over
F
. Nevertheless, trivial symmetries are also considered to be
gauge
symmetries.

(iv)
It follows that formally, the Lie algebra of all (implicit) infinitesimal gauge symmetries is the minimal subalgebra (in fact ideal) of local symmetries, containing all parametrized local symmetries
\big\{\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})\big\}_{\mathcal{R}}
and the trivial symmetries
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
,

\big(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L},\mathrm{gauge}}\,,[-,-]\big)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\big(\mathcal{X}^{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}),\,[-,-]\big)\,.

(v)
A parametrized gauge symmetry
\mathcal{R}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
is called a
generating set
if
any
infinitesimal gauge symmetry
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L},\mathrm{gauge}}
may be written as

\mathcal{Z}=R\circ(C\times_{F}\mathrm{id}_{J^{\infty}_{M}F})\circ j^{\infty}+\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}

for some bundle maps
C:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}E
and
K:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{2}VF\otimes\wedge^{d}TM
.
In simple words the vector field
\mathcal{Z}(\phi)
may be expressed via
\mathcal{R}
using a ‘field dependent gauge parameter’
\mathcal{C}(\phi)=C(j^{\infty}\phi)
,
up to a trivial gauge transformation
\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)
, and so in local coordinates

\delta_{Z}\phi^{a}=\Big(\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\phi)\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a}(\phi)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\phi)}{\partial x^{\mu}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\beta}^{a\mu}(\phi)+\cdots\Big)+\mathcal{K}^{[ab]}(\phi)\cdot\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{b}(\phi)\,.

There is a lot more to be said along these lines, which is however outside the scope of this manuscript.
For more details and explicit examples of the above concepts, albeit in the topologically trivial cases, see
[
HT92
]
.

The abstract characterization of the full Lie algebra of (implicit) infinitesimal gauge symmetries
(
Rem.
6.22
(iv)
)

is great as an abstract backbone, but is not directly amenable to explicit applications. In practice, one always treats gauge symmetries via explicit
parametrizations. Noether’s Second Theorem may be viewed as a way of detecting the existence of (parametrized) gauge symmetries of a local field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
,
and may be deduced immediately as an application of Noether’s First (Prop.
6.14
), essentially via an integration by parts argument.

Proposition 6.23

(
Noether’s Second Theorem).

Let

\mathcal{R}_{(-)}:\Gamma_{M}(E)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})

be an
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map defined by
\mathcal{R}_{e}:=R\circ\big(j^{\infty}e,j^{\infty}(-)\big)
for some bundle map

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}E\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 19.91684ptR}
\textstyle{VF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

linear in the
J^{\infty}_{M}E
fibers.

(i)
Then
\mathcal{R}_{(-)}
parametrizes a collection of infinitesimal gauge symmetries of a local field theory

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
if and only if the Euler–Lagrange differential operator satisfies the “Noether identity”

0=\mathcal{N}^{R}\circ(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\times_{\mathcal{F}}\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{F}})\quad:\quad\Gamma_{M}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)\times\Gamma_{M}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(E^{*}\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)\,,

where
\mathcal{N}^{R}:\Gamma_{M}(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)\times\Gamma_{M}(F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(E^{*}\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)

is the formal adjoint differential operator
(see, e.g.,
[
CH89
, §V.1.3]
)
of
\mathcal{R}=\Gamma_{M}(E)\times\Gamma_{M}(F)\rightarrow\Gamma_{M}(VF)
.

(ii)
Explicitly,
\mathcal{N}^{R}
is defined via (cohomological) integration by parts
77
77

77

In terms of jet bundles, this calculation corresponds to lifting the Lagrangian density via the projection
J^{\infty}_{M}E\times_{M}J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
, and applying the interior Euler operator (Def.
5.9
)
on
J^{\infty}_{M}(E\times_{M}F)
. This is, in particular, one way to justify the global validity of the local formulas which follow.
with respect to

e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
so that for any
\tilde{\phi}\in\Gamma_{M}(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)
,
\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)
and
e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)

\langle\tilde{\phi},\,\mathcal{R}_{e}(\phi)\rangle_{VF}=\langle\mathcal{N}^{R}(\tilde{\phi},\phi),\,e\rangle_{E}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{J}(\phi,\tilde{\phi},e)\,,

for some differential operator
\mathcal{J}:\Gamma_{M}(F)\times\Gamma_{M}(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)\times\Gamma_{M}(E)\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}(M)
which is,
in particular,
\mathbbm{R}
-linear in the second and third entries.

(iii)
In local coordinates,

\langle\mathcal{N}^{R}(\tilde{\phi},\phi),\,e\rangle_{E}=\sum_{|J|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|J|}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{J}}\big(\tilde{\phi}_{a}\cdot\mathcal{R}^{aJ}_{b}(\phi)\big)\cdot e^{b}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}

and so, for all
b
, the condition reads

0=\sum_{|J|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|J|}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{J}}\big(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)\cdot\mathcal{R}^{aJ}_{b}(\phi)\big)\,.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{R}_{e}
defines a symmetry, then by Noether’s First Theorem, the corresponding charge satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}(\phi)=\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),\,\mathcal{R}_{e}(\phi)\rangle_{VF}=\sum_{|J|=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)\cdot\frac{\partial e^{b}}{\partial x^{J}}\cdot\mathcal{R}^{aJ}_{b}(\phi)\,.

‘Integrating by parts’ (cohomologically) on the right-hand side with respect to the gauge parameter
e^{b}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{N}^{R}\big(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),\phi\big),\,e\rangle_{E}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{J}\big(\phi,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),e\big)

\displaystyle=e^{b}\cdot\sum_{|J|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|J|}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{J}}\big(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)\cdot\mathcal{R}^{aJ}_{b}(\phi)\big)+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{J}\big(\phi,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),e\big)\,,

one sees that
\langle\mathcal{N}^{R}\big(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),\phi\big),\,e\rangle_{E}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}(\phi)+\mathcal{J}(\phi,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),e)\big)

is an exact form on
M
for each field
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
and parameter
e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
. Proceeding as in the proof of
Lem.
6.19
, integrating locally over balls
\bar{B}_{p}^{d}\subset M
around each point
p\in M
implies that

\mathcal{N}^{R}\big(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),\phi\big)=0

on (the interior of)
M
, for
all
field configurations
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
. Conversely, reading the equations backward,
the vanishing identity implies that
\mathcal{R}_{e}
is a symmetry for every
e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
.
∎

Corollary 6.24

(
Trivial local currents from gauge symmetries).

(i)
The conserved current
\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}

of a gauge symmetry
\mathcal{R}_{e}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L},\mathrm{gauge}}(\mathcal{F})
satisfies

\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{J}_{e}\,:\,\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

for some local
\mathcal{J}_{e}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
which vanishes on the subspace
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
of on-shell fields.

(ii)
Furthermore, if the
(d-1)
-cohomology of
F
vanishes, then
\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}
is of the form

\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}=\mathcal{J}_{e}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{e}

for some local
\mathcal{T}_{e}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-2}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
. In particular, it defines a trivial on-shell local current
\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}|_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{e}
.

Proof.

By the proof of Prop.
6.23
, we have
the equality of
d
-form currents on the field space
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{J}_{e}
, where

\mathcal{J}_{e}:=\mathcal{J}\big(-,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(-),e\big)\,:\,\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

is a
(d-1)
-current whose dependence is linear in the second entry, and hence vanishes on-shell.

Now, both
\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}
and
\mathcal{J}_{e}
arise from bi-differential operators
\mathcal{P}_{R_{(-)}},\,\mathcal{J}_{(-)}\,:\,

\mathcal{F}\times\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(E)\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
induced by horizontal
(d-1)
-forms on
J^{\infty}(E\times_{M}F)
, and so by Prop.
5.18
if the
(d-1)
-cohomology of
E\times_{M}F
vanishes, then
\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}-\mathcal{J}_{e})=0
implies

\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}=\mathcal{J}_{e}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{e}

for some
\mathcal{T}_{e}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-2}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
. Note that, since
E\times_{M}F
is a (contractible) vector bundle over
F
,
their cohomologies coincide. Since
\mathcal{K}_{e}
vanishes on the smooth subspace of on-shell fields, it follows that

\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}|_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}}=0+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{e}|_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}}\,:\,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)\,.

∎

The vanishing of the
(d-1)
-cohomology in the above corollary is usually satisfied explicitly for physical theories (for instance, for field bundles
being vector bundles over Minkowski spacetime). If instead only the
(d-1)
-cohomology of the spacetime vanishes, then the current is pointwise
(in the field space
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
)
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact, but perhaps not with a local dependence in the field (Rem.
5.24
). In
[
Zu86
]
the statement is claimed without a proof and no mention of the cohomology assumption. In
[
Blo23
]

the statement is claimed as in
[
Zu86
]
, but we are not able to follow the proof therein.

Example 6.25

(
Noether identity for electromagnetism).

Consider the case of electromagnetism from Ex.
6.21
. Cohomologically integrating by parts with respect to
the gauge parameter the gauge invariance equation Eq. (
6.21
), we get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}^{2}e\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\mathrm{d}_{M}e\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A\big)+\mathrm{d}_{M}e\wedge\mathrm{d}_{M}\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}(A)+\mathrm{d}_{M}e\wedge\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}(A)+\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(e\wedge\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A)\big)-e\wedge\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A)\big)\,,

where we identified the conserved current (
164
) and the Euler–Lagrange operator (
163
).
It follows that the off-shell Noether identity corresponding to the electromagnetic gauge symmetry is given by

\mathcal{N}^{R}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A)):=\mathrm{d}_{M}\circ\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A)=\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathrm{d}_{M}\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A)=0\,,

and the corresponding current satisfies the off-shell identity

\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{R_{e}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{J}\big(A,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A),e\big):=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(-e\wedge\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(A)\big)\,.

Remark 6.26

(
Physical observables from gauge symmetries).

The potentiality of a trivial resulting current does not
imply that gauge symmetries do not produce meaningful observables via their charges over compact submanifolds with
boundary or non-compact submanifolds with suitable asymptotics, for choices of gauge parameters with support on the boundary
or suitable asymptotic support, respectively (e.g.
[
BB01
]
[
ABS15
]
).
Although we do not pursue reviewing this matter here, this story
should also naturally take place in this generalized smooth setting.

Remark 6.27

(
