derivative is given by the general form with respect to the total differential

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}+\mathcal{X}}=[\iota_{\mathcal{Z}+\mathcal{X}},\delta+\mathrm{d}_{M}]=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}+[\mathcal{X},\delta+\mathrm{d}_{M}]
, covering the previous cases of vertical local
and decomposable vector fields as special instances.

(ii)
From a physical and field-theoretic perspective, these extensions seem redundant.
Indeed, the vector fields needed are to be physically interpreted,
in particular, as infinitesimal symmetries of the
actual field space

\mathcal{F}
. As we have shown throughout
section

6.1

(and in particular Lem.
6.17
), this notion is fully captured solely by local vector fields on
\mathcal{F}
.
This perspective is implicitly shared by
[
Zu86
]
. In
[
DF99
]
, the addition of a vector field along
M
(constant along
\mathcal{F}
) is
used to bring the action of infinitesimal local symmetries of a Lagrangian into a nicer form, therein termed a “
manifest symmetry
”.
Nevertheless, this is
not always
possible (see therein) and, further, the action of any decomposable symmetry
\mathcal{Z}+X
on a
Lagrangian can always be re-expressed
99
99

99

This follows easily: In the extended local Cartan calculus,

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}+X}\mathcal{L}=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L}+\mathbb{L}_{X}\mathcal{L}=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}(\iota_{X}\mathcal{L})
since
\mathcal{L}
is in particular a top-form along
M
.
Thus if
\mathcal{Z}+X
is an infinitesimal symmetry,
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}+X}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}+X}
for some local
(d-1,0)
-form
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}+X}
,
and so
\mathcal{Z}
is a symmetry with
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}+X}-\iota_{X}\mathcal{L})
.
as a symmetry of
\mathcal{Z}
solely. Hence, the
extension of
(a)
above seems to be merely a calculational tool.
The more general vector fields of
(b)
, have (currently) no physical interpretation or application, as far as we are aware.

Relation to de Rham forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
.

As hinted in Rem.
7.2

(c)
and the corresponding
footnote, the de Rham forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
defined via the classifying space
\mathbold{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}
carry a natural bicomplex structure.
Employing Lem.
4.15
, it is possible to identify the bicomplex of local forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
arising from globally
finite order forms on the jet bundle with a subcomplex of the de Rham forms.

Lemma 7.9

(
Local forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
as de Rham forms).

The subalgebra
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{glb}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
arising by pulling back the globally
finite order differential forms
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
on the infinite jet bundle is canonically identified with a subalgebra
of de Rham forms on the
\mathcal{F}\times M
. That is, there is a canonical injective CGA map

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{glb}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

that furthermore respects the corresponding bi-complex structures.

Proof.

In cases where the pushforward of the prolongated evaluation map
\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}:T(\mathcal{F}\times M)\rightarrow T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is surjective, and hence
its precomposition is injective on the sets of differential forms, this follows immediately by Lem.
4.15
.
More explicitly, for a local
m
-form

(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\omega=\omega\circ\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}\quad:\quad T^{\times m}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,,

where
\omega=\pi_{k}^{*}\omega^{k}:T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\rightarrow T^{\times m}(J^{k}_{M}F)\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
, the corresponding de Rham
m
-form is given by

\tilde{\omega}\circ\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}=\tilde{\omega}^{k}\circ\pi_{k}\circ\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}\quad:\mathcal{F}\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{k}_{M}F\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Omega}_{\mathrm{dR}}\,\,.

In the cases where the pushfoward is not surjective, the statement follows by noticing that any local form on
\mathcal{F}\times M
depends on the underlying
jet bundle differential form only via its values along the image of
\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}
inside
J^{\infty}_{M}F
. That is, if
\omega_{1}^{k},\omega_{2}^{k}\in\Omega^{m}(J^{k}_{M}F)

define the same local
m
-form
(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\pi_{k}^{*}\omega_{1}^{k}=(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\pi_{k}^{*}\omega_{2}^{k}\in\Omega^{m}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
then it follows
that the corresponding de Rham forms
\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k},\tilde{\omega}_{2}^{k}:J^{k}_{M}F\rightarrow\mathbold{\Omega}^{m}_{\mathrm{dR}}
define the same de Rham
m
-forms

\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}\circ\pi_{k}\circ\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}=\tilde{\omega}_{2}^{k}\circ\pi_{k}\circ\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}\quad:\quad\mathcal{F}\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{m}_{\mathrm{dR}}\,,

since the latter depend only on the image of (plots of)
\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}
in
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, and vice versa.

To see that above map respects the corresponding bigradings, recall that the bigrading of de Rham forms is induced precisely by the product structure of

\mathcal{F}\times M
(see Rem.
7.2

(c)
and the corresponding footnote). On the other hand, the bi-grading of local
differential forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
, is induced by that of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
– which in turn corresponds to the product structure of
T(\mathcal{F}\times M)
(see Eqs. (
172
) and (
176
)). It follows that the inclusion

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{glb}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
necessarily respects the two bigradings.
Arguing along the same lines, it follows that it also respects the corresponding differentials and wedge products.
∎

If, as we expect, it is actually the case that
\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\cong\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
as maps of smooth sets,
then the above embedding canonically extends to local forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
induced by any
locally
finite order forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F

(although this is not necessary for virtually all existing examples of fields theories).
The identification of the above Lemma is implicit in
[
FSS13
]
[
FSS14
]
[
FRS14
]
, and is now officially formally justified. This abstract
reinterpretation of local forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
as (particular) maps into the classifying space
\mathbold{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}
allows
for many useful categorical arguments and constructions (see therein). Nevertheless, as we have made clear, the traditional description as maps out
of the tangent bundle is the most appropriate picture to make direct contact with the computational formulas and symbols appearing in the physics
literature. This fact will be further amplified in the following sections.

7.2
Presymplectic current and induced brackets

In this section, among several related results, we will rigorously formalize the observation of Zuckerman
[
Zu86
]

that any local Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
induces an “on-shell conserved (pre)symplectic
(d-1)
-current”
on its on-shell space of fields. To that end, recall the (cohomological)
‘integration by parts formula’ on the jet bundle (
5.1
), which may
now be pulled back via
\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
to give

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636pt\Omega^{d,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

(186)

where the term
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
here stands for the corresponding vertical 1-form on
\mathcal{F}\times M
given by

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi},X_{p})\longmapsto\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi),\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}\rangle(p)+\iota_{X_{p}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)\,.

(187)

Note that the latter term of the decomposition
100
100

100

The above decomposition gives an equivalent characterization of
on-shell fields, as those over which the vertical differential of the Langrangian

\delta\mathcal{L}|_{\phi}:\big(T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}\times TM\big)^{\times(d+1)}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\big(T\mathcal{F}\times TM\big)^{\times(d+1)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

vanishes, up to a
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact
local
form
\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
on
\mathcal{F}\times M
. However, this is not as natural as the criticality condition and pullback smooth set characterization.

(
5.1
), i.e. the ‘boundary term’, is defined up to a
choice
of
\theta_{\mathcal{L}}:=(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\theta_{L}
,
with the freedom to add a
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-closed local
(d-1,1)
-form. In fact, due to Prop.
5.5
, this freedom is
necessarily up to
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact local
(d-1,1)
-forms.

Definition 7.10

(
Presymplectic current).

The
presymplectic current
of a Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
is defined by

\omega_{\mathcal{L}}:=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M),

up to a
choice
of a
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact
(d,1)
-form.

This object is also termed the “universal current” in
[
Zu86
]
. The presymplectic adjective is justified since it
is in particular a
2
-form in the direction of
\mathcal{F}
, and further
\delta
-closed by construction

\delta\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta^{2}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=0\;.

It is often the case that this is a
degenerate

2
-form in the
\mathcal{F}
direction, in an appropriate sense, and hence generally only

presymplectic
and not actually symplectic. In particular, this degeneracy occurs whenever infinitesimal gauge symmetries exist (Prop.
7.23
).
Due to this definition, the chosen local
(d-1,1)
-form

\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
is also known as the
presymplectic potential current
.

Example 7.11

(
Presymplectic current for the O(
n
)-model and electromagnetism).

(i)
By pulling back the variational decomposition on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
from Ex.
5.15
,
or computing directly on
\mathcal{F}\times M
, it follows that for the O(
n
)-model Lagrangian

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=-\langle\mathrm{d}_{M}\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi+\star\phi,\delta\phi\rangle-\mathrm{d}_{M}(\langle\delta\phi\,,\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle\big)\,,

and so the presymplectic potential and current are given by

\displaystyle\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=-\langle\delta\phi\,,\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi\rangle=\delta\phi^{a}\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi_{a}\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=+\delta\phi^{a}\wedge\star\delta\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi_{a}\,.

(ii)
Similarly, the variational decomposition of the Lagrangian of pure electromagnetism from Ex.
6.21
reads

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=-\delta A\wedge\mathrm{d}_{M}\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A-\mathrm{d}_{M}(\delta A\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A)\,,

and so the presymplectic potential and current are given by

\displaystyle\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=-\delta A\wedge\star\mathrm{d}_{M}A\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta A\wedge\star\delta\mathrm{d}_{M}A\,.

The idea is now to ‘pullback/restrict’ the presymplectic current on
\mathcal{F}\times M
to a
(d-1,2)
-form on the smooth subspace

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M

and deduce its resulting properties by restricting Eq. (
186
).
For this to make sense, an appropriate definition of the tangent bundle (and hence forms)
on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
is required.

The correct intuition behind the construction is, once again, tangent vectors as first-order infinitesimal curves within

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
. As pointed out throughout the text, this too will become a special instance of the synthetic tangent bundle
in
[
GS24
]
. For now, we motivate this by considering actual
1
-parameter families of on-shell fields and calculating
the induced condition on the corresponding tangent vectors.

Consider first a 1-parameter family of (off-shell) fields
\phi_{t}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})

starting at
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
and the corresponding image
\mathbbm{R}^{1}
-plot of the variational cotangent bundle

T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}(\mathcal{F})=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M)
under the Euler–Lagrange operator

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t})\quad\in\quad T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})\,.

Denoting the corresponding tangent vector by
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}:=\partial_{t}\phi_{t}|_{t=0}\in T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}
and computing the
derivative
101
101

101

As usual, this means pointwise in
M
(see Ex.
2.15
).

at
t=0
in local coordinates via the chain rule gives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi_{t})\big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\big(EL_{a}\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}\bigg(\frac{\partial EL_{a}}{\partial u^{b}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\bigg)\cdot\partial_{t}\big(u^{b}_{I}\circ j^{\infty}\phi_{t}\big)\big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}\bigg(\frac{\partial EL_{a}}{\partial u^{b}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi\bigg)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\frac{\partial\phi^{b}_{t}}{\partial x^{I}}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{b}}{\partial x^{I}}\;.

The right-hand side manifestly depends only on the underlying tangent vector at
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
, and furthermore defines a tangent vector

102
102

102

The variational cotangent bundle is a smooth set of sections, hence Def.
2.19
applies.
We will not need the explicit form of this bundle.
in
T_{\mathrm{var}}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
. Hence, as with the pushforward of the prolongated evaluation
(Def.
7.4
), it defines a pushforward map of tangent bundles along
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
, which does in fact naturally correspond
to the synthetic pushforward
[
GS24
]
.

Definition 7.12

(
Pushforward of Euler–Lagrange operator).

The
pushforward of the Euler–Lagrange operator

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}:T\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum_{|I|=0}\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{b}}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\mathrm{d}u^{a}\,,

and similarly for higher plots.

Here we use
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}
for the pushward rather than ‘
\mathrm{d}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
’ so as to avoid confusion with the actual differentials of the bicomplex,
when
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
is viewed as a
(d,1)
-form on
\mathcal{F}\times M
. Consider now the case where
\phi_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})
is a 1-parameter
family of
on-shell fields
starting at
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(*)
, and so

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t})=0\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636ptT^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{1})\,.

Differentiating as above, it follows that corresponding the tangent vector
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}
in
\mathcal{F}
is
tangent to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}

in the sense that it vanishes along the pushforward

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi})=\sum_{|I|=0}\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{b}}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\mathrm{d}u^{a}=0\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636ptT_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)}(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})\;.

(188)

This equation is also known in the literature as the “linearized Euler–Lagrange equations” or the “
Jacobi equation
”
[
Zu86
]
,
and the corresponding tangent vectors
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}\in\Gamma_{M}(\phi^{*}VF)
as “
Jacobi fields
”. The non-trivial conditions on
the tangent vector are those of
|I|\geq 1
, since the
|I|=0
case vanishes by the Euler–Lagrange equation on
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(*)
.
The set of all tangent vectors to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
may be thought of as the pullback/intersection of the pushforward

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}:T\mathcal{F}(*)\rightarrow T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})(*)
and the (fiberwise) zero map
0_{\mathcal{F}*}:T\mathcal{F}(*)\rightarrow T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})(*)
.
All sets appearing in this intersection are of course the underlying points of actual smooth sets,
and hence the corresponding
tangent bundle smooth set

may be defined as the pullback/intersection with the zero map in
\mathrm{SmthSet}
.

Definition 7.13

(
Tangent bundle of on-shell field space).

The
smooth tangent bundle

T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
to the smooth subspace of on-shell fields is defined as the pullback

\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}_{*}}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{0_{\mathcal{F}}}_{*}}
\textstyle{T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})\,,}

in
\mathrm{SmthSet}
. In other words, this is the smooth spaces with
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots

T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})=\big\{\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k}}\in T\mathcal{F}\;|\;\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k}})=0_{\phi^{k}}\big\}\,.

Remark 7.14

(
On-shell tangent vectors vs line plots).

(i)
This is in line with the definition of on-shell tangent vectors of
[
Zu86
]
,
and hence also implicit in
[
DF99
]
.

(ii)
However, even though the definition is motivated by considering on-shell line plots and their corresponding on-shell
tangent vectors,
it is
not true
that
every
on-shell tangent vector
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}\in T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(*)
is represented by a line plot
in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
for arbitrary Lagrangians. In other words, the analog of Lem.
2.17
fails for the on-shell space of fields.

(iii)
In fact, this does not stem from the infinite dimensionality of the setting, but can be also seen in the

0
-dimensional field theory of Ex.
5.34
. Indeed, consider the case where
N=\mathbbm{R}
and

S:N\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
is given by
S(x)=\frac{x^{3}}{3}
, so that
\mathrm{d}S:\mathbbm{R}\rightarrow T^{*}\mathbbm{R}=\mathbbm{R}^{2}
is given by

\mathrm{d}S(x)=\big(x,x^{2})
. Then the smooth critical locus of
S
is a single point
\mathrm{Crit}(S)=\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}
,
but the points of tangent bundle of
\mathrm{Crit}(S)
in the above sense are given by
T_{0}\mathbbm{R}\hookrightarrow T\mathbbm{R}
.
Intuitively, this encodes the tangency of the parabola graph and the horizontal
x
-axis in
\mathbbm{R}^{2}
.

(iv)
Generally in the field-theoretic context, the question of whether all on-shell tangent vectors
are represented by on-shell paths of fields depends highly on the explicit form of the Lagrangian density
L
, and is intricately related to the fact that the prolongated shell
S^{\infty}_{L}
might not be a manifold (Rem.
5.17
). An explicit field theoretic example of this fact may be found in
[
Blo23
]
.

103
103

103

Therein a different definition of tangent bundle is employed,
inspired by constructions internal to diffeological spaces, which only includes on-shell tangent vectors which
are represented by line plots.

This conundrum of intuition is bypassed in the infinitesimally thickened setting where tangent vectors are given,
by definition, as
actual
infinitesimal line plots
[
GS24
]
whereby the above pullback construction
will arise as a proposition.

The definition of differential forms on
T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
and
T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
now follows as in Def.
7.1
, i.e.,
as fiberwise linear antisymmetric maps into
\mathbbm{R}\in\mathrm{SmthSet}
. The canonical subspace embedding

\iota_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}:\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\times M

induces a pushforward embedding map
T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times TM\hookrightarrow T\mathcal{F}\times TM
of tangent bundles, and hence a pullback/restriction of differential
forms. Here, we are only interested in the image of the local bicomplex under the restriction

\displaystyle(\iota_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}})^{*}:\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)

\displaystyle(ev^{\infty})^{*}\omega

\displaystyle\longmapsto(ev^{\infty})^{*}\omega|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\,,

which defines the
on-shell local forms
on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
. In other words, on-shell local forms are in 1-1 correspondence
with equivalency classes of local forms that agree when restricted to the on-shell subspace. Practically, this is quite straightforward:
A local 1-form
(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\omega:T\mathcal{F}\times TM\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

induces a 1-form on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
simply by ‘restricting its domain’

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\omega|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}:T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times TM\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}\times TM\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,.

In the local coordinate description of (
182
), one use the same notation

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\omega|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=\sum_{I_{1},\cdots,I_{p}=0}\omega_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}a_{1}\cdots a_{q}}^{I_{1}\dots I_{q}}\big(x,\{\partial_{J}\phi^{b}\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{M}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}_{M}x^{\mu_{p}}\wedge\delta(\partial_{I_{1}}\phi^{a_{1}})\wedge\cdots\wedge\delta(\partial_{I_{q}}\phi^{a_{q}})\,,

with the implicit understanding that the right-hand side is to be evaluated
only
on on-shell fields and their tangent vectors.
Along the same lines, the
on-shell local vector fields

\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)
are
(equivalency classes of) restrictions of local vector
fields
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}
which factor through
T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
(and hence satisfy (
188
) at each
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(*)
).

Remark 7.15

(
On-shell Cartan calculus caveats).

It is not obvious that the local Cartan calculus of
\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
descends to a Cartan calculus of local forms and
vector fields on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
, although often implicitly assumed in the literature.
104
104

104

This is often by stating the (often unjustified)
assumption that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
itself is
some kind
of an infinite-dimensional manifold.

(i)
In more detail, the vertical differential

\delta
does not, in general, descend.
For instance, consider the case where two local
(p,0)
-forms
\mathcal{P},\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
define the same on-shell local
(p,0)
-form

\mathcal{P}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\in\Omega^{p,0}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)
, i.e., such that

\mathcal{P},\tilde{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)
coincide along
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
but not necessarily on the complement.
In that case, the restrictions of their vertical differentials
\delta\mathcal{P},\delta\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
agree if and only if the difference
of currents is (locally) of the form

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}})(\phi)=\mathcal{J}^{I}(\phi)\cdot\partial_{I}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)\,,

(189)

(see proof of Prop.
7.23
). The same statements hold for the Lie bracket of local vector fields
descending to the set of on-shell local vector fields.

(ii)
Technically, at the level of the jet bundle, this is the case whenever the ideal of

C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
generated by (the components of) the prolongated Euler–Lagrange bundle map

\mathsf{pr}EL:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F)
is
point-determined
(see e.g.
[
MR91
]
).
Of course, the analogous statements can even fail in the finite-dimensional field-theoretic
setting of Ex.
5.34
, but it is true if, for instance, the corresponding action is regular enough
such that the on-shell critical set is an embedded submanifold (see e.g.
[
HT92
, Thm. 1.1]
).

(iii)
Along the same lines, the field-theoretic differential
\delta
descends to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
, for instance,
in the cases where the prolongated shell
S_{L}^{\infty}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
is an embedded Fréchet submanifold (Rem.
5.17
).
This is the case when the diagram of finite order prolongated shells
S^{q}_{L,k}\rightarrow S^{q-1}_{L,k}
from Rem.
5.17

consists of smooth (fin. dim.) manifolds and fibrations
[
Ts82
]
[
GP17
]
. In other words, when
S^{\infty}_{L}
is
a locally-pro submanifold of the infinite jet bundle (Def.
50
). PDEs with this property are called ‘formally integrable’,
which is often the case for those arising in field theory.

(iv)

The general question of whether smooth functions, and hence also forms, on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
which vanish on the prolongated shell
S^{\infty}
of
a differential operator
P
are proportional to its prolongation
\mathsf{pr}P
has, of course, been explored
in the PDEs literature. Indeed, such conditions may be viewed as the defining ingredients of ‘
diffieties
’ inside a jet bundle,

105
105

105

Traditionally, this school employs the pro-manifold picture – but many of the constructions and claims should
be applicable to the locally pro-manifold picture too (as done e.g. in
[
GP17
]
).

with the restriction of the variational bicomplex on
S^{\infty}

[
Ts82
]
being closely related to the ‘
Secondary Calculus
’
of the diffiety
[
Vin81
]
[
Vin84b
]

[
Vin13
]
[
Vita09
]
.

(v)
We do not need these analytical details for our developments, but we note that such explicit sufficient conditions in
terms of the Euler–Lagrange source form may be found, for instance, in
[
KV11
]
. Similar conditions in the field-theoretic setting may
be found in
[
HT92
, §12]
.
106
106

106

The jet bundle technology is not explicitly used therein, but the conditions
are in fact implicitly taking place in
J^{\infty}_{M}F
.

Many of the results that follow do not require the use
an on-shell Cartan calculus, as the manipulations take place on
\mathcal{F}\times M
, with the on-shell restrictions being applied
a posteriori. We will refer to this remark, in the instances where the extra assumptions are needed. Nevertheless,
as far as we are aware, sufficient regularity conditions are satisfied in all fundamental Lagrangians of interest
[
HT92
, §12]
.

Lemma 7.16

(
Local vector field is on-shell iff preserves EL-operator).

A local vector field
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
defines an on-shell vector field if and only if it preserves the Euler–Lagrange source
form
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\in\Omega^{d,1}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
along
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
. That is, for any on-shell field
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}

\mathcal{Z}(\phi)\in T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\iff\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})\big|_{\{\phi\}\times M}=0\,.

Proof.

Using the local Cartan calculus and standard abuse of notation
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\delta\phi^{a}
, we compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle=(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta+\delta\iota_{\mathcal{Z}})\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\big(\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\wedge\delta\phi^{a}\big)+\delta(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\mathcal{Z}^{a})

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\delta\phi^{a}-\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\mathcal{Z}^{a}+\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\mathcal{Z}^{a}-\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\delta\mathcal{Z}^{a}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\delta\phi^{a}-\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\delta\mathcal{Z}^{a}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\bigg(\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})}\cdot\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})\bigg)\wedge\delta\phi^{a}+\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\delta\mathcal{Z}^{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}^{b}}{\partial x^{I}}\bigg)\wedge\delta\phi^{a}+\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}\cdot\delta\mathcal{Z}^{a}\,.

At any on-shell field
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
, the second term vanishes by definition. The result then follows, since the
vanishing of the remaining term is exactly the tangency condition, i.e., the Jacobi equation of (
188
).
∎

This means
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})|_{\{\phi\}\times M}
actually vanishes at any
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
as a map of tangent vectors on
\mathcal{F}\times M
, and not only those tangent to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
.

This result allows us to prove the infinitesimal version of Prop.
5.28
and Prop.

5.42
, bearing in mind also Prop.
6.17
.
That is, since any finite local or spacetime covariant symmetry
\mathcal{P}\in\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
of a Lagrangian field theory

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
preserves the on-shell field space
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
, then any infinitesimal symmetry
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})
should
be
tangent
to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
. In other words, an infinitesimal symmetry of
\mathcal{L}
should define an on-shell vector field,
which is indeed the case.

Proposition 7.17

(
Infinitesimal symmetry is tangent to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
).

Let
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
be an infinitesimal symmetry of a Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}\in\Omega^{d,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
, and so

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}
.
Then
\mathcal{Z}
defines an on-shell vector field
\mathcal{Z}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}})
, i.e.,

\mathcal{Z}(\phi)\quad\in\quad T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}

for each
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}+\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
be the on-shell conserved current of the infinitesimal local
symmetry
\mathcal{Z}
(Prop.
6.14
), i.e.,
\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}
.
Computing the Lie derivative of
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
along
\mathcal{Z}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta+\delta\iota_{\mathcal{Z}})\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta(\delta\mathcal{L}-\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}})+\delta\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=0-\mathrm{d}_{M}(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{d}_{M}(\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}+\delta\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}})

where we used the bi-complex commutation relations and the local Cartan calculus of Lem.
7.7
repeatedly.
Next, it follows that for
any
local vector field
\mathcal{Z}
(not necessarily a symmetry), by
[
An89
, Cor. 3.22]

or even directly in local coordinates, that

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})=\mathcal{E}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L})\,.

This is the infinitesimal version of the covariance property of the Euler–Lagrange operator from Prop.
5.28
.
In other words, the Lie derivative
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})
may be equivalently seen as the Euler–Lagrange operator / source form of the
local Lagrangian
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L}=(\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}L)\circ j^{\infty}
.

Combining the two equations for the case where
\mathcal{Z}
is a local symmetry, we have

\mathcal{E}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L})=-\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}})\,,

which at the level of the infinite jet bundle says that the source form
E(\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}L)\in\Omega^{d-1,1}_{S}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is
\mathrm{d}_{H}
-exact.
By Prop.
5.10
, this is necessarily the zero source form, and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})=\mathcal{E}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L})=0\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636pt\Omega^{d-1,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,.

This equation holds as forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
and hence, in particular, restricts to equations of forms on
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
.
This completes the proof by the result of Lem.
7.16
.
∎

This statement appears in Theorem 13 (a) of
[
Zu86
]
without proof, and is implicitly used throughout
[
DF99
]
.
A more involved proof appears also in
[
Blo23
]
.
107
107

107

It seems to us the general covariance fact

\mathcal{E}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L})=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})
is not noted therein.

Note that the equation

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})=0\,,

(190)

appearing in the proof may be interpreted as the statement that (infinitesimal) symmetries of an action/Lagrangian
are also symmetries of the induced Euler–Lagrange equations.
A calculation along the lines of Lem.
7.16
shows that the vertical differential
of the Euler–Lagrange
(d-1,1)
-form vanishes on-shell.

Lemma 7.18

(
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
is
\delta
-closed on-shell).

The Euler–Lagrange local
(d,1)
-form
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\in\Omega^{d,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
satisfies

\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=0\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)\,.

Proof.

For any two on-shell tangent vectors
\mathcal{Z}^{1,2}_{\phi}=(\mathcal{Z}^{1,2}_{\phi})^{a}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}\,\in\,T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}
, we have

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{Z}^{1}_{\phi},\mathcal{Z}^{2}_{\phi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})}\cdot\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})\wedge\delta\phi^{a}\Big|_{(\phi,p)}\big(\mathcal{Z}^{1}_{\phi},\mathcal{Z}^{2}_{\phi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{b})}\cdot\frac{\partial(\mathcal{Z}^{1}_{\phi})^{b}}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot(\mathcal{Z}^{1}_{\phi})^{a}\bigg)(p)\,-\,(1\leftrightarrow 2)

\displaystyle=0

where we used the standard abuse of notation and the fact that each of the terms vanishes since they are proportional
to the Jacobi equation (
188
) for each
on-shell
tangent vector, respectively. The case of general
tangent vectors
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}+X_{p}\in T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times T_{p}M
follows similarly.
∎

This implies, in particular, that

\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{Z}^{1},\mathcal{Z}^{2})=0\quad\in\quad\Omega^{2,d}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)

for any two on-shell vector fields
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}})
, as can be also checked directly using the induced
local Cartan calculus. Furthermore, Lem.
7.18
immediately implies that the presymplectic
current is
conserved
on-shell.

Corollary 7.19

(
Presymplectic current is on-shell conserved).

The presymplectic current
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\in\Omega^{d-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
satisfies

\mathrm{d}_{M}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=0\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)\,.

In other words, it is an on-shell conserved current.

Proof.

Applying the vertical differential on Eq. (
186
), we have

0=\delta^{2}\mathcal{L}=\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}-\mathrm{d}_{M}\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\,.

That is,

\mathrm{d}_{M}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\in\Omega^{d,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
,
so that the result follows by restricting to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
and using Lem.
7.18
.
∎

By definition, infinitesimal local symmetries preserve the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
, up to a trivial Lagrangian, and we have shown they preserve
the Euler–Lagrange operator
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
(Prop.
7.17
). It should not come as a
surprise that they further preserve the induced presymplectic current, up to a ‘trivial’
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact
(d-1,2)
current.

Lemma 7.20

(
Infinitesimal symmetries preserve presymplectic current).

Let
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
be an infinitesimal symmetry of
\mathcal{L}\in\Omega^{d,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
. Then
\mathcal{Z}
preserves the
presymplectic current
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{Z}}\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636pt\Omega^{d-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

up to an exact local
(d-1,2)
-form.

Proof.

The local Cartan calculus gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\,,

while by the proof of Prop.
7.17
,
\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{Z}})=+\mathrm{d}_{M}(\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}})
.
Thus, applying
\mathrm{d}_{M}
to the first equation we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}\delta(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}})=-\delta\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}})

\displaystyle=-\delta\mathrm{d}_{M}\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}=+\delta^{2}\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

At the level of the
J^{\infty}_{M}F
,
\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\omega_{L}
is a
\mathrm{d}_{H}
-closed
(d-1,2)
form, and so by Prop.
5.5
it
is in fact exact
\mathbb{L}_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\omega_{L}=\mathrm{d}_{H}B_{Z}
. Pulling back to
\mathcal{F}\times M
via the prolongated evaluation map completes the proof.
∎

Borrowing intuition from finite-dimensional symplectic geometry, this result says that an infinitesimal local symmetry is a
‘
symplectic vector field
’ with respect to
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
, up to a horizontally exact local form. Of course,
as one might expect, this result is not restricted to infinitesimal symmetries. Indeed, any finite symmetry
(Def.
3.23
) of a Lagrangian field theory preserves the induced presymplectic current.
We include a brief proof of the local case, which parallels the above, for completeness.

Lemma 7.21

(
Finite local symmetries preserve presymplectic current).

Let
\mathcal{D}=D\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be a local symmetry of
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
as in Def.
3.23
.
Then
\mathcal{D}
preserves the presymplectic current
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}

\mathcal{D}^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{D}}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

up to an exact local
(d-1,2)
-form. The pullback here means
\mathcal{D}^{*}\omega:=(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\big(\mathsf{pr}D^{*}\omega)
where

\mathsf{pr}D:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
is the prolongated bundle map (Def.
3.16
).

108
108

108

This can be equivalently defined via the pushforward
\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}:T\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}
along
\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

We prove this for the case of a local finite symmetry, leaving the spacetime covariant one for the interested reader.
The proof is analogous to the infinitesimal case. Let
\mathcal{D}=D\circ j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be any local diffeomorphism.
Then

\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{D}^{*}\mathcal{L})=\mathcal{D}^{*}(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})=\mathcal{D}^{*}(\delta\mathcal{L}-\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}})=\delta\mathcal{D}^{*}\mathcal{L}-\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{D}^{*}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}

where the first step is the covariance of the Euler–Lagrange operator from Prop.
5.42

and (
135
). The commutation of both differentials with local self maps
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

can be seen in the local coordinate abuse of notation, and appears at the level of the jet bundle in

[
An89
, Thm. 3.15]
. This is simply the integrated analog of the evolutionary/local Cartan calculus relations.

Consider now the case where
\mathcal{D}
is actually local symmetry of
\mathcal{L}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{D}^{*}\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
. Then, we also have

\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{D}^{*}\mathcal{L})=\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{D}})=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\,,

whereby combining with the equation above, we arrive at

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=\delta\mathcal{D}^{*}\mathcal{L}-\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}^{*}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}\,.

Applying
\delta
on both sides,

\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=0+\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{D}^{*}\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{D}^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}\,,

but as in the proof of Cor.
7.19
, it is also the case that

\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{D}^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
. Subtracting the last equations, we get

\mathrm{d}_{M}(\mathcal{D}^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}-\omega_{\mathcal{L}})=0\,,

which at the level of the jet bundle says that
\mathsf{pr}D^{*}\omega_{L}-\omega_{L}
is a horizontally closed
(d-1,2)
-form. Once again,
the result follows as an application of Prop.
5.5
.
∎

Following the finite-dimensional heuristics, the result says that a finite symmetry of a local Lagrangian field theory is a
‘
symplectomorphism
’ with respect to
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
, up to a horizontally exact local form. Infinitesimal symmetries
and their currents are further intricately related to the properties of the presymplectic current.

Lemma 7.22

(
Symmetry conserved current vs symplectic current).

Let
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
be an infinitesimal symmetry of
\mathcal{L}\in\Omega^{d,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
with conserved current

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\Omega^{d-1,0}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
. Then the local
(d-1,1)
-form
\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}
is horizontally exact,
that is

\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

for some
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\Omega^{d-2,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
. That is,
\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}
is a‘trivial’

(d-1,1)
-form on
\mathcal{F}\times M
.

Proof.

Recall by Noether’s First Theorem (Prop.
6.14
), the conserved current satisfies
\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}
.
Computing the Lie derivative of
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
along
\mathcal{Z}
as in the proof of Prop.
7.17
,
we have

\displaystyle 0=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{M}(-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}-\delta\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}})\,,

and hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{M}(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}})=0\,.

At the level of the
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, this says that
\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\omega_{L}+\mathrm{d}_{V}P_{\mathsf{pr}Z}
is a
\mathrm{d}_{H}
-closed
(d-1,2)
form,
and so by Prop.
5.5
it is in fact exact
\iota_{\mathsf{pr}Z}\omega_{L}+\mathrm{d}_{V}P_{\mathsf{pr}Z}=\mathrm{d}_{H}T_{\mathcal{Z}}
. Pulling back to

\mathcal{F}\times M
via the prolongated evaluation map completes the proof.
∎

Let us expand on the interpretation of the above result in terms of the presymplectic potential current
\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
. Substituting

\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}
, then the above reads

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,,

(191)

and so
\mathcal{Z}
is heuristically a ‘
Hamiltonian vector field
’ for
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
, up to a local horizontally
exact form (a trivial current). In the same vein and equivalently, the current
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}
is a ‘
Hamiltonian form
’ for
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
.
From this point of view, Lem.
7.20
follows as a consequence by applying
\delta
,
with
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{Z}}=-\delta\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}
, i.e., any Hamiltonian vector field is a symplectic vector field. We will come back to this interpretation
shortly (see discussion around (
195
)).

Before that, recall by Cor.
6.24
that the current of a gauge symmetry satisfies
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{e}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{J}_{e}

for some local current
\mathcal{J}_{e}=\mathcal{J}(-,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(-),e)
that is linear in the second entry of
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
, and in particular vanishes
on-shell
\mathcal{J}_{e}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=0
,. In that case, repeating the proof above verbatim gives

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{e}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{J}_{e}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{e}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,.

(192)

This relation implies the following degenerate behavior for the presymplectic current.

Proposition 7.23

(
Gauge symmetry vs presymplectic current).

Let
\mathcal{R}_{e}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
by a gauge symmetry of
\mathcal{L}
, for any gauge parameter
e\in\Gamma_{M}E
.
Then the induced presymplectic current satisfies the on-shell relation

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{e}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}\big|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{e}\big|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)\,,

for some local
(d-2,1)
-form
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{e}
. In particular, for every on-shell tangent vector

\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}\in T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
,
the evaluation of the presymplectic form is an exact
(d-1)
-form on spacetime

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{L}}\big(\mathcal{R}_{e}(\phi),\,\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}\big)=\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{e}(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi})\big)\quad\in\quad\Omega^{d-1}(M)\,.

Proof.

By Lem.
7.22
and since we are dealing with a symmetry, we have the off-shell relation (
192
),
where

\mathcal{J}_{e}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d-1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(M)

is a local
(d-1)
-current
\mathcal{J}_{e}=\mathcal{J}(-,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(-),e)
linear in the second entry. Hence, it suffices to show that
the vertical differential
\delta\mathcal{J}_{e}
vanishes on-shell. We show this locally using the standard abuse of notation.
By construction (see also the proof of Prop.
6.23
),
\mathcal{J}_{e}
is of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{e}=\sum_{|I|=0}\mathcal{J}^{I}_{e}(\phi)\cdot\partial_{I}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)\,,

for some collection of differential operator
\{\mathcal{J}^{I}_{e}(\phi)\}_{|I|=0}
of
\phi
(also local in
e\in\Gamma_{M}(E)
)
but whose precise form is immaterial. Applying the vertical differential along
\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{J}_{e}=\sum_{|I|=0}\delta\mathcal{J}^{I}_{e}(\phi)\cdot\partial_{I}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)+\mathcal{J}^{I}_{e}(\phi)\cdot\delta\big(\partial_{I}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)\,,

where former terms in the sum immediately vanish on-shell, in the same manner as
\mathcal{J}_{e}
itself.
For the latter remaining terms, working as in Eq. (
179
) it is easy
to see that ‘the partial derivatives commute with the vertical differential’, i.e.,

\delta\big(\partial_{I}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi)\big)(\mathcal{Z}^{1}_{\phi},\mathcal{Z}^{2}_{\phi})=\partial_{I}\big(\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{Z}^{1}_{\phi},\mathcal{Z}^{2}_{\phi})\big)\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(M)\,,

for any pair of tangent vectors on the field space. Using Lem.
7.18
,
it follows that such terms vanish when applied to
on-shell
tangent vectors, and so

\delta(\partial_{I}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=\partial_{I}(\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}})=0\,.

Overall, we deduce that

\delta\mathcal{J}_{e}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=0\hskip 14.22636pt\in\hskip 14.22636pt\Omega^{d-1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)\,,

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.24

(
A subtlety in the literature).

The original source
[
Zu86
]
claims the statement in Prop.
7.23

(after composing with integration) in Theorem 13(b), and gives (a sketch of) a proof that relies on the
‘
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exactness’ of the corresponding conserved current of the gauge symmetry (Cor.
6.24
).
From our point of view, however, the exactness of the charge depends on the
(d-1)
-cohomology of the field bundle.
This seems to be an obstacle to the validity of the proof therein. Our proof for the degeneracy of the presymplectic
current evades this issue.

Brackets of Noether and Hamiltonian currents.

As we have eluded in (
185
), there is a natural
‘bracket operation’ for conserved local
(d-1,0)
-form currents, which maps the currents
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}
of any two local
infinitesimal symmetries of a local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
to the current of the corresponding commutator symmetry
\mathcal{P}_{[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]}
.
In fact, as we will see, this is closely related to a more general ‘Poisson-like’ bracket on a larger set of ‘Hamiltonian’ local
(d-1,0)
-form currents,
i.e., those that satisfy the ‘Hamiltonian’ condition of Lem.
7.22
, which is induced by the
presymplectic current
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
. To that end, we first
fix a choice
109
109

109

In principle, one can enhance the discussion below
to keep track of this choice. We conform with the literature and do not explicitly pursue this here.

of presymplectic current potential
\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
in the variational decomposition
\delta\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
.

Having fixed the presymplectic potential, then an infinitesimal symmetry
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
such that
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}

defines a family of conserved currents
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}+\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
parametrized by the freedom of adding a horizontally
closed form to
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}
. We keep track of these families by considering instead pairs of conserved charges and symmetries.

Definition 7.25

(
Noether pairs).

The set of
Noether pairs
of a local Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
, with respect to a presymplectic current potential
\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
,
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathrm{NoethPrs}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}}):=\Big\{(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{Z})\in\Omega^{d-1,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\times\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,\,\big|\,\,\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\Big\}\,.

Equivalently (Prop.
6.14
), Noether pairs are in 1-1 correspondence with pairs
(\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{Z})
such
that
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{L}=\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\delta\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}
, via
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}}+\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
.

Equation (
185
) defines a well-defined map of Noether pairs, by assigning the corresponding
current
\mathcal{P}_{[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]}
of the commutator symmetry for of two Noether pairs

\displaystyle\big((\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\big)\longmapsto\big(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}+\iota_{[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]}\theta_{\mathcal{L}},\,[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]\big)\,.

Using
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}=\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
and the local Cartan calculus repeatedly,
the first term on the right-hand side may be recast as

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}+\iota_{[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\big)-\tfrac{1}{2}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\big(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\big)+\tfrac{1}{2}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\big(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\big)\,.

Recall that by (
191
),
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}

for some local current
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}
, and so the terms apart from the Lie derivatives of original currents
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}

are in fact
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact. Thus, the above may be interpreted as saying that the current
\mathcal{P}_{[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]}
of the commutator
of two symmetries is given via the Lie derivatives along the currents, up to a
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact term – i.e., a trivial local
(d-1,0)
-form current.
In other words, to compute the corresponding charge
\mathcal{P}_{[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]}^{\Sigma^{d-1}}
over a compact, boundaryless submanifold

\Sigma^{d-1}\hookrightarrow M
, it is sufficient to consider only
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}
(or its antisymmetrization).

Definition 7.26

(
Bracket of Noether pairs).

The bracket of Noether pairs

\{-,-\}_{N}:\mathrm{NoethPrs}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}})\times\mathrm{NoethPrs}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{NoethPrs}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}})

is defined by assigning the pair of commutator of symmetries and the corresponding conserved current

\displaystyle\big\{(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\big\}_{N}:

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\,[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}})+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{d}_{M}(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}T_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}T_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}),\,[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]\big)\,,

where, by (
191
), the latter terms
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact terms are given by

\mathrm{d}_{M}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2,1}}T_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}=-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2,1}}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}}\theta_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1,2}})
.

The bracket of Noether pairs is manifestly
\mathbbm{R}
-linear and antisymmetric. However, a tedious but straightforward calculation
shows that the appearance of the
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact currents spoils the Jacobi identity, in turn by a
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact currents.
That is, the Jacobiator evaluates to

\displaystyle\mathrm{Jac}_{\{-,-\}_{N}}\big((\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}},\mathcal{Z}_{3})\big):

\displaystyle=\Big\{\big\{(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\}_{N}\,,\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}},\mathcal{Z}_{3})\Big\}_{N}+\mathrm{cyc}(1,2,3)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{d}_{M}(\cdots),\,0\big)\,,

(193)

for some horizontally exact (trivial) current, whose precise form is not important (see (
7.2
)).
It follows that the Noether bracket does not define a Lie algebra on Noether pairs, but instead only on the
quotient
of
Noether pairs by ‘trivial’, i.e., horizontally exact
(d-1,0)
-form currents.

Lemma 7.27

(
Dickey Lie algebra of Noether currents).

(i)
The bracket of Noether pairs descends to a Lie bracket on the quotient

\mathrm{NoethPrs}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}})/\mathrm{d}_{M}\Omega^{d-2,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

known as the Dickey Lie bracket of (on-shell) conserved currents (see
[
Di91
]
).

(ii)
Furthermore, the Dickey
Lie algebra is part of a short exact sequence of Lie algebras

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{d}_{M}}^{d-1,0}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{NoethPrs}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}})/\mathrm{d}_{M}\Omega^{d-2,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,,

(194)

where the former horizontal
(d-1,0)
-cohomology, i.e., the off-shell conserved currents modulo trivial currents, being thought
of as a trivial Lie algebra. In other words, the Dickey Lie algebra of (on-shell) conserved currents is a central extension of the
Lie algebra of infinitesimal local symmetries of the Lagrangian field theory.

Proof.

The bracket of Noether pairs (
7.26
) descends to a Lie bracket since the Jacobiator (
7.2
)
vanishes in cohomology. The short exact sequence follows immediately: the first map is the canonical inclusion

[\tilde{\mathcal{P}}]\mapstochar\rightarrow([\tilde{\mathcal{P}}],\,0)
and the second is the canonical projection
([\tilde{\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}}],\,\mathcal{Z})\mapstochar\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}

onto symmetries of
\mathcal{L}
. The kernel of the former coincides with the image of the latter, by definition. Note that
this subset represents exactly the remaining freedom of adding a
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-closed
(d-1,0)
-form to each current class
[\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}]

corresponding to a fixed symmetry
\mathcal{Z}
.
∎

In principle, a lot of information is discarded if works on the quotient by ‘trivial’ currents. From a physical point of view,
information of charges over submanifolds with boundary is ignored. Certain homotopical Lie algebraic structures naturally encode the breaking
of the Jacobi identity, in a controlled homotopical manner, which reduce to the above quotient description in an appropriate truncation
(Rem.
7.33
), but otherwise retain the full information of the bracket of currents.

There is a more general bracket defined on a larger class of
(d-1,0)
-local currents, which are not necessarily on-shell conserved.
Physically, upon integration over a codimension 1 submanifold, these correspond to observables that are not necessarily conserved
over ‘time evolution’. These are analogs of Hamiltonian functions from finite-dimensional (pre)symplectic geometry.
Explicitly, these are local
(d-1,0)
-form currents
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\Omega^{d-1,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
satisfying the condition

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}

(195)

for
some
local vector field
\mathcal{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
and local form
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\Omega^{d-2,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
. Local currents
satisfying this condition are known as
Hamiltonian currents
110
110

110

The right-hand side is often demanded to be identically zero.
However, to include all conserved currents and more general Hamiltonian observables, it is natural to relax the condition up to
a horizontally exact local form.
of
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
, while the corresponding local vector fields
\mathcal{Z}
as
Hamiltonian vector fields

of
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
.

Recall that this is exactly the relation appearing in Lem.
7.22
, which is now
interpreted as the statement that all Noether conserved currents of a field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
are in fact Hamiltonian for its
presymplectic current
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
. However, generally speaking, a Hamiltonian current need not be conserved on-shell,
i.e., generically
\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\neq 0
, and similarly a Hamiltonian vector field need not be a symmetry of
\mathcal{L}
.
Nevertheless, it is still true that Hamiltonian vector fields are a symmetry of
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
, since by applying
\delta

to (
195
) they preserve the presympectic current, up to a horizontally exact form

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,,

where
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{Z}}=-\delta\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}
. In other words, Hamiltonian vector fields of
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
are
symplectic
vector fields.

As with symmetries of a Lagrangian and Noether currents, to a fixed Hamiltonian vector field
\mathcal{Z}
satisfying (
195
),
there exists a corresponding
family
of Hamiltonian currents
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}
parametrized by the freedom of adding any
(d-1,0)
-current
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}

whose vertical differential vanishes - up to a horizontally exact form,
\delta\tilde{\mathcal{H}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\tilde{\mathcal{T}}
for some

\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\in\Omega^{d-2,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
. To keep track of these families, we consider instead pairs of Hamiltonian currents and vector fields.

Definition 7.28

(
Hamiltonian Pairs).

The set of
Hamiltonian pairs
of a local Lagrangian field theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{L})
, with respect to a presymplectic potential
\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
and hence
current
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=\delta\theta_{\mathcal{L}}
, is defined by

\displaystyle\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}}):=\big\{(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{Z})\in\Omega^{d-1,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\times\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,\,\big|\,\,\exists\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\,\mathrm{s.t.}\,\,\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}\big\}\,.

The subspace

\mathrm{StrHamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})

of Hamiltonian pairs such that
\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}=0
vanishes exactly (and not up to a
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact current)
are called
strict
Hamiltonian pairs.

Mimicking the construction of a Poisson bracket in finite-dimensional (pre)symplectic geometry, there is an analogous ‘Poisson-like’
bracket of Hamiltonian currents.

Definition 7.29

(
Bracket of Hamiltonian pairs).

The bracket of Hamiltonian pairs

\{-,-\}_{H}:\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})\times\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})

is defined by assigning the contraction of the presymplectic current

\displaystyle\big\{(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\big\}_{H}:

\displaystyle=\big(-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}},\,[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]\big)\,.

The fact that this is well-defined, i.e., the contraction
-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
is indeed a Hamiltonian current for

[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
, follows by a straightforward application of the local Cartan calculus. Indeed, a short calculation shows

\displaystyle-\delta\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=-\iota_{[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]}\omega+\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\delta\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\delta\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\big)\,,

where
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\in\Omega^{d-2,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
are any representative local
(d-2,1)
-forms for the Hamiltonian current
condition (
195
), for each of the pairs respectively. This is simply the Hamiltonian current condition for the pair

(-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}},\,[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}])
, hence exhibiting the bracket
\{-,-\}_{H}
as a well-defined map of Hamiltonian pairs.

The relation between the two brackets of Def.
7.26
and Def.
7.29
follows easily,
since by applying a further contraction on the defining Hamiltonian condition (
195
) we have

\displaystyle-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}

(196)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\big)+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\big)

with the latter equality holding by the manifest antisymmetry of the double contraction.

Lemma 7.30

(
Relation between brackets of currents).

On the subspace of Noether pairs
\mathrm{NoethPrs}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})
, the bracket
of Hamiltonian pairs coincides with that of Noether pairs, up to a horizontally exact current. Explicitly,

\displaystyle\big\{(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\big\}_{H}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{2}+\mathrm{d}_{M}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\,[\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}]\big)

\displaystyle=\big\{(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\big\}_{N}+\big(\mathrm{d}_{M}(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}),\,0\big)\,,

for any
(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\,\in\,\mathrm{NoetherPair}(\mathcal{L},\theta_{\mathcal{L}})
.

Proof.

By Lem.
7.22
, any Noether current is in particular Hamiltonian, hence Noether pairs
are indeed a vector subspace of Hamiltonian pairs. The result follows immediately by considering (
196
)
for the case of the Hamiltonian currents being Noether currents and comparing with the formulas from Def.
7.26
.
∎

As with the case of the Noether bracket, the bracket of Hamiltonian pairs is naturally antisymmetric and
\mathbbm{R}
-linear, but the horizontally-exact
terms spoil Jacobi identity. An easy way to see this is as follows: Since
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
is a local
(d-1,2)
-form, it follows that the contraction
with any three local vector fields vanishes
\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}=0
. Applying
\delta
on the equation and using
the local Cartan calculus repeatedly, one arrives at

0=\big(-(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{[\mathcal{Z}_{2},\mathcal{Z}_{3}]}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\mathrm{cyc}(1,2,3)\big)+\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(-\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\delta T_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}}+\mathrm{cyc}(1,2,3)\big)\,,

and so the Jacobiator reads

\displaystyle\mathrm{Jac}_{\{-,-\}_{H}}\big((\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2}),\,(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}},\mathcal{Z}_{3})\big):

\displaystyle=\Big\{\big\{(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}},\mathcal{Z}_{1}),\,(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{Z}_{2})\}_{H}\,,\,(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}},\mathcal{Z}_{3})\Big\}_{H}+\mathrm{cyc}(1,2,3)

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\iota_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\delta T_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}}+\mathrm{cyc}(1,2,3)\big),\,0\Big)\,.

(197)

The explicit form of the Jacobiator (
7.2
) for the Noether bracket can be deduced from
(
7.2
), by substituting the relation from Lem.
7.30
.

There are two ways to identify a Lie algebra structure from the above bracket. Obviously,
as in finite-dimensional geometry,
\{-,-\}_{H}
defines a strict Lie algebra structure on the subspace of
strict
Hamiltonian pairs

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{StrHamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})\,,\{-,-\}_{H}\big)

(198)

where the
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact terms never appear. Otherwise, the bracket induces a Lie algebra structure on the quotient of Hamiltonian pairs by horizontally
exact currents, inside which the Dickey Lie algebra of Lem.
7.27
naturally sits as a Lie subalgebra.

Lemma 7.31

(
Local Poisson Lie algebra of Hamiltonian currents).

(i)
The bracket of Hamiltonian pairs descends to a Lie bracket on the quotient

\displaystyle\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})/\mathrm{d}_{M}\Omega^{d-2,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

known as the local Poisson Lie bracket of (Hamiltonian) currents.

(ii)
Furthermore, the local Poisson Lie algebra
is part of a short exact sequence of Lie algebras

\displaystyle\Omega^{d-1,0}_{\delta=\mathrm{d}_{M}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)/\mathrm{d}_{M}\Omega^{d-2,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{HamPrs}(\mathcal{F}\times M,\omega_{\mathcal{L}})/\mathrm{d}_{M}\Omega^{d-2,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{Ham},\omega_{\mathcal{L}}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\,,

(199)

with the subspace of local
(d-1,0)
-forms on the left being those
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
whose vertical differential is
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact

\delta\tilde{\mathcal{H}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\tilde{\mathcal{T}}
, modulo horizontally exact currents, thought of as a trivial Lie algebra. That is,
the local Poisson Lie algebra of Hamiltonian currents (modulo trivial currents) is a central extension of the Lie algebra of
Hamiltonian vector fields of
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{Ham},\omega_{\mathcal{L}}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.

The proof follows identically as in Lem.
7.27
, by replacing the Noether with
the corresponding Hamiltonian counterparts.
The bracket of Hamiltonian pairs (
7.29
) descends to a Lie bracket since the Jacobiator (
7.2
)
vanishes in cohomology. The short exact sequence follows immediately: the first map is the canonical inclusion
[\tilde{\mathcal{H}}]\mapstochar\rightarrow([\tilde{\mathcal{H}}],\,0)

and the second is the canonical projection
([\tilde{\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Z}}}],\,\mathcal{Z})\mapstochar\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
onto Hamiltonian vector fields of
\omega_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The kernel of the former coincides with the image of the latter, by definition. This subset represents exactly the remaining freedom
of adding a
\delta
-closed
(d-1,0)
-form - up to an
\mathrm{d}_{M}
-exact current - to each class Hamiltonian current class
[\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}]

corresponding to a fixed Hamiltonian vector field
\mathcal{Z}
.
∎

Strictly speaking, Hamiltonian pairs are only a vector space and they do not have a commutative algebra structure. That is, the bracket of Hamiltonian pairs
is only a Lie bracket and not Poisson. The commutative algebra structure appears after transgressing currents as per Def.
3.20
,
whereby one may analogously define Hamiltonian functionals with respect to the transgressed symplectic
2
-form. It is in that setting that the actual
Poisson structure appears. We will explain this treatment in the following section and relate it to the brackets of currents defined above
in Lem.
7.49
.

Remark 7.32

(
Brackets of on-shell Hamiltonian currents).

For cases of classical field theories in which the local Cartan calculus descends to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M
, and so the requirements
of Rem.
7.15
are fulfilled, the above discussion applies analogously to
on-shell
Hamiltonian pairs.
More explicitly, these are on-shell pairs

(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{Z}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}})\in\Omega^{d-1,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\times M)\times\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}})

that satisfy the corresponding on-shell Hamiltonian condition

\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}|_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
.
It follows (modulo Rem.
7.15
), that such pairs are represented by pairs

(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{Z})\in\Omega^{d-1,0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
such that
\mathcal{Z}
is tangent to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}

and the Hamiltonian condition is satisfied off-shell up to a
(d-1,1)
-form proportional to a differential operator
applied to the Euler–Lagrange form

\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\omega_{\mathcal{L}}+\delta\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathrm{d}_{M}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Z}}+\mathcal{J}^{I}\partial_{I}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
. The above results follow for
the on-shell Hamiltonian pairs by replacing the off-shell objects and conditions with the corresponding on-shell versions.

Remark 7.33

(
Higher Poisson algebras of local observables).

(i)
Just as with the bracket of Noether pairs, taking the strict quotient by horizontally
exact Hamiltonian currents discards a lot of information.
In particular, information on charges of Hamiltonian currents over submanifolds with boundary is lost in this quotient.

(ii)
Readers familiar with higher/homotopical algebraic structures will naturally point out
that the breaking of the Jacobi identity – up to a homologically trivial term – suggests an underlying structure
of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra, i.e., a “higher Poisson
L_{\infty}
-algebra of local observables”.
This is indeed the case and ideas along this line of thought have been pursued in the literature, often under
the name of “higher presymplectic” geometry.

(iii)
We will not be delving into a discussion of higher structures in this manuscript.
The interested reader may find relevant ideas and constructions
in the following manuscripts: The earliest investigation specifically on the bigraded situation of
the jet bundle and field theory as described
in the current manuscript is found in
[
BFLS98
]
. General investigations of such structures in terms
of higher presymplectic geometry, i.e., in terms of finite-dimensional manifolds equipped with closed
m
-forms
(without a bigrading), were initiated in
[
Ro11
]
.
Further investigations with a viewpoint towards quantization, topological terms and BPS charges were pursued in
[
FRS14
]
[
SS17
]
.
A closely related treatment in terms of
L_{\infty}
-algebras of local observables, but not equivalent, to that of
[
BFLS98
]
may
be found in
[
De18
]
